\displaystyle{\big\|\mathcal{F}^{-1}_{\xi\mapsto x}e^{it\langle\xi\rangle}\partial_{t}T^{{\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2}},(1)}_{m}(h,h)\big\|}_{L^{8/7}_{x}}\lesssim\frac{\rho_{0}^{a}}{\langle t\rangle^{5/4}}

(6.52)

for some
a>0
.

Proof.

The proofs can be done similarly to those in Lemma
6.9
, and are in fact easier.
We provide some details for completeness.
Following the same steps in the proof of (
6.41
) we can write an estimate like (
6.44
) and reduce to estimating a term like the one in (
6.45
)
with
\widetilde{g_{{\epsilon}_{1}}}
replaced by
\varphi_{k_{1}}\widetilde{h_{{\epsilon}_{1}}}
;
for such a term, using Strichartz estimates with
(\widetilde{q},\widetilde{r},\gamma)=(6,3,2/3)
,
and Theorem
5.7
we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}&{\Big\|\int_{0}^{t}s\tau_{m}(s)e^{-isL}T_{1}^{S,1}[b_{1}]\big(e^{{\epsilon}_{1}is\langle\xi\rangle}\partial_{s}\widetilde{h_{{\epsilon}_{1}}},e^{{\epsilon}_{2}is\langle\xi\rangle}\varphi_{k_{2}}\widetilde{h_{{\epsilon}_{2}}}\big)\,ds\Big\|}_{L^{2}_{x}}\\
&\lesssim 2^{11m/6+\delta_{N}m}\cdot\sup_{s\approx 2^{m}}{\big\|T_{1}[b_{1}]\big(e^{{\epsilon}_{1}is\langle\xi\rangle}\varphi_{k_{1}}\partial_{s}\widetilde{h_{{\epsilon}_{1}}},e^{{\epsilon}_{2}is\langle\xi\rangle}\varphi_{k_{2}}\widetilde{h_{{\epsilon}_{2}}}\big)\big\|}_{L^{3/2}_{x}}\\
&\lesssim 2^{11m/6+\delta_{N}m}\sup_{s\approx 2^{m}}\Big[{\big\|e^{isL}P_{k_{1}}\partial_{s}h\big\|}_{L^{2+}}{\|e^{isL}P_{k_{2}}h\|}_{L^{6-}}\cdot 2^{C_{0}M_{0}}\\
&\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad+2^{-D}\cdot\mathcal{D}\big(e^{{\epsilon}_{1}is\langle\xi\rangle}\varphi_{k_{1}}\partial_{s}\widetilde{h_{{\epsilon}_{1}}},e^{{\epsilon}_{2}is\langle\xi\rangle}\varphi_{k_{2}}\widetilde{h_{{\epsilon}_{2}}}\big)\Big].\end{split}

(6.53)

From this we can deduce the desired bound, using the decay from (
4.20
) and (
4.19
),
and handling the remainder in the usual way.

The estimate on the
C_{2}
type terms follows by estimating

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\sum_{|k_{1}-k_{2}|<5,k_{2}\leqslant M_{0}}{\Big\|\int_{0}^{t}\tau_{m}(s)e^{isL}T_{1}^{S,1}[b_{1}]\big(e^{{\epsilon}_{1}is\langle\xi\rangle}\varphi_{k_{1}}\partial_{s}\widetilde{h_{{\epsilon}_{1}}},e^{{\epsilon}_{2}is\langle\xi\rangle}\partial_{\xi}\widetilde{h_{{\epsilon}_{2}}}\big)\,ds\Big\|}_{L^{2}_{x}}.\end{split}

(6.54)

This term is analogous to (
6.50
), but here the bound
follows directly after using the Strichartz estimate with indices
(\widetilde{q},\widetilde{r},\gamma)=(6,3,2/3)
,
followed by an
L^{6-}\times L^{2+}
product estimate via (
5.40b
),
Bernstein, (
4.20
) and (
4.13
).

For (
6.52
), using Theorem
5.7
we can bound the term on the left-hand side,
up to a faster decaying remainder, by

\displaystyle C\sum_{k_{1},k_{2}\leqslant M_{0}}\sup_{\approx 2^{m}}{\|\partial_{t}P_{k_{1}}h(t)\|}_{L^{2+}}{\|e^{itL}P_{k_{2}}h(t)\|}_{L^{8/3-}}\cdot 2^{C_{0}\delta_{N}m}

\displaystyle\lesssim 2^{(C_{0}+1)\delta_{N}m}\cdot\rho(2^{m})\cdot\rho(2^{m})^{1-\beta}2^{5/8m+10\delta_{N}m}\varepsilon^{\beta},

having used Bernstein and the bounds (
4.20
) and (
4.19
) for the last inequality.
This suffices for (
6.52
) and concludes the proof of the Lemma.
∎

With the proofs of Lemmas
6.6
,
6.8
,
6.9
and
6.10

(see also Lemmas
6.4
and
6.5
),
the proof of Proposition
6.2
is complete.
To finish the proof of the main Propositions
5.1
and
5.2
,
we are left with estimating the regular terms, see (
5.46
).
We proceed to do this in the next section.

7.
Regular quadratic terms

In this section we estimate the bilinear terms corresponding to the regular parts of the distribution.
Looking at (
5.46
) we define

\displaystyle\begin{split}T_{m}^{{\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2}},(3)}(G,H)&:=\int_{0}^{t}\tau_{m}(s)\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{6}}\frac{e^{-is\Phi_{{\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2}}}(\xi,\eta,\sigma)}}{\langle\eta\rangle\langle\sigma\rangle}\widetilde{G}(s,\eta)\widetilde{H}(s,\sigma)\\
&\times\varphi_{\leqslant M_{0}}(\langle\eta\rangle)\varphi_{\leqslant M_{0}}(\langle\sigma\rangle)\varphi_{\leqslant M_{0}}(\langle\xi\rangle)\mu^{R}(\xi,\eta,\sigma)\,d\eta d\sigma ds,\\
T_{m}^{{\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2}},(4)}(G,H)&:=\int_{0}^{t}\tau_{m}(s)\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{6}}\frac{e^{-is\Phi_{{\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2}}}(\xi,\eta,\sigma)}}{\langle\eta\rangle\langle\sigma\rangle}\widetilde{G}(s,\eta)\widetilde{H}(s,\sigma)\\
&\times\varphi_{\leqslant M_{0}}(\langle\eta\rangle)\varphi_{\leqslant M_{0}}(\langle\sigma\rangle)\varphi_{\leqslant M_{0}}(\langle\xi\rangle)\mu^{Re}(\xi,\eta,\sigma)\,d\eta d\sigma ds,\end{split}

(7.1)

where the distributions are defined in Proposition
5.6

and the phase is as in (
5.7
).
The goal of this section is to prove the analogue of Proposition
6.2
for the regular terms defined above:

Proposition 7.1
.

Under the assumptions (
4.13
), for all
m=0,1,...,L+1,

for all
{\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2}}\in\{+,-\},
we have for some
0<\delta<\beta
, and
j=3,4
:

\displaystyle\|\nabla_{\xi}T_{m}^{{\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2}},(j)}(G_{\epsilon_{1}},H_{\epsilon_{2}})\|_{L^{2}}\lesssim\rho(2^{m})^{1-\beta+\delta}2^{m}{\varepsilon}^{\beta},\qquad G,H\in\{g,h\}.

(7.2)

Moreover, there exist
0<a<1
and
\delta^{\prime}>0
such that

\displaystyle\bigg\|e^{itL}\widetilde{\mathcal{F}}^{-1}_{\xi\mapsto x}\big[T_{m}^{{\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2}},(j)}(G_{\epsilon_{1}},H_{\epsilon_{2}})\big]\bigg\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}\lesssim\frac{\rho_{0}^{a}}{\langle t\rangle^{1+a+\delta^{\prime}}},\qquad G,H\in\{g,h\}.

(7.3)

With Proposition
6.2
and Lemmas
5.4
and
5.5
,
this will complete the proof of Propositions
5.1
and
5.2
.

7.1.
Preliminaries

We first reduce the proof of (
7.2
) and (
7.3
) to some
relatively easier weighted
L^{2}
bounds.
We start by reducing (
7.3
) to
L^{p}
estimates for some
p>6/5
,
through the following analogue of Lemma
6.4
:

Lemma 7.2
.

Assume that there exist
0<a<b<1/10
, such that

\displaystyle\big\|\widehat{\mathcal{F}}^{-1}_{\xi\mapsto x}I(s)\big\|_{L^{6/(5+6b)}_{x}}\lesssim\frac{\rho_{0}^{a}}{\langle s\rangle^{1+2a}},

(7.4)

where

\displaystyle I(s):=e^{is\langle\xi\rangle}\partial_{s}T^{{\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2}},(j)}_{m}(G_{\epsilon_{1}},H_{\epsilon_{2}}),\qquad j=3,4.

(7.5)

Then

\displaystyle{\Big\|e^{itL}\widetilde{\mathcal{F}}^{-1}_{\xi\mapsto x}\big[T_{m}^{{\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2}},(j)}(G_{\epsilon_{1}},H_{\epsilon_{2}})\big]\Big\|}_{L^{\infty}_{x}}\lesssim\frac{\rho_{0}^{a}}{\langle t\rangle^{1+a+\delta^{\prime}}},\qquad j=3,4.

Proof.

The proof is almost identical to the one of Lemma
6.4
.
In this case we first use Bernstein to go from
L^{\infty}
to
L^{q}
with
q:=(1/6-b)^{-1}
.
Notice that
q^{\prime}=(5/6+b)^{-1}=6/(5+6b)
, consistently with the assumption (
7.4
).
Using that the linear decay rate for the propagator
e^{itL}
in
L^{q}
is

\langle t\rangle^{-(3/2)(1-2/q)}=\langle t\rangle^{-1-3b}
and that
b>a
, we obtain the desired conclusion.
∎

We will also need a bilinear lemma for operators with symbol
\partial_{\xi}\nu_{1}^{R}
.
Recall the definition (
5.34b
)-(
5.34c
);
in what follows, whenever needed, we will write explicitly the dependence on the measures
by denoting

\displaystyle\begin{split}T_{1}^{R,1}[\nu;b](g,h)(x)&:=\widehat{\mathcal{F}}^{-1}_{\xi\rightarrow x}\iint_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}g(\xi-\eta)h(\sigma)\,b(\xi,\eta,\sigma)\,\nu(\eta,\sigma)\,d\eta d\sigma,\\
T_{1}^{R,2}[\nu;b](g,h)(x)&:=\widehat{\mathcal{F}}^{-1}_{\xi\rightarrow x}\iint_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}g(-\eta-\sigma)h(\sigma)\,b(\xi,\eta,\sigma)\,\overline{\nu(\eta,\xi)}\,d\eta d\sigma,\\
T[\mu,b](g,h)(x)&:=\widehat{\mathcal{F}}^{-1}_{\xi\rightarrow x}\iint_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}g(\eta)h(\sigma)\,b(\xi,\eta,\sigma)\,\mu(\xi,\eta,\sigma)\,d\eta d\sigma.\end{split}

(7.6)

We then have the following analogue of Theorem
5.7
:

Lemma 7.3
.

With the definitions (
7.6
), for
b
(and
A
) as in the statement of Theorem
5.7
,
and for all
p,q\geqslant 1,r>1
with
1/p+1/q>1/r
, we have

\displaystyle{\big\|T_{1}^{R,1}[\partial_{1}\nu^{R}_{1};b](G,H)\big\|}_{L^{r}}+{\big\|T_{1}^{R,2}[\partial_{2}\nu^{R}_{1};b](G,H)\big\|}_{L^{r}}\lesssim 2^{C_{0}A}\|\widehat{G}\|_{L^{p}}\|\widehat{H}\|_{L^{q}},

(7.7)

for some absolute constant
C_{0}
.
Moreover, under the same assumptions, we have

\displaystyle{\big\|T[\partial_{1}\mu^{Re};b](G,H)\big\|}_{L^{r}}\lesssim 2^{C_{0}A}\|\widehat{G}\|_{L^{p}}\|\widehat{H}\|_{L^{q}}.

(7.8)

Proof.

(
7.7
) follows exactly as in the proof of (
5.40c
),
that is, from the standard bilinear Lemma 6.3 in
[
41
]
and the estimate (
5.19
) for the derivative of
\nu^{R}_{1}
.
(
7.8
) follows similarly using instead the estimate (
5.24
).
∎

Next, let us introduce the operators
which will appear when applying
\nabla_{\xi}
to (
7.1
). Let

\displaystyle\begin{split}D_{1,m,{\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2}}}(G,H)&:=\int_{0}^{t}s\,\tau_{m}(s)\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{6}}e^{-is\Phi_{\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2}}(\xi,\eta,\sigma)}\widetilde{G}(s,\eta)\,\widetilde{H}(s,\sigma)\,\,b(\xi,\eta,\sigma)\,\mu^{R}(\xi,\eta,\sigma)d\eta d\sigma\,ds,\\
D_{2,m,{\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2}}}(G,H)&:=\int_{0}^{t}\tau_{m}(s)\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{6}}e^{-is\Phi_{\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2}}(\xi,\eta,\sigma)}\widetilde{G}(s,\eta)\,\widetilde{H}(s,\sigma)\,\,b^{\prime}(\xi,\eta,\sigma)\,\nabla_{\xi}\mu^{R}(\xi,\eta,\sigma)d\eta d\sigma\,ds,\end{split}

(7.9)

where

\displaystyle b^{\prime}(\xi,\eta,\sigma)

\displaystyle:=\frac{\varphi_{\leqslant M_{0}}(\langle\eta\rangle)\varphi_{\leqslant M_{0}}(\langle\sigma\rangle)\varphi_{\leqslant M_{0}}(\langle\xi\rangle)}{\langle\eta\rangle\langle\sigma\rangle},\qquad b(\xi,\eta,\sigma):=\frac{\xi}{\langle\xi\rangle}b^{\prime}(\xi,\eta,\sigma),

(7.10)

and, for any
k_{1},k_{2}\leqslant M_{0}

\displaystyle\begin{split}E_{1,k_{1},k_{2},m,{\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2}}}(G,H)&:=\int_{0}^{t}s\,\tau_{m}(s)\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{6}}e^{-is\Phi_{\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2}}(\xi,\eta,\sigma)}\widetilde{G}(s,\eta)\,\widetilde{H}(s,\sigma)\,\\
&\qquad\qquad\times\,b_{Re}(\xi,\eta,\sigma)\,\mu^{Re}(\xi,\eta,\sigma)d\eta d\sigma\,ds,\\
E_{2,k_{1},k_{2},m,{\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2}}}(G,H)&:=\int_{0}^{t}\tau_{m}(s)\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{6}}e^{-is\Phi_{\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2}}(\xi,\eta,\sigma)}\widetilde{G}(s,\eta)\,\widetilde{H}(s,\sigma)\,\\
&\qquad\qquad\times\,b^{\prime}_{Re}(\xi,\eta,\sigma)\,\nabla_{\xi}\mu^{Re}(\xi,\eta,\sigma)d\eta d\sigma\,ds,\end{split}

(7.11)

where

\displaystyle b^{\prime}_{Re}(\xi,\eta,s)

\displaystyle:=\frac{\varphi_{k_{1}}(\eta)\varphi_{k_{2}}(\sigma)\varphi_{\leqslant M_{0}}(\langle\xi\rangle)}{\langle\eta\rangle\langle\sigma\rangle},\qquad b_{Re}(\xi,\eta,\sigma):=\frac{\xi}{\langle\xi\rangle}b^{\prime}_{Re}(\xi,\eta,s).

Note that we are omitting the variables
(t,\xi)
from the above expressions, as this should cause no confusion.
We are also omitting the
k_{1},k_{2}
dependence of the last two symbols.

By definition we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}\nabla_{\xi}T_{m}^{{\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2}},(3)}(G,H)&=D_{1,m,{\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2}}}[G,H](t,\xi)+D_{2,m,{\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2}}}[G,H](t,\xi)\\
\nabla_{\xi}T_{m}^{{\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2}},(4)}(G,H)&=\sum_{k_{1},k_{2}\leqslant M_{0}}\Big(E_{1,k_{1},k_{2},m,{\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2}}}(G,H)+E_{2,k_{1},k_{2},m,{\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2}}}(G,H)\Big).\end{split}

(7.12)

We can obviously bound
D_{2,m,{\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2}}}
and
E_{2,k_{1},k_{2},m,{\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2}}}
, respectively, by the quantities

\displaystyle D_{2,m,{\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2}}}^{\prime}(G,H):=2^{m}\sup_{s\approx 2^{m}}\Big|\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{6}}e^{is({\epsilon}_{1}\langle\eta\rangle+{\epsilon}_{2}\langle\sigma\rangle)}\widetilde{G}(s,\eta)\,\widetilde{H}(s,\sigma)\,\,b^{\prime}(\xi,\eta,\sigma)\,\nabla_{\xi}\mu^{R}(\xi,\eta,\sigma)d\eta d\sigma\Big|

(7.13)

and

\displaystyle\begin{split}E_{2,k_{1},k_{2},m,{\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2}}}^{\prime}(G,H):=2^{m}\sup_{s\approx 2^{m}}\Big|\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{6}}e^{is({\epsilon}_{1}\langle\eta\rangle+{\epsilon}_{2}\langle\sigma\rangle)}\widetilde{G}(s,\eta)\,\widetilde{H}(s,\sigma)\,\\
\times\,b^{\prime}_{Re}(\xi,\eta,\sigma)\,\nabla_{\xi}\mu^{Re}(\xi,\eta,\sigma)d\eta d\sigma\Big|.\end{split}

(7.14)

We are now ready to reduce the proof of Proposition
7.1

to proving bounds on the operators defined above.

Lemma 7.4
.

With the definitions (
7.9
), (
7.11
) and (
7.13
)-(
7.14
),
assume that for all
m=0,1,...,L+1

and all
G,H\in\{g,h\}
, the following estimates hold:

\displaystyle\|D_{1,m,{\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2}}}(G_{{\epsilon}_{1}},H_{{\epsilon}_{2}})\|_{L^{2}},\sum_{k_{1},k_{2}\leqslant M_{0}}\|E_{1,k_{1},k_{2},m,{\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2}}}(G_{{\epsilon}_{1}},H_{{\epsilon}_{2}})\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\rho(2^{m})^{1-\beta+\delta^{\prime}}2^{m}\varepsilon^{\beta},

(7.15)

\displaystyle\|D_{2,m,{\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2}}}^{\prime}(G_{{\epsilon}_{1}},H_{{\epsilon}_{2}})\|_{L^{2}},\sum_{k_{1},k_{2}\leqslant M_{0}}\|E_{2,k_{1},k_{2},m,{\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2}}}^{\prime}(G_{{\epsilon}_{1}},H_{{\epsilon}_{2}})\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim 2^{-m/10}\cdot\rho(2^{m})2^{m}\varepsilon^{\beta},

(7.16)

for some
\delta^{\prime}>\delta
.
Then, (
7.2
) and (
7.3
) hold.

Notice the extra factor of
2^{-m/10}
in (
7.16
).

Proof.

It is clear from (
7.12
) and the definitions (
7.13
) and (
7.14
)
that (
7.15
)-(
7.16
) imply (
7.2
).
To show that (
7.15
)-(
7.16
) imply (
7.3
)
we use Lemma
7.2
and an interpolation argument. First, we observe that

\displaystyle{\|f\|}_{L^{\frac{6}{5+6b}}_{x}}\lesssim{\|\langle x\rangle f\|}_{L^{2}_{x}}^{\alpha(b)}{\|\langle x\rangle^{2}f\|}_{L^{2}_{x}}^{1-\alpha(b)},

where
0<\alpha(b)\rightarrow 1
as
b\rightarrow 0
.
Then, using Lemma
7.2
we see that (
7.3
) would follow from
(we disregard the
L^{2}
-norms without derivatives which satisfy stronger bounds)

\displaystyle{\|\nabla_{\xi}I\|}_{L^{2}_{x}}^{\alpha(b)}\cdot{\|\nabla_{\xi}^{2}I\|}_{L^{2}_{x}}^{1-\alpha(b)}\lesssim\frac{\rho_{0}^{a}}{\langle s\rangle^{1+2a}},\qquad a\in(0,b),

(7.17)

where

\displaystyle I(s):=e^{is\langle\xi\rangle}\partial_{s}T^{{\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2}},(j)}_{m}(G_{\epsilon_{1}},H_{\epsilon_{2}}),\qquad j=3,4.

(7.18)

Let us just concentrate on the case
j=3
, as the case
j=4
is identical thanks
to the estimate satisfied by
\mu^{Re}
; see (
5.24
).
In particular, the definition (
7.13
) and the assumption (
7.16
) imply that, for all
s\approx 2^{m}
,

\displaystyle{\|\nabla_{\xi}I(s)\|}_{L^{2}}\lesssim 2^{-m/10}\cdot\rho(2^{m})\varepsilon^{\beta}.

(7.19)

Therefore, since
\alpha(b)
can be made sufficiently close to
1

by choosing
b
small (so that
a
is a also sufficiently small)
in order to obtain (
7.17
) it would suffice
to show that

\displaystyle\sup_{s\approx 2^{m}}{\|\nabla_{\xi}^{2}I(s)\|}_{L^{2}_{x}}\lesssim 2^{3m}.

(7.20)

This is of course far from optimal but suffices for our purpose since any growth in the above right-hand side
can be offset by an appropriate choice of small enough
b
.

To prove (
7.20
) we start by differentiating (
7.5
).
We can disregard the easier terms where
\nabla_{\xi}
hits the symbol
b^{\prime}
.
Using (
5.15
)-(
5.16
) to expand
\mu^{R}=\mu^{R}_{1}
,
exactly as we did for
\mu_{1}^{S}
in (
6.35
), we find that

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\Bigg\|\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{6}}e^{is({\epsilon}_{1}\langle\eta\rangle+{\epsilon}_{2}\langle\sigma\rangle)}\widetilde{G}(s,\eta)\,\widetilde{H}(s,\sigma)\,\,b^{\prime}(\xi,\eta,\sigma)\,\nabla_{\xi}^{2}\mu^{R}(\xi,\eta,\sigma)d\eta d\sigma\Bigg\|_{L^{2}}\\
&\lesssim\sup_{s\approx 2^{m}}\sum_{k_{1},k_{2}\leqslant M_{0}}\big\|T^{R,1}_{1}[\partial_{1}^{2}\nu_{1}^{R},b_{1}](e^{is{\epsilon}_{1}\langle\xi\rangle}\widetilde{G_{{\epsilon}_{1}}},e^{is\epsilon_{2}\langle\xi\rangle}\widetilde{H_{{\epsilon}_{2}}})\big\|_{L^{2}}\\
&+\big\|T^{R,1}_{1}[\partial_{1}^{2}\nu_{1}^{R};b_{1}^{\prime}](e^{is\epsilon_{2}\langle\xi\rangle}\widetilde{H_{{\epsilon}_{2}}},e^{is{\epsilon}_{1}\langle\xi\rangle}\widetilde{G_{{\epsilon}_{1}}})\big\|_{L^{2}}\\
&+\big\|T_{1}^{R,2}[\partial_{2}^{2}\nu_{1}^{R},b_{2}](e^{is\epsilon_{1}\langle\xi\rangle}\widetilde{G_{{\epsilon}_{1}}},e^{is\epsilon_{2}\langle\xi\rangle}\widetilde{H_{{\epsilon}_{2}}})\big\|_{L^{2}},\end{split}

(7.21)

where we are adopting the notation (
7.6
),
using
\partial_{j}\nu_{1}^{R}
to denote differentiation in the
j
-th variable,
and where the symbols are defined, as in (
6.21
), by

\displaystyle\begin{split}&b_{1}(\xi,\eta,\sigma)=b^{\prime}(\xi,\xi-\eta,\sigma)\varphi_{k_{1}}(\eta)\varphi_{k_{2}}(\sigma),\\
&b_{1}^{\prime}(\xi,\eta,\sigma)=b^{\prime}(\xi,\xi-\sigma,\eta)\varphi_{k_{1}}(\eta)\varphi_{k_{2}}(\sigma),\\
&b_{2}(\xi,\eta,\sigma)=b^{\prime}(\xi,-\eta-\sigma,\sigma)\varphi_{k_{1}}(\eta)\varphi_{k_{2}}(\sigma).\end{split}

(7.22)

We bound the expressions in (
7.21
) using
the pointwise bound (
5.19
) which, in particular, implies, for all
|p|\approx 2^{P}

and
|q|\approx 2^{Q}
with
P,Q\leqslant M_{0}+10
that

\displaystyle\begin{split}&|\nabla_{p}^{2}\nu_{1}^{R}(p,q)|\lesssim 2^{-4(P\vee Q)}\cdot 2^{22M_{0}},\\
&|\nabla_{q}^{2}\nu_{1}^{R}(p,q)|\lesssim 2^{-2(P\vee Q)}\cdot 2^{-Q}\cdot 2^{22M_{0}}.\end{split}

(7.23)

Recalling the definition (
7.6
),
using Hölder in
\xi
, and bounding
{\|b^{\prime}\|}_{L^{\infty}}\lesssim 1
, we find that, for
s\approx 2^{m}
,

\displaystyle\big\|T^{R,1}_{1}[\partial_{1}^{2}\nu_{1}^{R};b_{1}](e^{is\epsilon_{1}\langle\xi\rangle}\widetilde{G_{{\epsilon}_{1}}},e^{is\epsilon_{2}\langle\xi\rangle}\widetilde{H_{{\epsilon}_{2}}})\big\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim 2^{3M_{0}/2}\cdot\sup_{|\xi|\lesssim 2^{M_{0}}}\bigg|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{6}}e^{is({\epsilon}_{1}\langle\xi-\eta\rangle+{\epsilon}_{2}\langle\sigma\rangle)}b_{1}(\xi,\eta,\sigma)\widetilde{G_{{\epsilon}_{1}}}(s,\xi-\eta)\widetilde{H_{{\epsilon}_{2}}}(s,\sigma)\,\nabla^{2}_{\eta}\nu_{1}^{R}(\eta,\sigma)\,d\eta d\sigma\bigg|

\displaystyle\lesssim 2^{3M_{0}/2}\sup_{|\xi|\lesssim 2^{M_{0}}}\Big(\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}|\widetilde{G_{{\epsilon}_{1}}}(\xi-\eta)|\varphi_{k_{1}}(\eta)\,d\eta\Big)\Big(\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}|\widetilde{H_{{\epsilon}_{2}}}(\sigma)|\varphi_{k_{2}}(\sigma)d\sigma\Big)\cdot 2^{-4(k_{1}\vee k_{2})}2^{22M_{0}}

\displaystyle\lesssim 2^{25M_{0}}\cdot 2^{-4(k_{1}\vee k_{2})}\cdot 2^{5k_{1}/2}\|\widetilde{G_{{\epsilon}_{1}}}\|_{L^{6}}\cdot 2^{5k_{2}/2}\|\widetilde{H_{{\epsilon}_{2}}}\|_{L^{6}}

\displaystyle\lesssim 2^{25M_{0}}\cdot 2^{(5/2)(k_{1}+k_{2})}\cdot 2^{-4(k_{1}\vee k_{2})}\cdot\rho(2^{m})^{2}\cdot 2^{3m}m^{4},

(7.24)

where we used Sobolev’s embedding to get the crude bound

\displaystyle\|\widetilde{F}(\xi)\|_{L^{6}}\lesssim\|\nabla_{\xi}\widetilde{F}\|_{L^{2}}\lesssim\rho(2^{m})2^{3m/2}m^{2},\qquad F\in\{g,h\},

see (
4.13
) and (
4.26
).

Similarly, we also have

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\big\|T^{R,1}_{1}[\partial_{1}^{2}\nu_{1}^{R};b_{1}^{\prime}](e^{is\epsilon_{2}\langle\xi\rangle}\widetilde{H},e^{is\epsilon_{1}\langle\xi\rangle}\widetilde{G})\big\|_{L^{2}}\\
&\lesssim 2^{(5/2)(k_{1}+k_{2})}\cdot 2^{-4(k_{1}\vee k_{2})}2^{25M_{0}}\cdot\rho(2^{m})^{2}\cdot 2^{3m}m^{4}.\end{split}

(7.25)

The estimates (
7.24
)-(
7.25
) can then be summed over
k_{1},k_{2}\leqslant M_{0}
to obtain
a bound consistent with (
7.20
).

Finally, recalling the definition (
7.6
) and the second estimate in (
7.23
)
we get, for
k\leqslant M_{0}
,
s\approx 2^{m}
,

\displaystyle\big\|P_{k}T_{1}^{R,2}[\partial_{2}^{2}\nu_{1}^{R};b_{2}](e^{is\epsilon_{1}\langle\xi\rangle}\widetilde{G_{{\epsilon}_{1}}},e^{is\epsilon_{2}\langle\xi\rangle}\widetilde{H_{{\epsilon}_{2}}})\big\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim 2^{3k/2}\cdot\sup_{|\xi|\approx 2^{k}}\bigg|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{6}}|\widetilde{G_{{\epsilon}_{1}}}(s,-\eta-\sigma)|\,|\widetilde{H_{{\epsilon}_{2}}}(s,\sigma)|\,|\nabla^{2}_{\xi}\nu_{1}^{R}(\eta,\xi)|\,\varphi_{k_{1}}(\eta)\varphi_{k_{2}}(\sigma)\,d\eta d\sigma\bigg|

\displaystyle\lesssim 2^{3k/2}\cdot 2^{5k_{1}/2}\|\widetilde{G_{{\epsilon}_{1}}}\|_{L^{6}}\cdot 2^{5k_{2}/2}\|\widetilde{H_{{\epsilon}_{2}}}\|_{L^{6}}\cdot 2^{-2(k\vee k_{2})}2^{-k}2^{22M_{0}}

\displaystyle\lesssim 2^{k/2}2^{22M_{0}}\cdot 2^{5k_{1}/2}2^{k_{2}/2}\cdot\rho(2^{m})^{2}\cdot 2^{3m}m^{4}.

This can be easily summed over the indexes
k,k_{1},k_{2}
to obtain a bound as in (
7.20
).
We have then obtained (
7.20
) and concluded the proof of the lemma.
∎

In view of Lemma
7.4
, in order to prove Proposition
7.1

it suffices to prove the estimate (
7.15
) and (
7.16
) for the operators in (
7.9
)-(
7.11
)
and (
7.13
)-(
7.14
).
We first prove the bounds (
7.15
) and (
7.16
) for the
(g,g)
interaction in Subsection
7.2
.
The bound (
7.16
) is proved in a unified manner for all remaining cases in Lemma
7.6
.
We then prove (
7.15
) for the
(g,h)
interaction in Subsection
7.3
,
and for the
(h,h)
interaction in Subsection
7.4
.

7.2.
Fermi interactions

The next lemma treats the discrete component self-interaction.

Lemma 7.5
.

Under the assumptions of Proposition
7.1
, the estimates (
7.15
) and (
7.16
)
hold when
(G,H)=(g,g)
.

Proof.

Let us use the short-hand

\displaystyle D_{1}(t,\xi):=D_{1,m,{\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2}}}[g_{\epsilon_{1}},g_{\epsilon_{2}}](t,\xi),\qquad D_{2}^{\prime}(\xi):=D_{2,m,{\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2}}}^{\prime}[g_{\epsilon_{1}},g_{\epsilon_{2}}](\xi)

for the operators in (
7.9
) and (
7.13
).

Estimate of
D_{1}
.

From the identity (
5.37
) (recall also the notation (
5.35
)), we have that

\displaystyle\begin{split}\|\widehat{\mathcal{F}}^{-1}_{\xi\mapsto x}D_{1}(t,\xi)\|_{L^{2}}&\lesssim 2^{2m}\sum_{k_{1},k_{2}\leqslant M_{0}}\sup_{s\approx 2^{m}}\bigg[\big\|T_{1}^{R,1}[b_{1}](e^{\epsilon_{1}is\langle\xi\rangle}\widetilde{g_{\epsilon_{1}}},e^{\epsilon_{2}is\langle\xi\rangle}\widetilde{g_{\epsilon_{2}}})\big\|_{L^{2}_{\xi}}\\
&+\big\|T_{1}^{R,1}[b_{1}^{\prime}](e^{\epsilon_{2}is\langle\xi\rangle}\widetilde{g_{\epsilon_{2}}},e^{\epsilon_{1}is\langle\xi\rangle}\widetilde{g_{\epsilon_{1}}})\big\|_{L^{2}_{\xi}}+\big\|T_{1}^{R,2}[b_{2}](e^{\epsilon_{1}is\langle\xi\rangle}\widetilde{g_{\epsilon_{1}}},e^{\epsilon_{2}is\langle\xi\rangle}\widetilde{g_{\epsilon_{2}}})\big\|_{L^{2}_{\xi}}\bigg],\end{split}

(7.26)

where the symbols are defined as in (
7.22
).

Let
\ell_{0}:=\lfloor-m+\delta m\rfloor.

We insert cut-offs
\varphi_{\ell_{1}}^{(\ell_{0})}(\langle\eta\rangle-2\lambda)

and
\varphi_{\ell_{2}}^{(\ell_{0})}(\langle\sigma\rangle-2\lambda)
and write,
using Hölder and the volume restriction
|\xi|\lesssim 2^{M_{0}},

\displaystyle\begin{split}&2^{2m}\big\|T_{1}^{R,1}[b_{1}](e^{\epsilon_{1}is\langle\xi\rangle}\widetilde{g_{\epsilon_{1}}},e^{\epsilon_{2}is\langle\xi\rangle}\widetilde{g_{\epsilon_{2}}})\big\|_{L^{2}_{\xi}}\\
&\lesssim 2^{2m}\cdot 2^{3M_{0}/2}\cdot\sum_{k_{1},k_{2}}\sum_{\ell_{1},\ell_{2}\geqslant\ell_{0}}\sup_{|\xi|\leqslant 2^{M_{0}}}\sup_{s\approx 2^{m}}\bigg|\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{6}}e^{is\epsilon_{1}\langle\eta\rangle}\big(\varphi_{\sim 0}\widetilde{g_{\epsilon_{1}}}\big)(s,\eta)\,e^{is\epsilon_{2}\langle\sigma\rangle}\big(\varphi_{\sim 0}\widetilde{g_{\epsilon_{2}}}\big)(s,\sigma)\\
&\times\varphi_{\ell_{1}}^{(\ell_{0})}(\langle\eta\rangle-2\lambda)\varphi_{\ell_{2}}^{(\ell_{0})}(\langle\sigma\rangle-2\lambda)\,b_{1}(\xi,\xi-\eta,\sigma)\nu^{R}_{1}(\xi-\eta,\sigma)d\eta d\sigma\bigg|,\end{split}

(7.27)

where we recall that, by our notation,

b_{1}(\xi,\xi-\eta,\sigma):=b(\xi,\eta,\sigma)\varphi_{k_{1}}(\xi-\eta)\varphi_{k_{2}}(\sigma)
,
see (
6.21
) and (
7.10
).
We can then disregard the sum over
k_{2}
, since
|\sigma|\approx 1
on the support of
\widetilde{g}(\sigma)
.
As for the sum over
k_{1}
, we may assume that
k_{1}\geqslant-10m
since
the complementary case can be treated directly integrating in
\eta
, and using the
crude bound (
4.28
) to control
{\|\widetilde{g}\|}_{L^{\infty}}
,
and the bound from (
5.19
)

\displaystyle|\varphi_{P}(p)\varphi_{Q}(q)\nu_{1}^{R}(p,q)|\lesssim 2^{-2(P\vee Q)}2^{10M_{0}}.

(7.28)

Notice that in applying this bound to (
7.27
) we have
2^{Q}=2^{k_{2}}\gtrsim 1
.
We may then bound (
7.27
) by a factor of
O(m)
times the bound for
the integral expression with fixed
k_{1},k_{2}
.

In the following argument we may assume without loss of generality that
\ell_{1}\geqslant\ell_{2}
since the complementary case is analogous (although not exactly symmetric).

If
\ell_{1}=\ell_{2}=\ell_{0}
, we use (
7.28
)
and (
4.24
) to integrate directly in (
7.27
) and obtain the bound

\displaystyle\begin{split}&C2^{2m}\cdot 2^{2\ell_{0}}\cdot 2^{3M_{0}/2}\cdot 2^{3(k_{1}+k_{2})}\cdot 2^{-2(k_{1}\vee k_{2})}2^{10M_{0}}\cdot{\|\widetilde{g}\|}_{L^{\infty}_{\xi}}^{2}\\
&\lesssim 2^{2\delta m}2^{16M_{0}}\big(m2^{m}\rho(2^{m}))^{2}\lesssim 2^{2\delta m}2^{16M_{0}}2^{m}\rho(2^{m})\cdot Z\rho(2^{m})^{\beta/2}m^{2};\end{split}

(7.29)

having used
2^{m}\rho(2^{m})\leqslant Z\rho^{\beta/2}(2^{m})
(recall the definition (
6.7
) and Remark
6.3
);
the bound obtained above suffices for (
7.15
) since
\beta>2\delta^{\prime}
.

If
\ell_{2}>\ell_{0}
we integrate by parts in both
\eta
and
\sigma
.
Also in view of (
5.24
) which guarantees that
differentiating
\nu_{1}^{R}
is analogous to differentiating innocuous cutoffs,
we see that the worst term corresponds to the case where both derivatives hit the profiles,
which is bounded as follows, using (
7.28
) and (
4.27
):

\displaystyle\begin{split}C2^{2m}\cdot 2^{3M_{0}/2}\cdot\sup_{s\approx 2^{m}}\sup_{|\xi|\leqslant 2^{M_{0}}}\sum_{\ell_{1},\ell_{2}>\ell_{0}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{6}}\frac{1}{s}|\big(\varphi_{\sim 0}\nabla_{\sigma}\widetilde{g}\big)(s,\sigma)|\frac{1}{s}|\big(\varphi_{\sim 0}\nabla_{\eta}\widetilde{g}\big)(s,\eta)|\\
\times\varphi_{\ell_{1}}(\langle\eta\rangle-2\lambda)\varphi_{\ell_{2}}(\langle\eta\rangle-2\lambda)\big|\nu^{R}_{1}(\xi-\eta,\sigma)\big|d\eta d\sigma\\
\lesssim 2^{23M_{0}/2}m^{2}\|\varphi_{\ell_{1}}\nabla_{\eta}\widetilde{g}\|_{L^{1}_{\eta}}\|\varphi_{\ell_{2}}\nabla_{\sigma}\widetilde{g}\|_{L^{1}_{\sigma}}\\
\lesssim 2^{23M_{0}/2}m^{4}\rho(2^{m})^{\beta/2}\cdot 2^{m}\rho(2^{m})\cdot Z.\end{split}

(7.30)

As above, this last upperbound is sufficient.

The final case
\ell_{0}=\ell_{2}<\ell_{1}
is treated similarly,
integrating by parts in
\eta
and integrating directly in
\sigma.

This will give a bound like (
7.29
) with an extra factor of
2^{\delta m}
,
which is acceptable for
\delta<\beta/2
.

The estimate for the second term in (
7.26
) is similar by symmetry.
Also the term
T_{1}^{R,2}
can be treated similarly with small modifications;
the only difference is that for this term we need to additionally localize in
|\xi|\approx 2^{k}

but can then also rely on the
\xi/\langle\xi\rangle
factor in
b
(see (
7.10
)).
We first insert the cutoffs in the expression for
T_{1}^{R,2}
(see (
5.34d
)) as in (
7.27
)
which leads to the analogous estimate

\displaystyle\begin{split}&2^{2m}\big\|P_{k}T_{1}^{R,2}[b_{2}](e^{\epsilon_{1}is\langle\xi\rangle}\widetilde{g_{\epsilon_{1}}},e^{\epsilon_{2}is\langle\xi\rangle}\widetilde{g_{\epsilon_{2}}})\big\|_{L^{2}_{\xi}}\\
&\lesssim 2^{2m}\cdot 2^{3k/2}\cdot\sum_{k_{1},k_{2}}\sum_{\ell_{1},\ell_{2}\geqslant\ell_{0}}\sup_{|\xi|\approx 2^{k}}\sup_{s\approx 2^{m}}\bigg|\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{6}}\frac{\xi}{\langle\xi\rangle}e^{is\epsilon_{1}\langle\eta\rangle}\big(\varphi_{\sim 0}\widetilde{g_{\epsilon_{1}}}\big)(s,\eta)\,e^{is\epsilon_{2}\langle\sigma\rangle}\big(\varphi_{\sim 0}\widetilde{g_{\epsilon_{2}}}\big)(s,\sigma)\\
&\times\varphi_{\ell_{1}}^{(\ell_{0})}(\langle\eta\rangle-2\lambda)\varphi_{\ell_{2}}^{(\ell_{0})}(\langle\sigma\rangle-2\lambda)\,b_{2}(\xi,-\eta-\sigma,\sigma)\overline{\nu^{R}_{1}(-\eta-\sigma,\xi)}d\eta d\sigma\bigg|,\end{split}

(7.31)

where we recall our notation

b_{2}(\xi,-\eta-\sigma,\sigma):=b(\xi,\eta,\sigma)\varphi_{k_{1}}(-\eta-\sigma)\varphi_{k_{2}}(\sigma)
,
see (
6.21
) and (
7.10
).
As before, we can dispense of the
k_{2}
index and the associated summation,
and of the
k_{1}
summation as well, up to an
O(m)
factor.

In the case
\ell_{1},\ell_{2}>\ell_{0}
we integrate by parts in
\eta
and
\sigma

and see that the main contribution to (
7.31
) in this case is bounded by

\displaystyle\begin{split}C2^{2m}\cdot 2^{3k/2}2^{\min(k,0)}\sum_{\ell_{1},\ell_{2}>\ell_{0}}\sup_{|\xi|\approx 2^{k}}\sup_{s\approx 2^{m}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{6}}\frac{1}{s}|\varphi_{\sim 0}\nabla_{\eta}\widetilde{g_{\epsilon_{1}}}(s,\eta)|\,\frac{1}{s}|\varphi_{\sim 0}\nabla_{\sigma}\widetilde{g_{\epsilon_{2}}}(s,\sigma)|\\
\times\varphi_{\ell_{1}}(\langle\eta\rangle-2\lambda)\varphi_{\ell_{2}}(\langle\sigma\rangle-2\lambda)\,|\nu^{R}_{1}(-\eta-\sigma,\xi)|\,d\eta d\sigma\\
\lesssim 2^{3k/2}2^{\min(k,0)}\cdot{\|\varphi_{\ell_{1}}\nabla_{\eta}\widetilde{g}\|}_{L^{1}_{\eta}}{\|\varphi_{\ell_{2}}\nabla_{\sigma}\widetilde{g}\|}_{L^{1}_{\sigma}}\cdot 2^{-2k}2^{10M_{0}},\end{split}

(7.32)

having used (
7.28
) to bound
|\nu^{R}_{1}(-\eta-\sigma,\xi)|\lesssim 2^{-2k}
;
we can then conclude as in (
7.30
) using (
4.27
).

The remaining cases
\ell_{1}>\ell_{2}=\ell_{0}
and
\ell_{1}=\ell_{2}=\ell_{0}
can be handled
as explained before, with the additional exploitation of the extra
2^{k^{-}}
factor.

Estimate of
D_{2}^{\prime}(t,\xi)
.

We now show how to obtain (
7.16
) for the operator in (
7.13
) with (
7.10
).
Expanding
\mu^{R}
as above, see for example (
7.21
)
where there are two
\xi
derivatives instead of just one, as in the present case, we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}{\|\widehat{\mathcal{F}}^{-1}_{\xi\mapsto x}D_{2}^{\prime}(\xi)\|}_{L^{2}}&\leqslant 2^{m}\sup_{s\approx 2^{m}}{\Big\|\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{6}}e^{is({\epsilon}_{1}\langle\eta\rangle+{\epsilon}_{2}\langle\sigma\rangle)}\widetilde{g_{{\epsilon}_{1}}}(t,\eta)\,\widetilde{g_{{\epsilon}_{2}}}(t,\sigma)\,\,b^{\prime}(\xi,\eta,\sigma)\,\nabla_{\xi}\mu^{R}(\xi,\eta,\sigma)d\eta d\sigma\Big\|}_{L^{2}}\\
&\lesssim 2^{m}\sup_{s\approx 2^{m}}\sum_{k_{1},k_{2}\leqslant M_{0}}\big\|T^{R,1}_{1}[\partial_{1}\nu_{1}^{R},b_{1}](e^{is{\epsilon}_{1}\langle\xi\rangle}\widetilde{g_{{\epsilon}_{1}}},e^{is\epsilon_{2}\langle\xi\rangle}\widetilde{g_{{\epsilon}_{2}}})\big\|_{L^{2}}\\
&+\big\|T^{R,1}_{1}[\partial_{1}\nu_{1}^{R};b_{1}^{\prime}](e^{is\epsilon_{2}\langle\xi\rangle}\widetilde{g_{{\epsilon}_{2}}},e^{is{\epsilon}_{1}\langle\xi\rangle}\widetilde{g_{{\epsilon}_{1}}})\big\|_{L^{2}}\\
&+\big\|T_{1}^{R,2}[\partial_{2}\nu_{1}^{R},b_{2}](e^{is\epsilon_{1}\langle\xi\rangle}\widetilde{g_{{\epsilon}_{1}}},e^{is\epsilon_{2}\langle\xi\rangle}\widetilde{g_{{\epsilon}_{2}}})\big\|_{L^{2}}.\end{split}

(7.33)

The symbols are as in (
7.22
), and we are adopting the notation (
7.6
).

We note that in (
7.33
) the terms involving the operator
T_{1}^{R,1}
, see (
5.34b
),
are such that
|\sigma|\approx 1
on their support.
Then, in view of (
5.19
), the corresponding measure
\partial_{1}\nu^{R}_{1}(\eta,\sigma)
satisfies the same
pointwise estimate as
\nu^{R}_{1}(\eta,\sigma)
.
Therefore the exact same estimates as those established for
D_{1}
apply.
Comparing with (
7.26
), we see that in this case we have one less
2^{m}
factor
hence the estimates already established give a bound of the form
2^{-m}
times the right-hand side of (
7.15
)
which is sufficient for (
7.16
).

Next, we look at the operator
T_{1}^{R,2}[\partial_{2}\nu_{1}^{R},b_{2}]
on the last line of (
7.33
).
This term can be treated similarly to the one in (
7.31
).
The only difference is that we now have one additional advantageous factor of
2^{-m}

but also an extra
\xi
derivative on
\nu_{1}^{R}
,
which gives a
2^{-(k\vee k_{1})}
factor (see (
7.28
))
compared with the bound in (
7.32
).
Proceeding as before, with the same notation used in (
7.31
), we first estimate

\displaystyle\begin{split}&2^{m}\big\|P_{k}T_{1}^{R,2}[b_{2}](e^{\epsilon_{1}is\langle\xi\rangle}\widetilde{g_{\epsilon_{1}}},e^{\epsilon_{2}is\langle\xi\rangle}\widetilde{g_{\epsilon_{2}}})\big\|_{L^{2}_{\xi}}\\
&\lesssim 2^{2m}\cdot 2^{3k/2}\cdot\sum_{\ell_{1},\ell_{2}\geqslant\ell_{0}}\sup_{|\xi|\approx 2^{k}}\sup_{s\approx 2^{m}}\bigg|\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{6}}\frac{\xi}{\langle\xi\rangle}e^{is\epsilon_{1}\langle\eta\rangle}\big(\varphi_{\sim 0}\widetilde{g_{\epsilon_{1}}}\big)(s,\eta)\,e^{is\epsilon_{2}\langle\sigma\rangle}\big(\varphi_{\sim 0}\widetilde{g_{\epsilon_{2}}}\big)(s,\sigma)\\
&\times\varphi_{\ell_{1}}^{(\ell_{0})}(\langle\eta\rangle-2\lambda)\varphi_{\ell_{2}}^{(\ell_{0})}(\langle\sigma\rangle-2\lambda)\,b_{2}(\xi,-\eta-\sigma,\sigma)\partial_{\xi}\overline{\nu^{R}_{1}(-\eta-\sigma,\xi)}d\eta d\sigma\bigg|.\end{split}

(7.34)

Note that we have disregarded the innocuous sum over
k_{1},k_{2}
as before.
For
\ell_{1},\ell_{2}>\ell_{0}
we integrate by parts and obtain the bound (compare with (
7.32
))

\displaystyle\begin{split}C2^{m}\cdot 2^{3k/2}2^{\min(k,0)}\sum_{\ell_{1},\ell_{2}>\ell_{0}}\sup_{|\xi|\approx 2^{k}}\sup_{s\approx 2^{m}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{6}}\frac{1}{s}|\varphi_{\sim 0}\nabla_{\eta}\widetilde{g_{\epsilon_{1}}}(s,\eta)|\,\frac{1}{s}|\varphi_{\sim 0}\nabla_{\sigma}\widetilde{g_{\epsilon_{2}}}(s,\sigma)|\\
\times\varphi_{\ell_{1}}(\langle\eta\rangle-2\lambda)\varphi_{\ell_{2}}(\langle\sigma\rangle-2\lambda)\,|\partial_{\xi}\nu^{R}_{1}(-\eta-\sigma,\xi)|\,d\eta d\sigma\\
\lesssim 2^{-m}\cdot 2^{3k/2}2^{\min(k,0)}\cdot\sum_{\ell_{1},\ell_{2}>\ell_{0}}{\|\varphi_{\ell_{1}}\nabla_{\eta}\widetilde{g}\|}_{L^{1}_{\eta}}{\|\varphi_{\ell_{2}}\nabla_{\sigma}\widetilde{g}\|}_{L^{1}_{\sigma}}\cdot 2^{-3(k\vee k_{1})}2^{10M_{0}}\\
\lesssim 2^{-m+11M_{0}}\cdot 2^{-(1/2)(k\vee k_{1})}\cdot(m2^{m}\rho(2^{m}))^{2},\end{split}

(7.35)

having used (
7.28
) to bound, on the support of the integral,

|\partial_{\xi}\nu^{R}_{1}(-\eta-\sigma,\xi)|\lesssim 2^{-3(k\vee k_{1})}
and (
4.27
).
The bound (
7.35
) is more than sufficient
for (
7.16
) provided, for example, that
k\vee k_{1}\geqslant-m/4
.

In the remaining case
k\vee k_{1}<-m/4
, we only integrate by parts in
\sigma
but not in
\eta

and can bound as follows:

\displaystyle\begin{split}C2^{m}\cdot 2^{3k/2}2^{\min(k,0)}\sum_{\ell_{1},\ell_{2}>\ell_{0}}\sup_{|\xi|\approx 2^{k}}\sup_{s\approx 2^{m}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{6}}|\varphi_{\sim 0}\widetilde{g_{\epsilon_{1}}}(s,\eta)|\,\frac{1}{s}|\varphi_{\sim 0}\nabla_{\sigma}\widetilde{g_{\epsilon_{2}}}(s,\sigma)|\\
\times\varphi_{\ell_{1}}(\langle\eta\rangle-2\lambda)\varphi_{\ell_{2}}(\langle\sigma\rangle-2\lambda)\,|\partial_{\xi}\nu^{R}_{1}(-\eta-\sigma,\xi)|\,d\eta d\sigma\\
\lesssim 2^{3k/2}2^{\min(k,0)}\cdot\sum_{\ell_{1},\ell_{2}}2^{3k_{1}}{\|\varphi_{\ell_{1}}\widetilde{g}\|}_{L^{\infty}_{\eta}}{\|\varphi_{\ell_{2}}\nabla_{\sigma}\widetilde{g}\|}_{L^{1}_{\sigma}}\cdot 2^{-3(k\vee k_{1})}2^{10M_{0}}\\
\lesssim 2^{11M_{0}}\cdot 2^{(5/2)k_{1}}\cdot(m2^{m}\rho(2^{m}))^{2},\end{split}

having used (
4.24
) and (
4.27
).
Since
k_{1}\leqslant-m/4
this is enough for (
7.16
).

The estimates for the operators
E
with the
\mu^{Re}
distribution, see (
7.11
) and (
7.14
),
can be done very similarly to
T^{R,1}_{1}
, using the estimate (
5.24
),
therefore we can skip the details.
∎

Next, we prove (
7.16
) in a unified manner for all remaining cases.

Lemma 7.6
.

Under the assumptions of Proposition
7.1
the bound (
7.16
) holds for
(G,H)=(g,h),(h,g),(h,h).

Proof.

Since
h
has better localization than
g,
we can integrate by parts and
rely on the Hölder-type estimate associated to the operators

\partial_{1}\nu_{1}^{R}
,
\partial_{2}\nu_{1}^{R}
from Lemma
7.3
.
We use similar notation to the one in the proof of Lemma
7.5
,
and seek to estimate
{\|\widehat{\mathcal{F}}^{-1}_{\xi\mapsto x}D_{2}^{\prime}(\xi)\|}_{L^{2}}
,
see the definitions (
7.13
) and (
7.10
), by the right-hand side of (
7.16
).
For this it suffices to control, for
G\in\{g,h\}
,

\displaystyle\begin{split}&2^{m}\sup_{s\approx 2^{m}}{\Big\|\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{6}}e^{is({\epsilon}_{1}\langle\eta\rangle+{\epsilon}_{2}\langle\sigma\rangle)}\widetilde{G_{{\epsilon}_{1}}}(t,\eta)\,\widetilde{h_{{\epsilon}_{2}}}(t,\sigma)\,\,b^{\prime}(\xi,\eta,\sigma)\,\nabla_{\xi}\mu^{R}(\xi,\eta,\sigma)d\eta d\sigma\Big\|}_{L^{2}}\\
&\lesssim 2^{m}\sup_{s\approx 2^{m}}\sum_{k_{1},k_{2}\leqslant M_{0}}{\big\|T^{R,1}_{1}[\partial_{1}\nu_{1}^{R},b_{1}](e^{is{\epsilon}_{1}\langle\xi\rangle}\widetilde{G_{{\epsilon}_{1}}},e^{is\epsilon_{2}\langle\xi\rangle}\varphi_{\sim k_{2}}\widetilde{h_{{\epsilon}_{2}}})\big\|}_{L^{2}}\\
&+{\big\|T^{R,1}_{1}[\partial_{1}\nu_{1}^{R};b_{1}^{\prime}](e^{is\epsilon_{2}\langle\xi\rangle}\varphi_{\sim k_{2}}\widetilde{h_{{\epsilon}_{2}}},e^{is{\epsilon}_{1}\langle\xi\rangle}\widetilde{G_{{\epsilon}_{1}}})\big\|}_{L^{2}}\\
&+{\big\|T_{1}^{R,2}[\partial_{2}\nu_{1}^{R},b_{2}](e^{is\epsilon_{1}\langle\xi\rangle}\widetilde{G_{{\epsilon}_{1}}},e^{is\epsilon_{2}\langle\xi\rangle}\varphi_{\sim k_{2}}\widetilde{h_{{\epsilon}_{2}}})\big\|}_{L^{2}};\end{split}

(7.36)

see the analogous (
7.33
) (and recall (
7.22
) and (
7.6
))
and notice that we have explicitly written out the localization at frequencies
\approx 2^{k_{2}}
for the input
h
.

Let us look at the first term on the right-hand side of (
7.36
).
Recalling the definition (
7.6
) we integrate by parts in
\sigma
, the frequency variable for
h
,
when
k_{2}\geqslant-m/2+\delta m
, for some small
\delta>0
.
The main term that we obtain is the one where
\nabla_{\sigma}
hits the profile, that is (once again we can disregard the summation over
k_{1},k_{2}
)

\displaystyle\begin{split}2^{m}\sup_{s\approx 2^{m}}{\big\|T^{R,1}_{1}[\partial_{1}\nu_{1}^{R},b_{1}]\big(e^{is{\epsilon}_{1}\langle\xi\rangle}\widetilde{G_{{\epsilon}_{1}}},\,s^{-1}\,(\langle\xi\rangle\xi)/|\xi|^{2}\,e^{is\epsilon_{2}\langle\xi\rangle}\varphi_{k_{2}}\nabla_{\xi}\widetilde{h_{{\epsilon}_{2}}}\big)\big\|}_{L^{2}}.\end{split}

(7.37)

Using Lemma
7.3
,
the decay estimates (
4.18
)-(
4.19
)
which imply

\displaystyle\|e^{isL}G\|_{L^{8/3}}\lesssim\rho(2^{m})^{1-\beta}2^{5m/8+10\delta_{N}m},\qquad G\in\{g,h\},

Bernstein’s inequality, and the usual a priori bound (
4.13
), we control (
7.37
) by

\displaystyle C\|e^{isL}G\|_{L^{8/3}}\cdot 2^{-k_{2}}\|P_{k_{2}}\widetilde{\mathcal{F}}^{-1}\nabla_{\xi}\widetilde{h}\|_{L^{8}}

\displaystyle\lesssim 2^{(C_{0}+10)\delta_{N}m}\cdot\rho(2^{m})^{1-\beta}2^{5m/8}\cdot 2^{-k_{2}}\cdot 2^{9k_{2}/8}\rho(2^{m})^{1-\beta}2^{m}{\varepsilon}^{\beta};

this is bounded by the right-hand side of (
7.16
)
provided, as usual, that
\delta_{N}
and
\beta
are small enough.

In the case when
k_{2}\leqslant-m/2+\delta m
, we cannot integrate by parts; we instead
use directly
7.3
followed by Bernstein’s and Hardy’s inequality
to estimate (once again we disregard the
k_{1}
summation up to an
O(m)
factor)

\displaystyle\begin{split}&2^{m}\sup_{s\approx 2^{m}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}k_{1}\leqslant M_{0}\\
k_{2}\leqslant-m/2+\delta m\end{subarray}}{\big\|T^{R,1}_{1}[\partial_{1}\nu_{1}^{R},b_{1}](e^{is{\epsilon}_{1}\langle\xi\rangle}\widetilde{G_{{\epsilon}_{1}}},e^{is\epsilon_{2}\langle\xi\rangle}\varphi_{\sim k_{2}}\widetilde{h_{{\epsilon}_{2}}})\big\|}_{L^{2}}\\
&\lesssim 2^{m}\cdot 2^{(C_{0}+1)M_{0}}\cdot{\|G\|}_{L^{2}}\cdot\sum_{k_{2}\leqslant-m/2+\delta m}{\|P_{k_{2}}h\|}_{L^{\infty-}}\\
&\lesssim 2^{m}\cdot 2^{(C_{0}+2)M_{0}}\cdot\sum_{k_{2}\leqslant-m/2+\delta m}2^{(\frac{5}{2}-)k_{2}}{\|\nabla_{\xi}\widetilde{h}\|}_{L^{2}}\\
&\lesssim 2^{(C_{0}+3)M_{0}}\cdot 2^{-m/4}Z,\end{split}

which suffices for (
7.16
).

The second term on the right-hand side of (
7.36
) is symmetric so we can skip it.
The third term can also be treated similarly integrating by parts in
\partial_{\eta-\sigma}

(or changing variables
\eta\mapsto\eta-\sigma
, and then integrating by parts in
\sigma
as above),
and using Lemma
7.3
.

We can use the same arguments to estimate the
E^{\prime}
term as well (see the definition (
7.14
))
since
T[\partial_{1}\mu^{Re};b_{Re}^{\prime}]
(see the definition (
5.34b
)),
satisfies similar or better estimates than the operators just treated;
see the estimates (
7.8
) in Lemma
7.3
, and
the pointwise bound (
5.24
). ∎

7.3.
Fermi-continuous interaction

We now treat the regular terms with Fermi-continuous type interactions. We will use
several notation and arguments similar to those in Subsection
7.2
above.

Lemma 7.7
.

With the assumptions and notation of Proposition
7.1
,
the bound (
7.15
) holds for
(G,H)=(g,h),(h,g).

Proof.

Recall the definition of the terms
D_{1}(G,H):=D_{1,m,{\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2}}}(G,H)
and
E_{1}(G,H):=E_{1,k_{1},k_{2},m,{\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2}}}(G,H)

from (
7.9
) and (
7.11
).
We will mostly concentrate on
D_{1}(g,h)
;
the treatment of the terms in
D_{1}(h,g)
is similar (or simpler in many cases)
and we will only provide some indications on how to handle them. The starting point is the estimate

\displaystyle\begin{split}\|\widehat{\mathcal{F}}^{-1}_{\xi\mapsto x}D_{1}(t,\xi)\|_{L^{2}}&\lesssim\sum_{k_{1},k_{2}\leqslant M_{0}}\sup_{s\approx 2^{m}}\int_{0}^{t}s\tau_{m}(s)\bigg[\big\|T_{1}^{R,1}[b_{1}](e^{\epsilon_{1}is\langle\xi\rangle}\widetilde{g_{\epsilon_{1}}},e^{\epsilon_{2}is\langle\xi\rangle}\widetilde{h_{\epsilon_{2}}})\big\|_{L^{2}_{\xi}}\\
&+\big\|T_{1}^{R,1}[b_{1}^{\prime}](e^{\epsilon_{2}is\langle\xi\rangle}\widetilde{h_{\epsilon_{2}}},e^{\epsilon_{1}is\langle\xi\rangle}\widetilde{g_{\epsilon_{1}}})\big\|_{L^{2}_{\xi}}\\
&+\big\|T_{1}^{R,2}[b_{2}](e^{\epsilon_{1}is\langle\xi\rangle}\widetilde{g_{\epsilon_{1}}},e^{\epsilon_{2}is\langle\xi\rangle}\widetilde{h_{\epsilon_{2}}})\big\|_{L^{2}_{\xi}}\bigg]\,ds,\end{split}

(7.38)

which is a consequence of the identity (
5.37
) (see also the analogous (
7.26
));
as before, the symbols are defined as in (
7.22
) but with
b
instead of
b^{\prime}

on the right-hand side, and with
b
given by (
7.10
);
once again we left the
k_{1},k_{2}
dependence implicit.

Estimate of the
T_{1}^{R,1}[b_{1}]
contribution
.
We begin by looking at the main contribution
T_{1}^{R,1}[b_{1}]=T_{1}^{R,1}[\nu_{1}^{R};b_{1}]
.
The proof will proceed in several cases and subcases depending on the size of the
frequencies of the inputs, of the output and of the integration variables.
According to our usual notation (
7.6
), we localize the variables in

T_{1}^{R,1}[b_{1}]
by defining the following operator:
for fixed small
\delta
and
\underline{k}:=(k,k_{1},k_{2},\ell)
let

\displaystyle\begin{split}T_{\underline{k}}(t,\xi):=\int_{0}^{t}s\,\tau_{m}(s)\Big[\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{6}}e^{-is\Phi_{\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2}}(\xi,\xi-\eta,\sigma)}\widetilde{g_{\epsilon_{1}}}(s,\xi-\eta)\widetilde{h_{\epsilon_{2}}}(s,\sigma)\,\\
\times\varphi_{\underline{k}}(\xi,\eta,\sigma)\,b_{1}(\xi,\eta,\sigma)\,\nu_{1}^{R}(\eta,\sigma)\,d\eta d\sigma\Big]\,ds,\end{split}

(7.39)

where
\Phi_{\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2}}(\xi,\xi-\eta,\sigma)=-\langle\xi\rangle+{\epsilon}_{1}\langle\xi-\eta\rangle+{\epsilon}_{2}\langle\sigma\rangle
as
per our usual notation, and

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\varphi_{\underline{k}}(\xi,\eta,\sigma):=\varphi_{\sim k_{1}}(\eta)\varphi_{\sim k_{2}}(\sigma)\varphi_{k}(\xi)\varphi_{\ell}^{(\ell_{0})}(\langle\xi-\eta\rangle-2\lambda),\qquad\ell_{0}:=-m+\delta m,\\
&b_{1}(\xi,\eta,\sigma)=\frac{\xi}{\langle\xi\rangle}b^{\prime}(\xi,\xi-\eta,\sigma)\varphi_{k_{1}}(\eta)\varphi_{k_{2}}(\sigma),\end{split}

(7.40)

with
b^{\prime}
as in (
7.10
).
The variables in (
7.39
) are then localized as follows:

\displaystyle\begin{split}&|\xi|\approx 2^{k}\qquad|\eta|\approx 2^{k_{1}},\qquad|\sigma|\approx 2^{k_{2}},\qquad|\langle\xi-\eta\rangle-2\lambda|\approx 2^{\ell}.\\
&\mbox{with}\quad-5m<k,k_{1},k_{2}\leqslant M_{0},\qquad\ell_{0}<\ell<0,\end{split}

(7.41)

with
|\langle\xi-\eta\rangle-2\lambda|\lesssim 2^{-m+\delta m}
in the case
\ell=\ell_{0}
.

Our goal is to estimate the sum over
k,k_{1},k_{2},\ell
of the
L^{2}

norm of
T_{\underline{k}}
by the right-hand side of (
7.15
).
Since the sums are over
O(m^{4})
parameters, it also suffices to estimate

\|T_{\underline{k}}\|_{L^{2}}
for a single 4-tuple,
provided we can gain an arbitrary
2^{-(0+)m}
factor.

Case 1:
k_{1},k_{2}\leqslant-10
.
In this case
|\eta|,|\sigma|\leqslant 2^{-5}
and the phase is lower bounded uniformly:

\displaystyle|\Phi_{\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2}}(\xi,\xi-\eta,\sigma)|\gtrsim 1.

(7.42)

Indeed if
({\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2}})=(-,-)
then the result is obvious.
The cases
({\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2}})=(+,-),(+,+)
can be treated similarly by noticing that

\displaystyle|\Phi_{+,\epsilon_{2}}(\xi,\xi-\eta,\sigma)|

\displaystyle\geqslant 1-\big|-\langle\xi\rangle+\langle\xi-\eta\rangle|-|\langle\sigma\rangle-1|

\displaystyle\geqslant 1-2|\eta|-|\sigma|\gtrsim 1.

Finally, the case
({\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2}})=(-,+)
can be handled by noticing that

\displaystyle|\Phi_{-,+}(\xi,\xi-\eta,\sigma)|\geqslant\langle\xi\rangle+\langle\xi-\eta\rangle-1-|\langle\sigma\rangle-1|\gtrsim 1-|\sigma|.

Then we can proceed as in the proof of (
6.5
) in Proposition
6.2
.
More precisely, we can integrate by parts in
s
without any loss,
and reduce everything to estimating the same operators (
6.3
) and (
6.4
)
treated before, but with
\mu^{R}
instead of
\mu^{S}
.
Since for the operators associated to the regular measure
\nu_{1}^{R}

we have similar (in fact, better) bilinear bounds than for those associated to
\nu_{1}^{S}

(compare (
5.40b
)-(
5.40d
) with (
5.40c
)),
the proof carries out verbatim as in Subsection
6
;
see the proof of the estimate (
6.16
) in Lemma
6.6

and of the estimate (
6.41
) in Lemma
6.9
.

From now on we may assume
k_{1}\wedge k_{2}>-10
.
Our next goal is to try to integrate by parts in
\sigma
, but in order to do this,
we first need to deal with very small
k_{2}
. Let us fix a small
\delta>0
.

Case 2:
k_{2}\leqslant-m/2+\delta m
.
Notice that, in view of Case 1, we may assume
|\eta|\gtrsim 1
.
In particular, from (
5.19
) we
have that
\nu_{1}^{R}(\eta,\sigma)
is bounded by
2^{10M_{0}}
.
For
\ell>\ell_{0}
we integrate by parts in
\eta
, using that
\partial_{\eta}\langle\xi-\eta\rangle\approx 1

on the support of the integral (
7.39
).
We then obtain a main contribution when
\partial_{\eta}
hits
\widetilde{g}
, which is bounded by

\displaystyle\begin{split}\int_{0}^{t}\tau_{m}(s)\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{6}}|\nabla_{\eta}\widetilde{g_{\epsilon_{1}}}(s,\xi-\eta)|\,|\widetilde{h_{\epsilon_{2}}}(s,\sigma)|\varphi_{\underline{k}}(\xi,\eta,\sigma)\,b_{1}(\xi,\eta,\sigma)\,|\nu_{1}^{R}(\eta,\sigma)|\,d\eta d\sigma\,ds.\end{split}

(7.43)

The other terms where
\partial_{\eta}
hits
\nu_{1}^{R}
or the cutoff (in which case we can
repeat the integration by parts) are better. We then estimate

\displaystyle\begin{split}{\|\eqref{ghnupr5}\|}_{L^{2}}\lesssim 2^{m}2^{3k/2}\sup_{s\approx 2^{m}}{\|\varphi_{\ell}\nabla_{\eta}\widetilde{g_{\epsilon_{1}}}(s)\|}_{L^{1}}\cdot{\|\varphi_{k_{2}}\widetilde{h_{\epsilon_{2}}}(s)\|}_{L^{1}}\cdot 2^{10M_{0}}\\
\lesssim 2^{m}2^{12M_{0}}\cdot 2^{m}\rho(2^{m})m\cdot 2^{5k_{2}/2}Z,\end{split}

(7.44)

having used (
4.27
), Hölder, and the a priori assumptions.
Since
k_{2}\leqslant-m/2+\delta m
the above suffices.

When
\ell=\ell_{0}
we do not need to integrate by parts in
\eta
, but can directly
do the integration in (
7.39
)-(
7.40
), using (
4.24
),
and obtain the same bound (provided
\delta\leqslant\delta_{N}/2
):

\displaystyle\begin{split}{\|T_{\underline{k}}\|}_{L^{2}}\lesssim 2^{2m}2^{3k/2}\sup_{s\approx 2^{m}}{\|\varphi_{\leqslant\ell_{0}}\widetilde{g_{\epsilon_{1}}}(s)\|}_{L^{1}}\cdot{\|\varphi_{k_{2}}\widetilde{h_{\epsilon_{2}}}(s)\|}_{L^{1}}\cdot 2^{10M_{0}}\\
\lesssim 2^{12M_{0}}\cdot 2^{m}\rho(2^{m})m\cdot 2^{5k_{2}/2}Z.\end{split}

(7.45)

In what follows we may assume
k_{2}\geqslant-m/2+\delta m
,
and will always have the option of integrating by parts in
\sigma
.
We then distinguish two more cases depending on whether
k_{2}
is relatively close to zero or not.

Case 3:
k_{2}\geqslant-m/10
.
In this case
k_{2}
is not small so that we can efficiently use integration by parts in
\sigma
, then Strichartz estimates,
our usual bilinear estimate, and exploit the decay of
e^{itL}g
to conclude.
More precisely, integrating by parts in (
7.39
) gives a main term of the form

\displaystyle\begin{split}\int_{0}^{t}\tau_{m}(s)\Big[\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{6}}e^{-is\Phi_{\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2}}(\xi,\xi-\eta,\sigma)}\widetilde{g_{\epsilon_{1}}}(s,\xi-\eta)\,\frac{\sigma\langle\sigma\rangle}{|\sigma|^{2}}\cdot\nabla_{\sigma}\widetilde{h_{\epsilon_{2}}}(s,\sigma)\,\\
\times\varphi_{\underline{k}}(\xi,\eta,\sigma)\,b_{1}(\xi,\eta,\sigma)\,\nu_{1}^{R}(\eta,\sigma)\,d\eta d\sigma\Big]\,ds.\end{split}

(7.46)

For the other terms where the derivatives hit the cutoff
\varphi_{k_{2}}
or,
equivalently, the factor
1/|\sigma|
we have a net gain and can repeat the integration by parts;
if the derivatives hit
\nu_{1}^{R}
we have a similar or better contribution, in view of (
5.19
).
We then write

\displaystyle\begin{split}&{\|\eqref{ghnupr6}\|}_{L^{2}}\\
&\lesssim{\Big\|\int_{0}^{t}\tau_{m}(s)e^{-isL}P_{k}T_{1}^{R,1}[b_{1}]\Big(e^{is\epsilon_{1}\langle\xi\rangle}\varphi_{\ell}^{[\ell_{0},0]}(\langle\xi\rangle-2\lambda)\widetilde{g_{{\epsilon}_{1}}},e^{is\epsilon_{2}\langle\xi\rangle}\varphi_{k_{2}}(\xi)\big(\frac{\xi\langle\xi\rangle}{|\xi|^{2}}\cdot\nabla\big)\widetilde{h_{{\epsilon}_{2}}}\Big)ds\Big\|}_{L^{2}_{x}}\end{split}

(7.47)

and estimate this using the Strichartz estimate (Lemma
2.6
) with parameters
(q,r,\gamma)=(6,3,2/3)
,
i.e., landing in
L^{6/5}_{s}\langle D\rangle^{-2/3}L^{3/2}_{x}
,
and the bilinear estimate (
5.40c
):

\displaystyle\begin{split}{\|\eqref{ghnupr6}\|}_{L^{2}}&\lesssim 2^{2k/3}{\Big\|P_{k}T_{1}^{R,1}[b_{1}]\Big(e^{is\epsilon_{1}\langle\xi\rangle}\varphi_{\ell}^{[\ell_{0},0]}(\langle\xi\rangle-2\lambda)\widetilde{g},e^{is\epsilon_{2}\langle\xi\rangle}\varphi_{k_{2}}(\xi)\big(\frac{\xi\langle\xi\rangle}{|\xi|^{2}}\cdot\nabla\big)\widetilde{h}\Big)\Big\|}_{L^{6/5}_{s\approx 2^{m}}L^{3/2}_{x}}\\
&\lesssim 2^{(C_{0}+1)M_{0}}\cdot\|e^{isL}g\|_{L^{6/5}_{s\approx 2^{m}}L^{6-}_{x}}\cdot 2^{-k_{2}}\|\nabla\widetilde{h}\|_{L^{\infty}_{s}L^{2}_{x}}\\
&\lesssim 2^{(C_{0}+1)M_{0}}\cdot\big(\rho^{6/5}(2^{m})\,2^{(0+)m}\,2^{m}\big)^{5/6}\cdot 2^{-k_{2}}\cdot Z\end{split}

(7.48)

having used (
4.18
).
This gives the desired estimate since
k_{2}\geqslant-m/10
.

Case 4:
-m/2+\delta m<k_{2}<-m/10
.
In this case,
k_{2}
is relatively small, but we can still
integrate by parts in the frequencies and, in addition, exploit the fact
that we must have
|\eta|\gtrsim 1
so that the measure
\nu_{1}^{R}
does not create any harm.

We skip the case
\ell=\ell_{0}
as it is similar to what we will do below,
and the estimates can be done without integrating by parts in
\eta
.
For
\ell>\ell_{0}
we integrate by parts in
\sigma
in the formula (
7.39
)
obtaining a term as in (
7.46
), and then also integrate by parts in
\eta
,
as done before in order to arrive at (
7.43
).
We then obtain the main contribution when both derivatives hit the profiles, and this is bounded by

\displaystyle\begin{split}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{6}}s\,\tau_{m}(s)\frac{1}{s}|\nabla_{\eta}\widetilde{g_{\epsilon_{1}}}(s,\xi-\eta)|\,\frac{1}{s}\frac{1}{|\sigma|}|\nabla_{\sigma}\widetilde{h_{\epsilon_{2}}}(s,\sigma)|\,\,\varphi_{\underline{k}}(\xi,\eta,\sigma)\,|\nu_{1}^{R}(\eta,\sigma)|\,d\eta d\sigma\,ds.\end{split}

(7.49)

Using Hölder’s inequality,
the pointwise bounds on the measure (
5.19
), (
4.27
) and (
4.13
),
we can estimate

\displaystyle\begin{split}{\|\eqref{ghnupr8}\|}_{L^{2}}&\lesssim 2^{3k^{+}/2}\cdot 2^{-k_{2}}\|\varphi_{k_{2}}\nabla_{\sigma}\widetilde{h_{{\epsilon}_{2}}}\|_{L^{1}_{\sigma}}\cdot\|\varphi_{\ell}\nabla_{\eta}\widetilde{g_{{\epsilon}_{1}}}\|_{L^{1}_{\eta}}\\
&\lesssim 2^{3M_{0}/2}\cdot 2^{k_{2}/2}Z\cdot\rho(2^{m})2^{m}m,\end{split}

(7.50)

which suffices since
k_{2}<-m/10
.

Estimate of the
T_{1}^{R,1}[b_{1}^{\prime}]
contribution
.
The estimates in this case are similar to the previous ones, but not symmetric;
in fact they are easier since the second input is
g
which is localized around frequencies of size
\approx 1
,
and therefore the measure
\nu_{1}^{R}
is always bounded by
2^{10M_{0}}
.
We provide some details for the convenience of the reader.

Similarly to (
7.39
)-(
7.40
) it suffices to look at the operator

\displaystyle\begin{split}T_{\underline{k}^{\prime}}^{\prime}(t,\xi):=\int_{0}^{t}s\,\tau_{m}(s)\Big[\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{6}}e^{-is\Phi_{{\epsilon}_{2},{\epsilon}_{1}}(\xi,\xi-\eta,\sigma)}\widetilde{h_{\epsilon_{2}}}(s,\xi-\eta)\widetilde{g_{\epsilon_{1}}}(s,\sigma)\,\\
\times\psi_{\underline{k}^{\prime}}(\xi,\eta,\sigma)\,b_{1}^{\prime}(\xi,\eta,\sigma)\,\nu_{1}^{R}(\eta,\sigma)\,d\eta d\sigma\Big]\,ds,\end{split}

(7.51)

where
\underline{k}^{\prime}=(k,k_{1},k_{3},\ell)
and

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\psi_{\underline{k}^{\prime}}(\xi,\eta,\sigma):=\varphi_{\sim k_{1}}(\eta)\varphi_{k_{3}}(\xi-\eta)\varphi_{k}(\xi)\varphi_{\ell}^{(\ell_{0})}(\langle\sigma\rangle-2\lambda),\qquad\ell_{0}:=\lfloor-m+\delta m\rfloor,\\
&b_{1}^{\prime}(\xi,\eta,\sigma)=\frac{\xi}{\langle\xi\rangle}b^{\prime}(\xi,\xi-\sigma,\eta)\varphi_{k_{1}}(\eta)\varphi_{k_{2}}(\sigma).\end{split}

(7.52)

As before, we may easily reduce to at most
O(m^{4})
terms in the sum over the indexes,
and do the estimates for a fixed
4
-tuple.
We fix
\delta>0
small, and look at three main cases depending on the size of
k_{3}
.

Case 1b:
k_{3}\leqslant-m/2+\delta m
.
In this case we cannot integrate by parts in
\eta
, but we integrate by parts in
\sigma
and
can proceed identically to Case 2 above, and obtain the same bounds as in (
7.44
) and (
7.45
)
with
k_{3}
instead of
k_{2}
; this suffices.

Case 2b:
-m/2+\delta m<k_{3}<-m/10
.
This case is analogous to Case 4 above: we integrate by parts in
\sigma
and in
\eta
,
obtain a main term similar to (
7.49
) with the roles of
g
and
h
exchanged,
a factor of
1/|\xi-\eta|
instead of
1/|\sigma|
, and
\psi_{\underline{k}^{\prime}}
instead of
\varphi_{\underline{k}}
.
We can then use Hölder’s inequality to obtain the same bound as in (
7.50
)
with
k_{3}
instead of
k_{2}
, which suffices.

Case 3b:
k_{3}\geqslant-m/10
.
This case can be treated similarly to the previous Case 3. We integrate by parts only in
\eta
and
obtain a main contribution similar to (
7.46
), which, similarly to (
7.47
),
is bounded in
L^{2}
by

\displaystyle\begin{split}C{\Big\|\int_{0}^{t}\tau_{m}(s)e^{-isL}P_{k}T_{1}^{R,1}[b_{1}^{\prime}]\Big(e^{is\epsilon_{2}\langle\xi\rangle}\varphi_{k_{3}}(\xi)\big(\frac{\xi\langle\xi\rangle}{|\xi|^{2}}\cdot\nabla\big)\widetilde{h_{{\epsilon}_{2}}},e^{is\epsilon_{1}\langle\xi\rangle}\varphi_{\ell}^{(\ell_{0})}(\langle\xi\rangle-2\lambda)\widetilde{g_{{\epsilon}_{1}}}\Big)ds\Big\|}_{L^{2}_{x}}.\end{split}

(7.53)

We can then estimate using the Strichartz estimate exactly as in (
7.48
),
and obtain the same bound with
k_{3}
instead of
k_{2}
.
Since
k_{3}\geqslant-m/10
this is enough to obtain
a bound by the right-hand side of (
7.15
), as desired.

Note that the estimates obtained so far also take care of the
T_{1}^{R,1}
operators
when the roles of
h
and
g
are reversed.
To conclude the proof of the lemma we then need to estimate the
T_{1}^{R,2}
contribution
in the last line of (
7.38
).

Estimate of the
T_{1}^{R,2}
contribution
.
The estimates for this term are similar but not completely analogous to the previous ones,
so we provide the details.
With our notation from (
7.6
) and (
7.10
) we look at

T_{1}^{R,2}[b_{2}](e^{\epsilon_{1}is\langle\xi\rangle}\widetilde{g_{\epsilon_{1}}},e^{\epsilon_{2}is\langle\xi\rangle}\widetilde{h_{\epsilon_{2}}})

and begin by defining a suitable localized version of this operator,
as done similarly before in (
7.39
) and (
7.51
).

For
\underline{k}^{\prime\prime}:=(k,k_{1},k_{2},\ell)
let

\displaystyle\begin{split}T^{\prime\prime}_{\underline{k}^{\prime\prime}}(t,\xi):=\int_{0}^{t}s\,\tau_{m}(s)\Big[\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{6}}e^{-is\Phi_{\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2}}(\xi,-\eta-\sigma,\sigma)}\widetilde{g_{\epsilon_{1}}}(s,-\eta-\sigma)\widetilde{h_{\epsilon_{2}}}(s,\sigma)\,\\
\times\rho_{\underline{k}^{\prime\prime}}(\xi,\eta,\sigma)\,b_{2}(\xi,\eta,\sigma)\,\overline{\nu_{1}^{R}(\eta,\xi)}\,d\eta d\sigma\Big]\,ds,\end{split}

(7.54)

where
\Phi_{\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2}}(\xi,-\eta-\sigma,\sigma)=-\langle\xi\rangle+{\epsilon}_{1}\langle-\eta-\sigma\rangle+{\epsilon}_{2}\langle\sigma\rangle
,

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\rho_{\underline{k}^{\prime\prime}}(\xi,\eta,\sigma):=\varphi_{\sim k_{1}}(\eta)\varphi_{\sim k_{2}}(\sigma)\varphi_{k}(\xi)\varphi_{\ell}^{(\ell_{0})}(\langle\eta+\sigma\rangle-2\lambda),\qquad\ell_{0}:=\lfloor-m+\delta m\rfloor,\\
&b_{2}(\xi,\eta,\sigma)=\frac{\xi}{\langle\xi\rangle}b^{\prime}(\xi,-\eta-\sigma,\sigma)\varphi_{k_{1}}(\eta)\varphi_{k_{2}}(\sigma),\end{split}

(7.55)

with
b^{\prime}
as in (
7.10
).
As before, we can reduce to obtaining a slightly better bound than the claimed one for fixed
\underline{k}^{\prime\prime}
.
Also, we may assume that
\ell>\ell_{0}
, since the estimates for
\ell=\ell_{0}

can be done without integrating by parts in
\eta
.

Notice that in contrast with the previous estimates, in what follows we rely on the

\xi/\langle\xi\rangle
factor in the symbol
b_{2}
in order to cancel
the mild singularity from the measure
\nu_{1}^{R}(\eta,\xi)
of the form
(|\eta|\vee|\xi|)^{-2}
,
see (
5.19
).

Case 1c:
k_{2}\leqslant-m/2+\delta m
.
This case is analogous to Case 2 and Case 1b above.
For
\ell>\ell_{0}
we integrate by parts in
\eta
in (
7.54
)
obtaining a contribution bounded pointwise, similarly to (
7.43
), by

\displaystyle\begin{split}\int_{0}^{t}\tau_{m}(s)\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{6}}|\nabla_{\eta}\widetilde{g_{\epsilon_{1}}}(s,-\eta-\sigma)|\,|\widetilde{h_{\epsilon_{2}}}(s,\sigma)|\rho_{\underline{k}^{\prime\prime}}(\xi,\eta,\sigma)\,b_{2}(\xi,\eta,\sigma)\,|\nu_{1}^{R}(\eta,\xi)|\,d\eta d\sigma\,ds.\end{split}

(7.56)

Taking the
L^{2}
norm, using Hölder, the bound
|b_{2}||\nu_{1}^{R}|\lesssim 2^{k}\cdot 2^{-2(k\vee k_{1})}2^{10M_{0}}
,
and the usual a priori bounds (
4.27
) and (
4.13
), we get, similarly to (
7.44
),

\displaystyle\begin{split}{\|\eqref{ghnupr5''}\|}_{L^{2}}\lesssim 2^{m}2^{3k/2}\sup_{s\approx 2^{m}}{\|\varphi_{\ell}\nabla_{\eta}\widetilde{g_{\epsilon_{1}}}(s)\|}_{L^{1}}\cdot{\|\varphi_{k_{2}}\widetilde{h_{\epsilon_{2}}}(s)\|}_{L^{1}}\cdot 2^{k}2^{-2(k\vee k_{1})}2^{10M_{0}}\\
\lesssim 2^{m}2^{12M_{0}}\cdot 2^{m}\rho(2^{m})m\cdot 2^{5k_{2}/2}Z,\end{split}

(7.57)

which suffices.

Case 2c:
-m/2+\delta m<k_{2}<-m/50
.
In this case we can integrate by parts in
\sigma-\eta
as well, using that

e^{-is\Phi_{\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2}}(\xi,-\eta-\sigma,\sigma)}=(-is{\epsilon}_{2}\sigma/\langle\sigma\rangle)^{-1}(\partial_{\sigma}-\partial_{\eta})e^{-is\Phi_{\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2}}(\xi,-\eta-\sigma,\sigma)}
.
We can then proceed as in Case 4 above, see (
7.49
) and (
7.50
).
More precisely, taking also advantage of the extra
2^{k}
factor, we get a bound by

\displaystyle C2^{3k/2}\cdot{\|\varphi_{\ell}\nabla_{\eta}\widetilde{g_{{\epsilon}_{1}}}\|}_{L^{1}_{\eta}}\cdot 2^{-k_{2}}{\|\varphi_{k_{2}}\nabla_{\sigma}\widetilde{h_{{\epsilon}_{2}}}\|}_{L^{1}_{\sigma}}\cdot 2^{k}2^{-2(k\vee k_{1})}2^{10M_{0}}

\displaystyle\lesssim 2^{11M_{0}}\cdot 2^{k_{2}/2}Z\cdot\rho(2^{m})2^{m}m,

which is stronger than the right-hand side of (
7.15
).

Case 3c:
k_{2}\geqslant-m/50
.
This case is similar to Cases 3 and 3b above. We integrate by parts only in
\sigma
,
obtaining the main contribution (compare with (
7.47
) and (
7.53
))

\displaystyle\begin{split}{\Big\|\int_{0}^{t}\tau_{m}(s)e^{-isL}P_{k}T_{1}^{R,2}[b_{2}]\Big(e^{is\epsilon_{1}\langle\xi\rangle}\varphi_{\ell}(\langle\xi\rangle-2\lambda)\widetilde{g_{{\epsilon}_{1}}},e^{is\epsilon_{2}\langle\xi\rangle}\varphi_{k_{2}}(\xi)\big(\frac{\xi\langle\xi\rangle}{|\xi|^{2}}\cdot\nabla\big)\widetilde{h_{{\epsilon}_{2}}}\Big)ds\Big\|}_{L^{2}_{x}},\end{split}

which, using Lemma
2.6
with
(q,r,\gamma)=(6,3,2/3)
,
the bilinear estimate (
5.40c
), (
4.18
) and (
4.13
),
can be estimated (exactly as in (
7.48
)) by

\displaystyle\begin{split}2^{(C_{0}+1)M_{0}}\cdot\big(\rho^{6/5}(2^{m})\,2^{(0+)m}\,2^{m}\big)^{5/6}\cdot 2^{-k_{2}}\cdot Z\lesssim 2^{m(-1/6+(C_{0}+2)\delta_{N}+\beta)}\cdot 2^{-k_{2}}\cdot\rho(2^{m})2^{m}\cdot{\varepsilon}^{\beta}.\end{split}

This suffices for (
7.15
) provided
\delta_{N}
and
\beta
are small enough.

The same arguments above can be used to treat

T_{1}^{R,2}[b_{2}](e^{\epsilon_{1}is\langle\xi\rangle}\widetilde{h_{\epsilon_{1}}},e^{\epsilon_{2}is\langle\xi\rangle}\widetilde{g_{\epsilon_{2}}})
,
that is, the case when the roles of
g
and
h
are reversed,
by inserting a cutoff in the size of the frequency of
h
,
letting
|\eta+\sigma|\approx 2^{k_{3}}
, and distinguishing the same three case depending on
the size of
k_{3}
.

The estimates for the terms
E_{1}(G,H):=E_{1,k_{1},k_{2},m,{\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2}}}(G,H)
,
G,H\in\{(h,g),(g,h)\}

are easier.
In fact, the operator associated to the measure
\mu^{Re}

satisfies the same bilinear bounds used above (see (
5.40c
) and (
5.40e
)),
and the measure
\mu^{Re}
satisfies better pointwise bounds
than the measures
\nu_{1}^{R}
that appeared above,
as we can see by comparing (
5.19
) and (
5.24
),
and noticing that, since the input
g
is localized at frequency of size
\approx 1
,
on the support of
E_{1}
we have
(see the notation (
7.11
))
\max\{|\xi|,|\eta|,|\sigma|\}\gtrsim 1
.
We therefore obtain the same bounds for the
E_{1}
terms and the proof of the lemma is complete.
∎

7.4.
Continuous interactions

To complete the proof of Proposition
7.1
we are left with showing the following:

Lemma 7.8
.

Under the assumptions of Proposition
7.1
, the bound (
7.15
) holds
(G,H)=(h,h).

Proof.

We will use similar notation and arguments to those in the proof of Lemma
7.7
.
We concentrate only on the
D_{1}
terms as the
E_{1}
terms can be treated in the same way
(using (
5.40e
) instead of (
5.40c
), and (
5.24
) instead of (
5.19
)).
Recall the notation (
5.34b
)-(
5.34c
) and the identity (
5.37
);
using also the symmetry of the inputs, we can write (compare with (
7.38
)),

\displaystyle{\big\|\widehat{\mathcal{F}}^{-1}_{\xi\mapsto x}D_{1}(t,\xi)\big\|}_{L^{2}}\lesssim\sum_{k_{1},k_{2}\leqslant M_{0}}{\|I^{(1)}(t)\|}_{L^{2}}+{\|I^{(2)}(t)\|}_{L^{2}},

\displaystyle I^{(j)}(t,\xi):=\int_{0}^{t}s\,\tau_{m}(s)e^{-isL}T_{1}^{R,j}[b_{j}]\big(e^{i\epsilon_{1}s\langle\xi\rangle}\widetilde{h_{\epsilon_{1}}},e^{i\epsilon_{2}s\langle\xi\rangle}\widetilde{h_{\epsilon_{2}}}\big)\,ds.

As before, the symbols
b_{1},b_{2}
(that depend on
k_{1},k_{2}
)
are defined as in (
6.21
) and (
7.10
), and we suppressed the dependence on

m,{\epsilon}_{1},{\epsilon}_{2}
for lighter notation.
We proceed in parallel to the proof of Lemma
7.7
by looking first at
I^{(1)}
and then at
I^{(2)}
.

Estimate of
I^{(1)}
.
Similarly to (
7.38
), we define a localized version of
I^{(1)}
as follows:
for
\underline{k}:=(k,k_{1},k_{2},k_{3})
, let

\displaystyle\begin{split}I_{\underline{k}}(t,\xi):=\int_{0}^{t}s\,\tau_{m}(s)\Big[\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{6}}e^{-is\Phi_{\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2}}(\xi,\xi-\eta,\sigma)}\widetilde{h_{\epsilon_{1}}}(s,\xi-\eta)\widetilde{h_{\epsilon_{2}}}(s,\sigma)\,\\
\times\varphi_{\underline{k}}(\xi,\eta,\sigma)\,b_{1}(\xi,\eta,\sigma)\,\nu_{1}^{R}(\eta,\sigma)\,d\eta d\sigma\Big]\,ds,\\
\varphi_{\underline{k}}(\xi,\eta,\sigma):=\varphi_{\sim k_{1}}(\eta)\varphi_{\sim k_{2}}(\sigma)\varphi_{k_{3}}(\xi-\eta)\varphi_{k}(\xi),\end{split}

(7.58)

where
\Phi_{\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2}}(\xi,\xi-\eta,\sigma)=-\langle\xi\rangle+{\epsilon}_{1}\langle\xi-\eta\rangle+{\epsilon}_{2}\langle\sigma\rangle
,
and
b_{1}(\xi,\eta,\sigma)=\frac{\xi}{\langle\xi\rangle}b^{\prime}(\xi,\xi-\eta,\sigma)\varphi_{k_{1}}(\eta)\varphi_{k_{2}}(\sigma)
as before.
The variables in (
7.39
) are localized as follows:

\displaystyle\begin{split}&|\xi|\approx 2^{k},\qquad|\eta|\approx 2^{k_{1}},\qquad|\sigma|\approx 2^{k_{2}},\qquad|\xi-\eta|\approx 2^{k_{3}},\\
&\mbox{with}\quad-5m<k,k_{1},k_{2},k_{3}\leqslant M_{0}.\end{split}

(7.59)

The lower bound on
k_{1}
is not a consequence of our earlier reductions (see Lemma
5.5
),
but can be assumed by treating the case of
k_{1}<-5m
just using Hölder and a straightforward
integration in (
7.58
).

We proceed to estimate
{\|I_{\underline{k}}\|}_{L^{2}}
for fixed
\underline{k}

showing that a bound by the right-hand side of (
7.15
) times a factor of
2^{-(0+)m}
holds true.
We look at three main cases.

Case 1:
k_{1},k_{2}\leqslant-10
. As in Case 1 in the proof of Lemma
7.7
, we observe that if
|\eta|,|\sigma|\leqslant 2^{-8}
the phase is uniformly lower bounded,
and can then proceed by integration by parts in
s
.
In what follows we may assume
k_{1}\vee k_{2}>-10
so that, in particular, see (
5.19
),

\displaystyle|\partial_{\eta}^{a}\partial_{\sigma}^{b}\nu_{1}^{R}(\eta,\sigma)|\lesssim 2^{(|a|+|b|+2)5M_{0}}\max\{1,2^{(1-|b|)k_{2}^{-}}\}.

(7.60)

We fix
\delta>0
small and distinguish cases depending on the sizes of the input frequencies.

Case 2:
k_{2}\vee k_{3}\leqslant-m/2+\delta m
.
In this case, we cannot integrate by parts in either
\sigma
or
\eta
.
However, we have
|\nu_{1}^{R}(\eta,\sigma)|\lesssim 2^{10M_{0}}
(since
|\eta|\gtrsim 1
)
and direct Hölder estimates give us

\displaystyle\begin{split}{\|I_{\underline{k}}(t)\|}_{L^{2}}\lesssim 2^{2m}\cdot 2^{3k^{+}/2}2^{k^{-}}\sup_{s\approx 2^{m}}{\|\varphi_{k_{3}}\widetilde{h_{\epsilon_{1}}}(s)\|}_{L^{1}}\cdot{\|\varphi_{k_{2}}\widetilde{h_{\epsilon_{2}}}(s)\|}_{L^{1}}\cdot 2^{10M_{0}}\\
\lesssim 2^{2m}2^{12M_{0}}\cdot 2^{5k_{3}/2}Z\cdot 2^{5k_{2}/2}Z\end{split}

(7.61)

which largely suffices in the current scenario.

Case 3:
k_{2}\wedge k_{3}\leqslant-m/2+\delta m
and
k_{2}\vee k_{3}\geqslant-m/2+\delta m
.
In this case we can integrate by parts at least in one of the variables
\sigma
or
\eta
.
Let us assume without loss of generality that
k_{3}\leqslant-m/2+\delta m\leqslant k_{2}
.
We then integrate by parts in
\sigma
in the formula (
7.58
); the leading order contribution
is the one where
\partial_{\sigma}
hits the profile, which is estimated using Hölder,
followed by (
4.13
), by

\displaystyle\begin{split}C2^{2m}\cdot 2^{3k/2}\sup_{s\approx 2^{m}}{\|\varphi_{k_{3}}\widetilde{h_{\epsilon_{1}}}(s)\|}_{L^{1}}\cdot 2^{-m-k_{2}}{\|\varphi_{k_{2}}\nabla_{\sigma}\widetilde{h_{\epsilon_{2}}}(s)\|}_{L^{1}}\cdot 2^{10M_{0}}\\
\lesssim 2^{m}2^{12M_{0}}\cdot 2^{5k_{3}/2}Z\cdot 2^{k_{2}/2}Z.\end{split}

(7.62)

Since
k_{3}\leqslant-m/2+\delta m
this bound suffices.

In what follows we may assume
k_{2},k_{3}\geqslant-m/2+\delta m
,
so that we can integrate by parts in both
\eta
and
\sigma
.

Case 4:
k_{2}\wedge k_{3}\geqslant-m/2+\delta m
.
We look at two sub-cases:

Sub-case 4.1:
k_{2}\wedge k_{3}\geqslant-m/10
.
We integrate by parts in
\eta
and
\sigma
in the formula (
7.58
),
and obtain the following leading order contribution when both derivatives hit the profiles
(the terms where the cutoffs or the measure are hit all have better estimates):

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}s^{-1}\,\tau_{m}(s)e^{isL}T_{1}^{R,1}[b_{1}]\Big(e^{i\epsilon_{1}s\langle\xi\rangle}\varphi_{k_{3}}\big(\frac{\langle\xi\rangle\xi}{|\xi|^{2}}\cdot\nabla\big)\widetilde{h_{\epsilon_{1}}},e^{i\epsilon_{2}s\langle\xi\rangle}\varphi_{k_{2}}\big(\frac{\langle\xi\rangle\xi}{|\xi|^{2}}\cdot\nabla\big)\widetilde{h_{\epsilon_{2}}}\Big)ds.

(7.63)

We then use Strichartz estimates with parameters
(2+,\infty-,0+)
,
followed by an
L^{2}\times L^{2}\rightarrow L^{1+}
bilinear estimate (
5.40c
) and (
4.13
),
to bound

\displaystyle\begin{split}&{\|\eqref{hhnuR5}\|}_{L^{2}}\\
&\lesssim 2^{(0+)M_{0}}{\Big\|s^{-1}\cdot T_{1}^{R,1}[b_{1}]\Big(e^{i\epsilon_{1}s\langle\xi\rangle}\varphi_{k_{3}}\big(\frac{\langle\xi\rangle\xi}{|\xi|^{2}}\cdot\nabla\big)\widetilde{h_{\epsilon_{1}}},e^{i\epsilon_{2}s\langle\xi\rangle}\varphi_{k_{2}}\big(\frac{\langle\xi\rangle\xi}{|\xi|^{2}}\cdot\nabla\big)\widetilde{h_{\epsilon_{2}}}\Big)\Big\|}_{L^{2-}_{s\approx 2^{m}}L^{1+}_{x}}\\
&\lesssim 2^{(C_{0}+1)M_{0}}\cdot 2^{-(m/2)+}\cdot 2^{-k_{2}}Z\cdot 2^{-k_{3}}Z.\end{split}

(7.64)

Using
-k_{2}-k_{3}\leqslant-m/5,
this gives a sufficient bound.

Sub-case 4.2:
k_{2}\wedge k_{3}<-m/10
. In this case we can use Hölder and (
7.60
) to write,
for
s\approx 2^{m}
,

\displaystyle\begin{split}{\|\eqref{hhnuR5}\|}_{L^{2}}&\lesssim 2^{3k/2}\cdot 2^{10M_{0}}\cdot 2^{-k_{2}-k_{3}}{\|\varphi_{k_{3}}\nabla_{\eta}\widetilde{h}\|}_{L^{1}}{\|\varphi_{k_{2}}\nabla_{\sigma}\widetilde{h}\|}_{L^{1}}\\
&\lesssim 2^{12M_{0}}\cdot 2^{(k_{2}+k_{3})/2}Z^{2}.\end{split}

(7.65)

This is enough to conclude.

Estimate of
I^{(2)}
.
For this second and last term we can use similar arguments.
Recalling the notation (
5.34c
) with (
6.21
) and (
7.10
), and arguing as before,
we reduce to estimating the
L^{2}
norm of the localized operator

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}s\,\tau_{m}(s)e^{-isL}P_{k}T_{1}^{R,2}[b_{2}]\big(e^{i\epsilon_{1}s\langle\xi\rangle}\varphi_{k_{3}}\widetilde{h_{\epsilon_{1}}},e^{i\epsilon_{2}s\langle\xi\rangle}\varphi_{\sim k_{2}}\widetilde{h_{\epsilon_{2}}}\big)\,ds,

(7.66)

for
m=1,2,\dots
and fixed
k,k_{1},k_{2},k_{3}\in[-5m,M_{0}]
.

We can proceed in parallel to the Cases 1-4 from the previous part of the proof of this lemma.
We first observe that if
k\wedge k_{1}\leqslant-10
(which means
|\xi|,|\eta|\leqslant 2^{-5}

with the variables as in (
5.34c
)) then the phase of the operator in Fourier space is uniformly lower bounded:

|-\langle\xi\rangle+{\epsilon}_{1}\langle\eta+\sigma\rangle+{\epsilon}_{2}\langle\sigma\rangle|\gtrsim 1
.
Sufficient bounds can then be obtained by integration by parts in
s
;
see also Case 1 on page
7.3
.
In the complementary case when
k\vee k_{1}\leqslant-10
the measure
\nu_{1}^{R}(\eta,\xi)

is uniformly bounded (up to innocuous
2^{M_{0}}
factors) with all its derivatives on the support of (
7.66
).
We then argue as follows:

- For
k_{2}\vee k_{3}\leqslant-m/2+\delta m
, for some small enough
\delta>0
,
we estimate as in Case 2 from earlier in the proof of this lemma, and obtain the same bound (
7.61
);

- For
k_{3}\leqslant-m/2+\delta m\leqslant k_{2}
we integrate by parts in
\eta-\sigma
as in Case 3
from earlier in this proof, and estimate exactly as in (
7.62
).
The case
k_{2}\leqslant-m/2+\delta m\leqslant k_{3}
can be handled in the same way integrating by parts in
\eta
;

- For
k_{3}\wedge k_{2}\geqslant-m/2+\delta m
we integrate by parts both in

\eta-\sigma
and
\sigma
as in Case 4, obtaining a term like (
7.64
).
We then estimate as in (
7.65
) when
k_{3}\wedge k_{2}\leqslant-m/10
,
while for
k_{3}\wedge k_{2}\geqslant-m/10
we use the same Strichartz estimate,
with the bilinear bound (
5.40c
), to obtain the same final upper bound in (
7.64
).

The proof of the lemma and of the main Proposition
7.1
is concluded.
∎

8.
Mixed terms

In this section we deal with the mixed terms identified in (
4.8
) and (
4.9
).
We will first concentrate on the leading order terms (
4.8a
) and (
4.8b
)
which, recall, are given by

\displaystyle M_{1}(t,\xi)

\displaystyle:=-i\int_{0}^{t}B(s)\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}e^{-is(\langle\xi\rangle-\langle\eta\rangle-\lambda)}\langle\eta\rangle^{-1}\widetilde{h}(s,\eta)\nu(\xi,\eta)d\eta\,ds,

(8.1)

\displaystyle M_{2}(t,\xi)

\displaystyle:=i\int_{0}^{t}B(s)\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}e^{-is(\langle\xi\rangle-\langle\eta\rangle-\lambda)}\langle\eta\rangle^{-1}\widetilde{g}(s,\eta)\nu(\xi,\eta)d\eta\,ds.

(8.2)

We aim to show the following main proposition:

Proposition 8.1
.

Under the a priori assumptions of Proposition
4.13
we have

\displaystyle{\|\partial_{\xi}M_{1}(t)\|}_{L^{2}}+{\|\partial_{\xi}M_{2}(t)\|}_{L^{2}}\lesssim\rho(t)^{1-\beta+\delta}t\varepsilon^{\beta},

(8.3)

for some
\delta>0
.

Consistently with Remark
4.3
, the estimate (
8.3
)
suffices to obtain the desired result, as in (
4.14
), for these terms.
In Subsection
8.6
we will discuss how to handle the remainder terms in (
4.9
).

8.1.
Preliminaries

We first recall some basic facts that will be useful in the rest of the section,
and then we set up the proof of Proposition
8.1
.

Recall that we have defined

\displaystyle\nu(\xi,\eta)=\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\overline{\psi(x,\xi)}\psi(x,\eta)\phi(x)\,dx.

(8.4)

In view of the estimates

\displaystyle|\partial_{x}^{\alpha}\partial_{\xi}^{\beta}\psi(x,\xi)|\lesssim_{\alpha,\beta}(\langle\xi\rangle^{|\alpha|}+(|\xi|/\langle\xi\rangle)^{1-|\beta|})\langle x\rangle^{|\beta|},\qquad 0\leqslant|\alpha|,|\beta|\leqslant N,

(8.5)

for
V
with sufficiently many bounded Schwartz semi-norms (see Lemma 3.2 in
[
41
]
for a precise statement),
we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\big|\varphi_{k}(\xi)\varphi_{k_{1}}(\eta)\nabla_{\xi}^{a}\nabla^{b}_{\eta}\nu(\xi,\eta)\big|\lesssim 1\qquad|a|,|b|\leqslant 1,\\
&\big|\varphi_{k}(\xi)\varphi_{k_{1}}(\eta)\nabla_{\xi}^{a}\nabla_{\eta}^{b}\nu(\xi,\eta)\big|\lesssim_{a,b}2^{-(|a|-1)k^{-}}2^{-(|b|-1)k_{1}^{-}},\qquad 1\leqslant|a|,|b|\leqslant N.\end{split}

(8.6)

In particular,
\nu
does not behave worse than cutoff functions, so we will not need to worry much about
derivatives in
\eta
falling on it during our integration by parts procedures.

We also recall the following lemma that will be useful in this section:

Lemma 8.2
.

Let
F:\mathbb{R}^{3}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
be smooth in a ball

B_{R}(z)\subset\mathbb{R}^{3},z\in\mathbb{R}^{3},R>0.
Then

\displaystyle\int_{B_{R}(z)}\varphi_{\leqslant\lambda}\big(F(x)\big)\varphi_{\geqslant\mu}\big(\nabla F(x)\big)dx\leqslant 2^{-\mu}2^{\lambda}R^{2}.

Proof.

The proof is a straightforward adaptation of Lemma 6.2 part (1) in
[
40
]
.
∎

We start the analysis of
M_{1}
and
M_{2}
; differentiating and introducing
localizations in
s,\xi,\eta
, and write, for
i=1,2
(up to irrelevant constants)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\partial_{\xi}M_{i}&=\sum_{m,k,k_{1}}I_{m,k,k_{1}}(G_{i})+II_{m,k,k_{1}}(G_{i}),\qquad G_{1}:=h,\quad G_{2}:=g,\\
I_{m,k,k_{1}}(G_{i})&:=\varphi_{k}(\xi)\frac{\xi}{\langle\xi\rangle}\int_{0}^{t}sB(s)\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}e^{-is(\langle\xi\rangle-\langle\eta\rangle-\lambda)}\varphi_{k_{1}}(\eta)\widetilde{G_{i}}(s,\eta)\nu(\xi,\eta)d\eta\,\tau_{m}(s)ds,\\
II_{m,k,k_{1}}(G_{i})&:=\varphi_{k}(\xi)\int_{0}^{t}B(s)\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}e^{-is(\langle\xi\rangle-\langle\eta\rangle-\lambda)}\varphi_{k_{1}}(\eta)\widetilde{G_{i}}(s,\eta)\partial_{\xi}\nu(\xi,\eta)d\eta\,\tau_{m}(s)ds.\end{split}

(8.7)

The terms
I_{m,k,k_{1}}(g)
and
I_{m,k,k_{1}}(h)
need quite different treatments,
with the latter being more challenging. This will be carried out in Subsections
8.2
and
8.3
-
8.4
respectively.
The terms
II_{m,k,k_{1}}(g)
and
II_{m,k,k_{1}}(h)
can essentially be handled in the same way.
This will be done in Subsection
8.5
.
In the rest of the section we will drop the dependence of
I_{m,k,k_{1}}
and
II_{m,k,k_{1}}

on the profiles when there is no confusion.

8.2.
Estimate of
I_{m,k,k_{1}}(g)

We begin with
I_{m,k,k_{1}}(g)
which is easier to handle than
I_{m,k,k_{1}}(h)
.
The main result of this subsection is

Lemma 8.3
.

For
m=0,1,\dots
we have for some
\delta>0

\displaystyle\Big\|\sum_{k}I_{m,k,k_{1}}(g)\Big\|_{L^{2}_{\xi}}\lesssim\rho(2^{m})^{1-\beta+\delta}2^{m}{\varepsilon}^{\beta}.

(8.8)

Note that since
g
is localized at frequencies
\sim 1,
the sum over the index
k_{1}
is finite.

Proof.

In this case we recombine the sum on
k,
and denote
I_{m,k_{1}}:=\sum_{k}I_{m,k,k_{1}}
.
First, let us observe that if we restrict the frequency integral to
the region where
|\langle\eta\rangle-2\lambda|\leqslant 2^{-10m}
, which has approximate size
2^{-10m}
,
then (
8.8
) can be obtained easily using
|\nu|\lesssim 1
and the bound (
4.24
)
which gives
{\|\widetilde{g}(s)\|}_{L^{\infty}}\lesssim m
for
s\approx 2^{m}
.
Therefore, in the rest of the proof we may assume that
|\langle\eta\rangle-2\lambda|\geqslant 2^{-10m}
;
in particular, integrating by parts in the formula for
\widetilde{g}
we can slightly abuse notation inserting the corresponding cutoff,
and write

\displaystyle\widetilde{g}(s,\eta)

\displaystyle=i\varphi_{[-10m,-C]}(\langle\eta\rangle-2\lambda)\big(\widetilde{g}_{1}(s,\eta)+\widetilde{g}_{2}(s,\eta)\big),

(8.9)

\displaystyle\widetilde{g}_{1}(s,\eta)

\displaystyle:=\frac{e^{-is(\langle\eta\rangle-2\lambda)}}{\langle\eta\rangle-2\lambda}B^{2}(s)\widetilde{\theta}(\eta)-\frac{B^{2}(0)}{\langle\eta\rangle-2\lambda}\widetilde{\theta}(\eta),\qquad\theta:={\bf{P_{c}}}\phi^{2},

(8.10)

\displaystyle\widetilde{g}_{2}(s,\eta)

\displaystyle:=\int_{0}^{s}\dot{B}(\tau)B(\tau)e^{-i\tau(\langle\eta\rangle-2\lambda)}\,\frac{\widetilde{\theta}(\eta)}{\langle\eta\rangle-2\lambda}\,d\tau.

(8.11)

For convenience, we may omit the cutoff
\varphi_{[-10m,-C]}
.
Plugging the contribution from (
8.10
) into the integral (
8.7
) gives the terms

\displaystyle I_{m,k_{1}}^{(11)}(g):=

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}sB^{3}(s)e^{-is(\langle\xi\rangle-3\lambda)}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\frac{\widetilde{\theta}(\eta)}{\langle\eta\rangle-2\lambda}\nu(\xi,\eta)d\eta\,\tau_{m}(s)ds,

\displaystyle I_{m,k_{1}}^{(12)}(g):=

\displaystyle B(0)^{2}\int_{0}^{t}sB(s)\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}e^{-is(\langle\xi\rangle-\langle\eta\rangle-\lambda)}\frac{\widetilde{\theta}(\eta)}{\langle\eta\rangle-2\lambda}\nu(\xi,\eta)d\eta\,\tau_{m}(s)ds.

We bound the first term using Plancherel and the inhomogeneous Strichartz estimate in Lemma
2.6

with
(q,r,\gamma)=(\infty,2,0)
and
(\widetilde{q},\widetilde{r},\gamma)=(2,\infty,0)
:

\displaystyle{\|I_{m,k_{1}}^{(11)}(g)\|}_{L^{2}_{\xi}}

\displaystyle={\Big\|\int_{0}^{t}sB^{3}(s)e^{-is(L-3\lambda)}\phi\,\frac{1}{L-2\lambda}\theta\,\tau_{m}(s)ds\Big\|}_{L^{2}_{x}}

\displaystyle\lesssim{\Big\|sB^{3}(s)\phi\,\frac{1}{L-2\lambda}\theta\,\tau_{m}(s)\Big\|}_{L^{2}_{s}L^{1}_{x}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|sB^{3}(s)\|_{L^{2}_{s\approx 2^{m}}}{\Big\|\phi\frac{1}{L-2\lambda}\theta\Big\|}_{L^{1}}\lesssim\rho(2^{m})^{3/2}2^{3m/2},

having used the boundedness of the resolvent on weighted
L^{2}
spaces. Recalling Remark
6.3
, this is sufficient.

For the term
I_{m,k_{1}}^{(12)}(g)
above
we can use again Lemma
2.6
followed by the local decay estimates from Lemma
2.7

to make up for the lack of a
B^{2}(s)
factor compared to the term
I_{m,k_{1}}^{(11)}
:

\displaystyle{\|I_{m,k_{1}}^{(12)}(g)\|}_{L^{2}_{\xi}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\varepsilon_{0}\|sB(s)\|_{L^{2}_{s\approx 2^{m}}}\sup_{s\approx 2^{m}}{\Big\|\phi\,e^{isL}\,\frac{1}{L-2\lambda}\theta\Big\|}_{L^{1}_{x}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\varepsilon_{0}\|sB(s)\|_{L^{2}_{s\approx 2^{m}}}\cdot 2^{-(3/2)m}\lesssim\varepsilon_{0}\rho(2^{m})^{1/2}2^{3m/2}\cdot 2^{-(3/2)m},

which is sufficient.

Plugging the contribution from (
8.11
) into the integral in (
8.7
),
and summing over
k
as above, gives the term

\displaystyle I_{m,k_{1}}^{(2)}(g):=\int_{0}^{t}e^{-is(\langle\xi\rangle-\lambda)}sB(s)\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\widetilde{G}(s,\eta)\,\nu(\xi,\eta)d\eta\,\tau_{m}(s)ds,

\displaystyle\widetilde{G}(s,\eta):=\int_{0}^{s}e^{2i\lambda\tau}\dot{B}(\tau)B(\tau)e^{i(s-\tau)\langle\eta\rangle}\,\frac{\widetilde{\theta}(\eta)}{\langle\eta\rangle-2\lambda}\,d\tau.

Using Lemmas
2.7
and
3.5
, for
s\approx 2^{m}
, we see that

\displaystyle{\|\langle x\rangle^{-10}G(s)\|}_{L^{1}_{x}}\lesssim\int_{0}^{s}|\dot{B}(\tau)||B(\tau)|{\Big\|\langle x\rangle^{-10}e^{i(s-\tau)L}\frac{\theta}{L-2\lambda}\Big\|}_{L^{1}_{x}}\,d\tau

\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{0}^{s}\big(\rho^{2}(\tau)+\rho^{3/2-a}(\tau)\langle\tau\rangle^{-1}\big)\langle s-\tau\rangle^{-3/2}d\tau\lesssim\rho(2^{m})^{3/2-}.

Applying the same Strichartz estimate used for
II
above gives

\displaystyle{\|II_{m,k_{1}}^{(2)}(g)\|}_{L^{2}_{\xi}}\lesssim{\|sB(s)\|}_{L^{2}_{s\approx 2^{m}}}\sup_{s\approx 2^{m}}{\|\phi\,G(s)\|}_{L^{1}}\lesssim 2^{3m/2}\cdot\rho(2^{m})^{2-},

which suffices and concludes the proof of the lemma.
∎

8.3.
Estimate of
I_{m,k,k_{1}}(h)

This term is harder to treat than the previous one and we need a more refined analysis.
We estimate the terms
I_{m,k,k_{1}}
in the remainder of this subsection and the next,
and treat the simpler term
II_{m,k,k_{1}}
in Subsection
8.5
.

The main goal of this subsection is then to prove the following:

Proposition 8.4
.

Under the a priori assumptions of Proposition
4.2
,
and with the definition (
8.7
), we have, for all
m=0,1,\dots
,
