\displaystyle\sum_{k,k_{1}\in{\mathbb{Z}}}\|I_{m,k,k_{1}}(h)\|\lesssim\rho(2^{m})^{1-\beta+\delta}2^{m}\varepsilon^{\beta},

(8.12)

for some
\delta\in(0,\beta/2)
.

In view of (
8.7
), the above result is consistent with the desired bound in the main Proposition
8.1
.
Proposition
8.4
will follow from Lemmas
8.5
and
8.6
and
Proposition
8.7
.

Let us first deal with very small or large frequencies.

Lemma 8.5
.

Under the assumptions of Proposition
8.4
, we have

\displaystyle\sum_{\min(k,k_{1})<-3m}\|I_{m,k,k_{1}}(h)\|\lesssim\rho(2^{m})^{1-\beta+\delta}2^{m}\varepsilon^{\beta},

(8.13)

and

\displaystyle\sum_{\max(k,k_{1})>\beta m}\|I_{m,k,k_{1}}(h)\|\lesssim\rho(2^{m})^{1-\beta+\delta}2^{m}\varepsilon^{\beta}.

(8.14)

Proof.

Using Hölder, Sobolev’s embedding, and the a priori estimates, we have

\displaystyle{\|P_{k_{1}}h(t)\|}_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\min\big(2^{-Nk_{1}^{+}}{\|h(t)\|}_{H^{N}},\,2^{k_{1}}{\big\|\widetilde{h}(t)\big\|}_{H^{1}_{\xi}}\big)\lesssim\big(2^{-Nk_{1}^{+}}\wedge 2^{k_{1}}\big)\rho(t)^{1-\beta}t{\varepsilon}^{\beta}.

(8.15)

Then we can estimate using Plancherel and (
8.15
)

\displaystyle{\|I_{m,k,k_{1}}(h)\|}_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim 2^{k^{-}}\int_{0}^{t}s|B(s)|{\big\|P_{k}(\phi P_{k_{1}}L^{-1}e^{isL}h(s))\big\|}_{L^{2}}\tau_{m}(s)ds

(8.16)

\displaystyle\lesssim 2^{2m}\rho(2^{m})\cdot 2^{k^{-}}\sup_{s\approx 2^{m}}2^{-(N/2)k^{+}}{\big\|P_{k_{1}}L^{-1}e^{isL}h(s)\big\|}_{H^{N/2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim 2^{k^{-}}2^{-(N/2)k^{+}}\cdot\big(2^{-(N/2)k_{1}^{+}}\wedge 2^{k_{1}}\big)\cdot 2^{2m}\rho(2^{m})^{2-\beta}2^{m}{\varepsilon}^{\beta}.

This gives simultaneously (
8.13
) as well as (
8.14
), since, by our choice (
1.45
), we have
\beta N/2>1
.
∎

Note that, in view of the above lemma, there are only
O(m^{2})
terms left
to estimate in the sums in (
8.12
); therefore, to obtain Proposition
8.4

it suffices to show that for some
\delta^{\prime}>\delta

\displaystyle\begin{split}{\|I_{m,k,k_{1}}(h)\|}_{L^{2}_{\xi}}\lesssim\rho(2^{m})^{1+\delta^{\prime}-\beta}2^{m}{\varepsilon}^{\beta},\qquad-3m\leqslant k,k_{1}\leqslant\beta m.\end{split}

(8.17)

We make another reduction in the following lemma
by treating the case of relatively small
|\eta|
or
|\xi|
.

Lemma 8.6
.

Let
\min(k,k_{1})\geqslant-3m
and
\max(k,k_{1})\leqslant\beta m
,
and assume in addition that
\min(k,k_{1})\leqslant-5\beta m
. Then we have

\displaystyle{\|I_{m,k,k_{1}}(h)\|}_{L^{2}}\lesssim\rho(2^{m})2^{m}.

Proof.

We apply Plancherel and the Strichartz estimate from Lemma
2.6
to estimate

\displaystyle\begin{split}{\|I_{m,k,k_{1}}(h)\|}_{L^{2}_{\xi}}&=c{\Big\|P_{k}\frac{R|H|^{1/2}}{L}\int_{0}^{t}sB(s)e^{isL}\big(\phi\,e^{isL}L^{-1}h\big)\,\tau_{m}(s)ds\Big\|}_{L^{2}}\\
&\lesssim 2^{k^{-}}\cdot{\big\|sB(s)\big\|}_{L^{2}_{s\approx 2^{m}}}\sup_{s\approx 2^{m}}{\big\|\phi\,\big(L^{-1}e^{isL}P_{k_{1}}h\big)\big\|}_{L^{1}}\\
&\lesssim 2^{k^{-}}\cdot 2^{3m/2}\rho(2^{m})^{1/2}\cdot\sup_{s\approx 2^{m}}{\big\|\langle x\rangle^{2}\phi\,(L^{-1}e^{isL}P_{k_{1}}h)\big\|}_{L^{2}}.\end{split}

(8.18)

Here
R
is the distorted Riesz transform
\widetilde{Rf}(\xi)=(\xi/|\xi|)\widetilde{f}(\xi)

and
H=-\Delta+V
the Schrödinger operator,
\widetilde{\mathcal{F}}(|H|^{\alpha}f)(\xi)=|\xi|^{\alpha}\widetilde{f}(\xi)
.
Let us define

\displaystyle H_{k_{1}}(s,\xi)=\widetilde{\mathcal{F}}\Big(\langle x\rangle^{2}\phi\,\big(L^{-1}e^{isL}P_{k_{1}}h\big)\Big)=\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}e^{is\langle\eta\rangle}\varphi_{k_{1}}(\eta)\,\langle\eta\rangle^{-1}\widetilde{h}(s,\eta)\nu^{\prime}(\xi,\eta)d\eta,

(8.19)

where

\displaystyle\nu^{\prime}(\xi,\eta):=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\overline{\psi(x,\xi)}\psi(x,\eta)\langle x\rangle^{2}\phi(x)\,dx.

Notice that
\nu^{\prime}
satisfies the estimates (
8.6
) as well.
By (
8.18
) and Plancherel it
suffices to show that, for
s\approx 2^{m}
, we have

\displaystyle 2^{k^{-}}{\big\|H_{k_{1}}(s)\big\|}_{L^{2}_{\xi}}\lesssim 2^{-m},\quad\mbox{when}\quad\min(k,k_{1})\leqslant-5\beta m.

(8.20)

Since we can estimate, using Bernstein and (
8.15
),

\displaystyle{\|H_{k_{1}}(s)\|}_{L^{2}}\lesssim{\|e^{isL}P_{k_{1}}h\|}_{L^{\infty}}\lesssim 2^{5k_{1}/2}\cdot Z,

we immediately get (
8.20
) provided

k_{1}\leqslant-m/2+\delta m
, for
\delta
small enough (recall
2^{m}\geqslant\rho_{0}^{-1/2}
).

When instead
k_{1}\geqslant-m/2+\delta m
we can integrate by parts and write

\displaystyle H_{k_{1}}(s,\xi)

\displaystyle:=H_{k_{1}}^{(1)}(s,\xi)+H_{k_{1}}^{(2)}(s,\xi)+\,\{\mbox{similar terms}\},

\displaystyle H_{k_{1}}^{(1)}(s,\xi)

\displaystyle:=\frac{i}{s}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}e^{is\langle\eta\rangle}\frac{\eta}{|\eta|^{2}}\cdot\nabla_{\eta}\widetilde{h}(s,\eta)\,\varphi_{k_{1}}(\eta)\nu^{\prime}(\xi,\eta)d\eta,

\displaystyle H_{k_{1}}^{(2)}(s,\xi)

\displaystyle:=\frac{i}{s}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}e^{is\langle\eta\rangle}\widetilde{h}(s,\eta)\varphi_{k_{1}}(\eta)\,\frac{\eta}{|\eta|^{2}}\cdot\nabla_{\eta}\nu^{\prime}(\xi,\eta)d\eta,

where “similar terms” are those with
\nabla_{\eta}
hitting the cutoff or
\eta/|\eta|^{2}
; it will be
clear how such terms can be handled like the other ones.

Then we bound, for
s\approx 2^{m}
,

\displaystyle\begin{split}{\|H_{k_{1}}^{(1)}(s)\|}_{L^{2}}&\lesssim 2^{-m}{\big\|\widetilde{\mathcal{F}}^{-1}\big(\varphi_{k_{1}}e^{is\langle\eta\rangle}\eta/|\eta|^{-2}\cdot\nabla_{\eta}\widetilde{h}\big)\big\|}_{L^{\infty}}\lesssim 2^{-m}2^{k_{1}/2}\cdot Z.\end{split}

(8.21)

Similarly, we can handle the remaining term using Plancherel and
|\nabla_{\eta}\psi|\lesssim 1
:

\displaystyle\begin{split}{\|H_{k_{1}}^{(2)}(s)\|}_{L^{2}}&\lesssim 2^{-m}{\Big\|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\overline{\psi(x,\xi)}\langle x\rangle^{2}\phi(x)\Big[\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}e^{is\langle\eta\rangle}\widetilde{h}(s,\eta)\varphi_{k_{1}}(\eta)\,\frac{\eta}{|\eta|^{2}}\cdot\nabla_{\eta}\psi(x,\eta)d\eta\Big]\,dx\Big\|}_{L^{2}_{\xi}}\\
&\lesssim 2^{-m}{\Big\|\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}e^{is\langle\eta\rangle}\widetilde{h}(s,\eta)\varphi_{k_{1}}(\eta)\,\frac{\eta}{|\eta|^{2}}\cdot\nabla_{\eta}\psi(x,\eta)d\eta\ \Big\|}_{L^{\infty}_{x}}\\
&\lesssim 2^{-m}2^{-k_{1}}{\|\varphi_{k_{1}}\widetilde{h}\|}_{L^{1}}\lesssim 2^{-m}2^{k_{1}/2}\cdot Z.\end{split}

(8.22)

In view of (
8.21
) and (
8.22
),
and since
2^{k^{-}}2^{-m}2^{k_{1}/2}\cdot\rho(2^{m})^{1-\beta}2^{m}{\varepsilon}^{\beta}\lesssim 2^{-m}
when
\min(k,k_{1})\leqslant-5\beta m
,
we obtain (
8.20
) and conclude the proof.
∎

With Lemmas
8.5
and
8.6
we have reduced the proof Proposition
8.4

to the proof of the following proposition, which will occupy the rest of this subsection.

Proposition 8.7
.

With the assumptions and notation of Proposition
8.4
,
assume that
-5\beta m\leqslant k,k_{1}\leqslant\beta m
.
Then

\displaystyle\|I_{m,k,k_{1}}(h)\|_{L^{2}}\lesssim\rho(2^{m})^{1-\beta+\delta^{\prime}}2^{m}{\varepsilon}^{\beta},

(8.23)

for some
\delta^{\prime}>\delta
.

Proof.

We introduce an additional localization in
\Phi.
More precisely let us define, for
m=1,2,\dots

\displaystyle\begin{split}I_{m,k,k_{1},p}(t,\xi):=\frac{\xi}{\langle\xi\rangle}\varphi_{k}(\xi)\int_{0}^{t}sB(s)\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}e^{-is\Phi(\xi,\eta)}\varphi_{p}^{(p_{0})}(\Phi(\xi,\eta))\varphi_{k_{1}}(\eta)\,\langle\eta\rangle^{-1}\widetilde{h}(s,\eta)\\
\times\nu(\xi,\eta)d\eta\,\tau_{m}(s)ds,\end{split}

(8.24)

\displaystyle\Phi(\xi,\eta):=\langle\xi\rangle-\langle\eta\rangle-\lambda,\qquad p_{0}:=\lfloor-3m/5\rfloor.

We divide the proof in various cases and subcases depending on the sizes of the parameters

p
,
2^{p}\approx|\Phi|
, and
k_{1}
,
2^{k_{1}}\approx|\eta|
.

Case 1:
p=p_{0}
.

In this case the phase is small and there are few oscillations in
s
.
We integrate by parts in frequency in (
8.24
) and write (up to irrelevant constants)

\displaystyle\begin{split}&I_{m,k,k_{1},p_{0}}=J_{m,k,k_{1},p}\,+\,\{\mbox{easier terms}\},\\
&J_{m,k,k_{1},p}:=\frac{\xi}{\langle\xi\rangle}\int_{0}^{t}B(s)\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}e^{-is\Phi(\xi,\eta)}\frac{\eta}{|\eta|^{2}}\cdot\nabla_{\eta}\widetilde{h}(s,\eta)\varphi_{k}(\xi)\varphi_{k_{1}}(\eta)\varphi_{p_{0}}(\Phi)\nu(\xi,\eta)\,d\eta\,\tau_{m}(s)ds.\end{split}

(8.25)

The “easier terms” are those where
\nabla_{\eta}
hits the cutoff
\varphi_{k_{1}}
(or the factor of
\eta/|\eta|^{2}
),
or the cutoff
\varphi_{p}(\Phi)
, or the measure
\nu
;
in all these cases there is a net gain in negative powers of
s
.
For example, we see that
\partial_{\eta}\varphi_{p}(\Phi)=\varphi_{\sim p}(\Phi)2^{-p}\eta/\langle\eta\rangle
;
then, we can repeat the integration by parts in
\eta
gaining an
s^{-1}
factor, while we have only lost

2^{-p}\lesssim 2^{3m/5}
, and apply the same arguments given below.

Denote

K_{2}(\xi,\eta):=2^{-k_{1}}\varphi_{k}(\xi)\varphi_{k_{1}}(\eta)\varphi_{p_{0}}(\Phi)\nu(\xi,\eta).

Then, using Lemma
8.2
,

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}|K_{2}(\xi,\eta)|d\eta

\displaystyle\lesssim 2^{p_{0}-k_{1}-k_{1}^{-}}2^{2\beta m}\sup_{|\xi|\approx 2^{k},|\eta|\approx 2^{k_{1}}}|\nu(\xi,\eta)|,

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}|K_{2}(\xi,\eta)|d\xi

\displaystyle\lesssim 2^{p_{0}-k_{1}}2^{-k^{-}}2^{2\beta m}\sup_{|\xi|\approx 2^{k},|\eta|\approx 2^{k_{1}}}|\nu(\xi,\eta)|.

In particular, we see that (recall the notation (
1.69
))

\displaystyle{\|K_{2}\|}_{Sch}\lesssim 2^{p_{0}-k_{1}-k_{1}^{-}/2-k^{-}/2}2^{2\beta m}\lesssim 2^{-11m/20},

provided
\beta
is small enough.
As a consequence of Schur’s test we get

\displaystyle{\big\|J_{m,k,k_{1},p}\big\|}_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim 2^{m}\sup_{s\approx 2^{m}}{\Big\||B(s)|\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\big|K_{2}(\xi,\eta)\big|\,|\nabla_{\eta}\widetilde{h}(s,\eta)|d\eta\Big\|}_{L^{2}}\lesssim 2^{m}\rho(2^{m})^{1/2}{\|K_{2}\|}_{Sch}\cdot Z,

(8.26)

which is bounded by the right-hand side of (
8.23
).

Case 2:
p>p_{0}
.
In this case we can also take advantage of integration by parts in time,
writing

\displaystyle|I_{m,k,k_{1},p}|\leqslant\big|I^{(1)}_{m,k,k_{1},p}\big|+\big|I^{(2)}_{m,k,k_{1},p}\big|,

\displaystyle I^{(1)}_{m,k,k_{1},p}(t,\xi):=\tau_{m}(t)\,t\frac{\xi}{\langle\xi\rangle}B(t)\varphi_{k}(\xi)\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}e^{-it\Phi}\frac{\varphi_{p_{0}}(\Phi)}{\Phi}\varphi_{k_{1}}(\eta)\widetilde{h}(t,\eta)\langle\eta\rangle^{-1}\nu(\xi,\eta)d\eta,

(8.27)

\displaystyle I^{(2)}_{m,k,k_{1},p}(t,\xi):=\int_{0}^{t}\varphi_{k}(\xi)\frac{\xi}{\langle\xi\rangle}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}e^{-is\Phi}\frac{\varphi_{p}(\Phi)}{\Phi}\partial_{s}\big(s\,B(s)\tau_{m}(s)\widetilde{h}(s,\eta)\big)\varphi_{k_{1}}(\eta)\langle\eta\rangle^{-1}\nu(\xi,\eta)d\eta ds.

(8.28)

Denoting

\displaystyle K_{3;p,k,k_{1}}(\xi,\eta)=K_{3}(\xi,\eta):=\frac{\varphi_{p}\big(\Phi(\xi,\eta)\big)}{\Phi(\xi,\eta)}\varphi_{[k_{1}-2,k_{1}+2]}(\eta)\varphi_{k}(\xi)\langle\eta\rangle^{-1}\nu(\xi,\eta),

(8.29)

we have

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}|K_{3}(\xi,\eta)|d\eta

\displaystyle\lesssim\min\big(2^{-k_{1}^{+}-k_{1}^{-}}2^{2\beta m},2^{-p+3k_{1}}\big)\sup_{|\xi|\approx 2^{k},|\eta|\approx 2^{k_{1}}}|\nu(\xi,\eta)|,

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}|K_{3}(\xi,\eta)|d\xi

\displaystyle\lesssim\min\big(2^{-k_{1}^{+}}2^{-k^{-}}2^{2\beta m},2^{-p+3k}\big)\sup_{|\xi|\approx 2^{k},|\eta|\approx 2^{k_{1}}}|\nu(\xi,\eta)|.

Then, using (
8.6
),
we get (recall the definition (
1.69
))

\displaystyle{\|K_{3}\|}_{Sch}\lesssim\min\big(2^{(1/2)(-2p+3k_{1}+3k)},2^{-(1/2)(k_{1}^{-}+k^{-})}\big)2^{2\beta m}\lesssim 2^{7\beta m},

(8.30)

where the last inequality follows from
k_{1},k\geqslant-5\beta m
.

Estimate of (
8.27
)
.
To treat
I^{(1)}_{m,k,k_{1},p}
in (
8.27
) we integrate by parts in frequency and find that

\displaystyle I^{(1)}_{m,k,k_{1},p}=\tau_{m}(t)\frac{\xi}{\langle\xi\rangle}B(t)\varphi_{k}(\xi)\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}e^{-it\Phi}\frac{\varphi_{p}(\Phi)}{\Phi}\,\frac{\eta}{|\eta|^{2}}\cdot\nabla_{\eta}\widetilde{h}(t,\eta)\varphi_{k_{1}}(\eta)\nu(\xi,\eta)d\eta\,+\,\{\mbox{easier terms}\}.

The “easier terms” are similar to those in (
8.25
),
and can be dealt with arguing as in the paragraph that follows that expression.
Denoting
K_{4}(\xi,\eta):=\varphi_{p}(\Phi)\Phi^{-1}\varphi_{k_{1}}(\eta)\,\eta|\eta|^{-2}\,\nu(\xi,\eta)
,
we have, by the same reasoning as in (
8.30
),

\displaystyle{\|K_{4}\|}_{Sch}\lesssim\min\big(2^{-k_{1}-k_{1}^{-}/2-k^{-}/2},2^{-p+k_{1}/2+3k/2}\big)2^{2\beta m}\lesssim 2^{12\beta m}.

(8.31)

Using this fact, we can bound, for
|t|\approx 2^{m}

\displaystyle{\big\|I^{(1)}_{m,k,k_{1},p}(t)\big\|}_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\rho^{1/2}(2^{m})\cdot{\|K_{4}\|}_{Sch}\cdot{\|\nabla_{\eta}\widetilde{h}(t)\|}_{L^{2}}\lesssim 2^{-m/2}\cdot 2^{12\beta m}\cdot Z,

(8.32)

which is sufficient.

Estimate of (
8.28
)
.
Moving on to
I^{(2)}_{m,k,k_{1},p},
we start with the easier case where the time derivative falls
on the amplitude
B(s)
. We denote the corresponding term

\displaystyle I^{(21)}_{m,k,k_{1},p}(t,\xi):=\int_{0}^{t}\frac{\xi}{\langle\xi\rangle}\varphi_{k}(\xi)\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}e^{-is\Phi}\frac{\varphi_{p}(\Phi)}{\Phi}\,\frac{d}{ds}\big(sB(s)\big)\,\widetilde{h}(s,\eta)\varphi_{k_{1}}(\eta)\langle\eta\rangle^{-1}\nu(\xi,\eta)d\eta\,\tau_{m}(s)ds.

Notice that this is similar to the boundary term just treated and, integrating by parts in
\eta
and using Lemma
3.5
, we find

\displaystyle{\big\|I_{m,k,k_{1},p}^{(21)}(t,\xi)\big\|}_{L^{2}}\lesssim 2^{m}\cdot\big(\rho(2^{m})^{3/2}+\rho(2^{m})^{3/2-a}2^{-m}+\rho(2^{m})^{1/2}2^{-m}\big)\cdot Z.

This yields the desired result.

Next, we treat the case where the
s
-derivative in (
8.28
) falls on the profile
\widetilde{h}
, that is,

\displaystyle I^{(22)}_{m,k,k_{1},p}(t,\xi):=\int_{0}^{t}s\frac{\xi}{\langle\xi\rangle}\varphi_{k}(\xi)\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}e^{-is\Phi}\frac{\varphi_{p}(\Phi)}{\Phi}B(s)\tau_{m}(s)\,\partial_{s}\widetilde{h}(s,\eta)\varphi_{k_{1}}(\eta)\langle\eta\rangle^{-1}\nu(\xi,\eta)d\eta ds.

(8.33)

Let
\psi(x)=\varphi(x)/x\in\mathcal{S}
, we can write
(recall that
R
is the distorted Riesz transform and
H=-\Delta+V
the Schrödinger operator)

\displaystyle I^{(22)}_{m,k,k_{1},p}(t,\xi)

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{t}s\frac{\xi}{\langle\xi\rangle}\varphi_{k}(\xi)\int_{\mathbb{R}}2^{-p}\check{\psi}(z)\Big[\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}e^{-i(s+z2^{-p})\Phi}B(s)\tau_{m}(s)\,\langle\eta\rangle^{-1}\partial_{s}\widetilde{h}(s,\eta)\varphi_{k_{1}}(\eta)\nu(\xi,\eta)d\eta\Big]dzds

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}}2^{-p}\check{\psi}(z)\,\widetilde{\mathcal{F}}^{-1}\Big[P_{k}\frac{R|H|^{1/2}}{L}\int_{0}^{t}s\,e^{-i(s+z2^{-p})L}\big(e^{-i(s+z2^{-p})\lambda}B(s)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\times\phi\,e^{i(s+z2^{-p})L}P_{k_{1}}L^{-1}\partial_{s}h(s)\big)\tau_{m}(s)ds\Big]dz.

(8.34)

Therefore, we can write that for any
q\in[2,\infty],

\displaystyle{\big\|I^{(22)}_{m,k,k_{1},p}(t,\xi)\big\|}_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{\mathbb{R}}2^{-p}|\check{\psi}(z)|{\big\|sB(s)\cdot\phi\,e^{i(s+z2^{-p})L}P_{k_{1}}\partial_{s}h(s)\big\|}_{L^{2}_{s\approx 2^{m}}L^{1}_{x}}\,dz

\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{\mathbb{R}}2^{-p}|\check{\psi}(z)|\cdot 2^{3m/2}\rho(2^{m})^{1/2}\cdot\sup_{s\approx 2^{m}}{\big\|e^{i(s+z2^{-p})L}P_{k_{1}}\partial_{s}h(s)\big\|}_{L^{q}_{x}}\,dz.

(8.35)

Next, recall that (see (
4.5
) and (
4.3
) or, equivalently, (
1.17
))

\displaystyle\begin{split}\partial_{s}\widetilde{h}(s)&=e^{-is\langle\eta\rangle}\Big[\widetilde{\mathcal{F}}(a^{2}(s)\theta)+\widetilde{\mathcal{F}}\big(a(s)\phi\,L^{-1}\Im w(s)\big)+\widetilde{\mathcal{F}}\big(\big(L^{-1}\Im w(s)\big)^{2}\big)\Big]-\partial_{s}\widetilde{g}(s).\end{split}

(8.36)

In particular, from the definition of
g
, we see that

\displaystyle\begin{split}\partial_{s}h(s)-e^{-isL}\big(L^{-1}\Im w\big)^{2}=e^{-isL}O(\rho(s))\theta+e^{-isL}\big(a(s)\phi\cdot L^{-1}\Im w(s)\big).\end{split}

(8.37)

We can then use (
8.35
)
to estimate the contributions of the two terms on the right-hand side above to (
8.34
).
For the first term we use the estimate, for all
s\approx 2^{m}
,

\displaystyle{\|e^{iz2^{-p}L}\rho(s)\theta\|}_{L^{\infty}_{x}}\lesssim(1+z2^{-p})^{-3/2}\rho(2^{m}),

which, when plugged in (
8.35
) with
q=\infty
, gives the upperbound

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}}2^{-p}|\check{\psi}(z)|\cdot 2^{3m/2}\rho(2^{m})^{3/2}(1+z2^{-p})^{-3/2}dz\lesssim 2^{(0+)m},

(recall that
\psi
is Schwartz).
For the second term on the right-hand side of (
8.37
) we can proceed similarly, using instead the estimate

\displaystyle{\big\|e^{iz2^{-p}L}a(s)\phi\cdot L^{-1}\Im w(s)\phi\big\|}_{L^{\infty}_{x}}

\displaystyle\lesssim(1+z2^{-p})^{-3/2}{\big\|a(s)\phi\cdot L^{-1}\Im w(s)\big\|}_{W^{3,1}\cap H^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim(1+z2^{-p})^{-3/2}\cdot\rho(2^{m})^{1/2}\cdot\rho(2^{m})^{1-\beta}2^{5\delta_{N}m}{\varepsilon}^{\beta}

relying on (
2.14
) (with
p=6
) and
Sobolev’s embedding, the
H^{N}
a priori bound and Sobolev-Gagliardo-Nirenberg interpolation
to deal with the couple extra derivatives.

To complete the estimate of
I^{(22)}_{m,k,k_{1},p}(t,\xi)
it then suffices to estimate
by the right-hand side of (
8.23
) the remaining contribution to (
8.33
) given by

\displaystyle A_{m,k,k_{1},p}(t,\xi):=\frac{\xi}{\langle\xi\rangle}\int_{0}^{t}s\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}e^{-is(\langle\xi\rangle-\lambda)}B(s)\,\widetilde{\mathcal{F}}\big(\big(L^{-1}\Im w\big)^{2}\big)(s,\eta)K_{3}(\xi,\eta)d\eta\,\tau_{m}(s)ds,

(8.38)

where
K_{3}(\xi,\eta)
is the kernel defined in (
8.29
) which satisfies (
8.30
).

We can treat right away the case of relatively large
p
using (
8.35
).
Indeed, using
L^{6}-L^{3}
estimates from Lemma
4.4
we see that

\displaystyle{\big\|e^{-iz2^{-p}L}\big(L^{-1}\Im w\big)^{2}(s)\big\|}_{L^{2}_{x}}\lesssim\rho(2^{m})^{2-2\beta}\cdot 2^{m/2+2\delta_{N}m}{\varepsilon}^{2\beta},

which plugged into (
8.35
) (with
q=2
), gives an acceptable bound if, for example,
p\geqslant-m/3
.

To summarize, we see that in order to complete the estimate of
I^{(22)}_{m,k,k_{1},p}(t,\xi)
, hence the proof of Proposition
8.7
,
it will then suffice to show the following:

Lemma 8.8
.

With the assumptions and notation of Proposition
8.7
, with the definition (
8.38
),
for all
m=1,2,\dots
, and
-3m/5<p<-m/3
we have

\displaystyle{\big\|A_{m,k,k_{1},p}\big\|}_{L^{2}}\lesssim\rho(2^{m})^{1-\beta+\delta^{\prime}}2^{m}{\varepsilon}^{\beta},

(8.39)

for some
\delta^{\prime}>\delta
.

The proof of this lemma occupies the next subsection.

8.4.
Proof of Lemma
8.8

The last main term to treat for the proof of Proposition
8.4
is (
8.38
).
Expanding out
\widetilde{\mathcal{F}}(L^{-1}\Im w)^{2}
, disregarding irrelevant constants, we can write

\displaystyle\begin{split}&A=A_{m,k,k_{1},p}=\sum_{\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2}\in\{+,-\}}A^{\epsilon_{1}\epsilon_{2}},\qquad A^{\epsilon_{1}\epsilon_{2}}=A_{S}^{\epsilon_{1}\epsilon_{2}}+A_{R}^{\epsilon_{1}\epsilon_{2}}+A_{Re}^{\epsilon_{1}\epsilon_{2}}\\
&A_{\ast}^{\epsilon_{1}\epsilon_{2}}(t,\xi)=\int_{0}^{t}s\,B(s)\,\frac{\xi}{\langle\xi\rangle}\varphi_{k}(\xi)\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{9}}e^{-is\Phi^{\epsilon_{1}\epsilon_{2}}(\eta,\sigma,\rho)}\langle\sigma\rangle^{-1}\widetilde{f_{\epsilon_{1}}}(s,\sigma)\,\langle\rho\rangle^{-1}\widetilde{f_{\epsilon_{2}}}(s,\rho)\\
&\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\times\mu^{\ast}(\eta,\sigma,\rho)\,K_{3}(\xi,\eta)d\sigma d\rho\,d\eta\,\tau_{m}(s)ds,\qquad\ast\in\{S,R,Re\},\\
&f_{+}=f,\quad f_{-}=\overline{f},\quad\\
&\Phi^{\epsilon_{1}\epsilon_{2}}(\eta,\sigma,\rho):=\langle\xi\rangle-\lambda-\epsilon_{1}\langle\sigma\rangle-\epsilon_{2}\langle\rho\rangle.\end{split}

(8.40)

Note that we used Proposition
5.6
to decompose the quadratic terms into
the piece
A_{S}^{\epsilon_{1}\epsilon_{2}}
corresponding to the singular part of the measure,
the piece
A_{R}^{\epsilon_{1}\epsilon_{2}}
corresponding to the regular part of the measure,
and remainders
A_{Re}^{\epsilon_{1}\epsilon_{2}}
.
We treat these terms in separate sections below.

8.4.1.
Estimate of
A_{S}^{\epsilon_{1}\epsilon_{2}}
.

We use (
5.15
) to further decompose (again we disregard irrelevant constants)

\displaystyle A_{S}^{\epsilon_{1}\epsilon_{2}}=A_{0}^{\epsilon_{1}\epsilon_{2}}+A_{1}^{\epsilon_{1}\epsilon_{2}},

(8.41)

where

\displaystyle\begin{split}A_{0}^{\epsilon_{1}\epsilon_{2}}(t,\xi):=\sum_{k_{2},k_{3}\in{\mathbb{Z}}}\int_{0}^{t}s\frac{\xi}{\langle\xi\rangle}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{6}}e^{-is\Phi_{0}^{\epsilon_{1}\epsilon_{2}}(\xi,\eta,\sigma)}B(s)\,\langle\sigma\rangle^{-1}\big(\varphi_{k_{2}}\widetilde{f_{\epsilon_{1}}}\big)(\sigma)\\
\qquad\qquad\times\langle\eta-\sigma\rangle^{-1}\big(\varphi_{k_{3}}\widetilde{f_{\epsilon_{2}}}\big)(s,\eta-\sigma)\,d\sigma\,K_{3}(\xi,\eta)d\eta\,\tau_{m}(s)ds,\end{split}

(8.42)

\displaystyle\Phi_{0}^{\epsilon_{1}\epsilon_{2}}(\xi,\eta,\sigma):=\langle\xi\rangle-\lambda-\epsilon_{1}\langle\sigma\rangle-\epsilon_{2}\langle\eta-\sigma\rangle,

with
K_{3}=K_{3;p,k,k_{1}}
defined in (
8.29
),
and where
A_{1}^{\epsilon_{1}\epsilon_{2}}
contains the terms corresponding to the
\nu_{1}
measures;
these latter are explicitly written out in (
8.50
) and then estimated after that.
Note that we have inserted additional localization in

|\sigma|\approx 2^{k_{2}}
and
|\eta-\sigma|\approx 2^{k_{3}}
.
We may also restrict to

\displaystyle k_{2},k_{3}\in[-3m,\beta m]\cap{\mathbb{Z}},

(8.43)

since very low and high frequencies are easier to estimate, as it will be clear to the reader.
Since the sums over
k_{2}
and
k_{3}
will then cost at most an
O(m^{2})
factor,
we will abuse notation slightly and discard them from (
8.42
).
To improve legibility, we may sometime drop the indices
\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2}

in the estimates of
A_{0}^{{\epsilon}_{1},{\epsilon}_{2}}=A_{0}
,
since all the cases can be treated identically.
Recall that we have also restricted our estimates to
p<-m/3
already.

Estimate of
A_{0}
.

We notice that the phase in (
8.42
) satisfies

\displaystyle|\Phi_{0}^{\epsilon_{1}\epsilon_{2}}|\gtrsim|\langle\eta\rangle-\epsilon_{1}\langle\sigma\rangle-\epsilon_{2}\langle\eta-\sigma\rangle\big|-\big|\langle\xi\rangle-\lambda-\langle\eta\rangle|\gtrsim 2^{-\beta m}+O(2^{p})\gtrsim 2^{-\beta m},

(8.44)

in view of Lemma
5.10
, inequality (
5.47
),
and the restrictions
|\eta|,|\sigma|\lesssim 2^{\beta m}
, as well as
p<-m/3.

We can then integrate by parts in
s
in (
8.42
) at very little cost. Moreover, we have

\displaystyle\big|\partial_{\eta}\Phi_{0}^{\epsilon_{1}\epsilon_{2}}\big|\approx 2^{k_{3}},\qquad\big|\partial_{\eta+\sigma}\Phi_{0}^{\epsilon_{1}\epsilon_{2}}\big|\approx 2^{k_{2}},

(8.45)

and, therefore we can also integrate by parts in
\eta
or
\eta+\sigma
unless
k_{3}
or
k_{2}
are too small.

Integrating by parts in
s
gives two terms that are similar (depending on which one of the two profiles
gets differentiated) plus easier ones (when
B
or
\tau_{m}
are differentiated);
therefore, we can write

\displaystyle\begin{split}\|A_{0}\|_{L^{2}}&\lesssim\Bigg\|\int_{0}^{t}sB(s)\frac{\xi}{\langle\xi\rangle}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{6}}e^{-is\Phi_{0}}\langle\sigma\rangle^{-1}\,\partial_{s}\widetilde{f}(s,\sigma)\,\langle\eta-\sigma\rangle^{-1}\widetilde{f}(s,\eta-\sigma)\,\\
&\times\frac{\varphi_{k_{2}}(\sigma)\varphi_{k_{3}}(\eta-\sigma)}{\Phi_{0}(\xi,\eta,\sigma)}K_{3}(\xi,\eta)d\sigma d\eta\,\tau_{m}(s)ds\Bigg\|_{L^{2}}+\{\textrm{similar or easier terms}\}.\end{split}

For the leading order term above we have the upper bound

\displaystyle\begin{split}&C2^{2m}\rho(2^{m})^{1/2}\cdot\sup_{s\approx 2^{m}}{\Big\|\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}K^{f}(\xi,\sigma)\,e^{is\langle\sigma\rangle}\partial_{s}\widetilde{f}(s,\sigma)d\sigma\Big\|}_{L^{2}_{\xi}},\\
&K^{f}(\xi,\sigma):=\langle\sigma\rangle^{-1}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}e^{is\langle\eta-\sigma\rangle}\langle\eta-\sigma\rangle^{-1}\widetilde{f}(s,\eta-\sigma)\,\frac{\varphi_{k_{2}}(\sigma)\varphi_{k_{3}}(\eta-\sigma)}{\Phi_{0}(\xi,\eta,\sigma)}K_{3}(\xi,\eta)d\eta.\end{split}

(8.46)

Starting from the above estimate we distinguish a few cases to eventually obtain a uniform bound
on the
L^{2}
-norm of
A_{0}
. Since
{\|\partial_{s}f\|}_{L^{2}}\lesssim\rho(2^{m})
, see Lemma
4.5
,
applying Schur’s test to (
8.46
), we see that it suffices to prove that

\displaystyle{\|K^{f}\|}_{Sch}\lesssim 2^{-m/2-10\beta m}.

(8.47)

To prove (
8.47
) we split
f=h+g
, denoting
K^{h}
and
K^{g}
the corresponding kernels.
To deal with
K^{h}
we integrate by parts in frequency when
k_{3}\geqslant-m/2+\delta m

for some small
\delta>0
, and obtain (again we disregard lower order terms)

\displaystyle{\|K^{h}(\xi,\sigma)\|}_{Sch}

\displaystyle\lesssim 2^{\beta m}\cdot 2^{-m}2^{-k_{3}}{\Big\|\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}|\nabla\widetilde{h}(s,\eta-\sigma)|\,\varphi_{k_{3}}(\eta-\sigma)|K_{3}(\xi,\eta)|d\eta\Big\|}_{Sch}

(8.48)

\displaystyle\lesssim 2^{\beta m-m}\cdot 2^{-k_{3}}\|K_{3}\|_{Sch}\|\varphi_{k_{3}}\nabla\widetilde{h}\|_{L^{1}}\lesssim 2^{11\beta m-m}\cdot 2^{k_{3}/2}\cdot Z,

having used (
8.30
) and (
8.44
). This yields (
8.47
).
If instead
k_{3}\leqslant-m/2+\delta m
, we just estimate

\displaystyle{\|K^{h}(\xi,\sigma)\|}_{Sch}

\displaystyle\lesssim 2^{\beta m}\cdot\|K_{3}\|_{Sch}\|\varphi_{k_{3}}\widetilde{h}\|_{L^{1}}\lesssim 2^{\beta m}\cdot 2^{5k_{3}/2}\cdot Z,

which again is more than sufficient.

For the
K^{g}
kernel, we introduce a cut-off
\varphi_{\ell}^{(\ell_{0})}(\langle\eta-\sigma\rangle-2\lambda),\ell\geqslant\ell_{0}:=\lfloor-m+\delta m\rfloor
.
We integrate directly in frequency when
\ell=\ell_{0}
and integrate by parts in frequency for the other terms:

\displaystyle\begin{split}\|K^{g}(\xi,\sigma)\|_{Sch}&\lesssim 2^{\beta m}{\Big\|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}|\widetilde{g}(s,\eta-\sigma)|\varphi_{\ell_{0}}\langle\eta-\sigma\rangle-2\lambda)|K_{3}(\xi,\eta)|d\eta\Big\|}_{Sch}\\
&+2^{\beta m}\cdot 2^{-m}\sum_{\ell_{0}<\ell<-10}{\Big\|\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}|\nabla\widetilde{g}(s,\eta-\sigma)|\,|\varphi_{\ell}(\langle\eta-\sigma\rangle-2\lambda)|K_{3}(\xi,\eta)|d\eta\Big\|}_{Sch}\\
&\lesssim 2^{11\beta m}\cdot 2^{\ell_{0}}\cdot 2^{m}\rho(2^{m})m+2^{11\beta m-m}\cdot 2^{m}\rho(2^{m})m,\end{split}

(8.49)

where we used (
8.30
) and the bounds (
4.24
) and (
4.27
)
for the last line. This yields (
8.47
) and concludes the estimate of
A_{0}
.

8.4.2.
Estimate of
A_{1}^{\epsilon_{1}\epsilon_{2}}

We now look at the other terms in (
8.41
).
Inserting cutoffs
\varphi_{k_{2}}(\sigma)
and
\varphi_{k_{3}}(\rho)
,
similarly to the case of
A_{0}
above, we can write
(using the symmetry in
(\rho,\sigma)
, with a slight abuse of notation)

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\|A_{1}^{\epsilon_{1}\epsilon_{2}}\|_{L^{2}}\lesssim\|A_{1,1}^{\epsilon_{1}\epsilon_{2}}\|_{L^{2}}+\|A_{1,2}^{\epsilon_{1}\epsilon_{2}}\|_{L^{2}},\\
\\
\hfil\displaystyle\begin{split}A_{1,1}^{\epsilon_{1}\epsilon_{2}}(t,\xi):=\int_{0}^{t}sB(s)\frac{\xi}{\langle\xi\rangle}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{9}}e^{-is\Phi_{1}^{\epsilon_{1}\epsilon_{2}}(\xi,\sigma,\rho)}\,\langle\sigma\rangle^{-1}\widetilde{f}(s,\sigma)\langle\rho\rangle^{-1}\widetilde{f}(s,\rho)\\
\varphi_{k_{2}}(\sigma)\varphi_{k_{3}}(\rho)\nu_{1}^{S}(-\eta+\sigma,\rho)d\sigma d\rho\,K_{3;k,k_{1},p}(\xi,\eta)d\eta\,\tau_{m}(s)ds,\end{split}\\
\hfil\displaystyle\begin{split}A_{1,2}^{\epsilon_{1}\epsilon_{2}}(t,\xi):=\int_{0}^{t}sB(s)\frac{\xi}{\langle\xi\rangle}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{9}}e^{-is\Phi_{1}^{\epsilon_{1}\epsilon_{2}}(\xi,\sigma,\rho)}\,\langle\sigma\rangle^{-1}\widetilde{f}(s,\sigma)\langle\rho\rangle^{-1}\widetilde{f}(s,\rho)\\
\varphi_{k_{2}}(\sigma)\varphi_{k_{3}}(\rho)\overline{\nu_{1}^{S}(-\sigma-\rho,\eta)}d\sigma d\rho\,K_{3;k,k_{1},p}(\xi,\eta)d\eta\,\tau_{m}(s)ds,\end{split}\\
\\
&\Phi_{1}^{\epsilon_{1}\epsilon_{2}}(\xi,\sigma,\rho):=\langle\xi\rangle-\lambda-\epsilon_{1}\langle\sigma\rangle-\epsilon_{2}\langle\rho\rangle.\end{split}

(8.50)

As before, we may assume,

\displaystyle-3m\leqslant k_{2}\leqslant k_{3}\leqslant\beta m,\qquad p\leqslant-m/3.

(8.51)

In what follows
A_{1,1}^{\epsilon_{1}\epsilon_{2}}
and
A_{1,2}^{\epsilon_{1}\epsilon_{2}}

will be treated very similarly.
Therefore, we will mostly focus on the first term, and then indicate how the argument
can be adapted for
A_{1,2}^{\epsilon_{1}\epsilon_{2}}
.

Recall the definition of
\nu_{1}^{S}
in (
5.17
)-(
5.18
).
As it will be clear from the argument below, it suffices to look at the
leading order contribution from
the first term in (
5.17
) containing the
\mathrm{p.v.}
distribution; that is,
it suffices to look at (
8.50
) with
\nu_{1}^{S}(p,q)
replaced by

\displaystyle\varphi_{\leqslant-M_{0}-5}(|p|-|q|)\frac{b_{0}(p,q)}{|p|}\mathrm{p.v.}\frac{1}{|p|-|q|}.

(8.52)

The
\delta(|p|-|q|)
part can be handled similarly (see also the proof of Theorem 6.1 from
[
41
]
).
Without loss of generality we may also replace
b_{0}
by
1
.
One can easily handle the remaining parts from (
5.17
) using Lemma
B.3
, along with the fast decay of the
K_{a}
functions appearing in (
5.17
),
and the summation properties of
b_{a,J}
.

We insert a localization in the singularity of the measure above,
by decomposing

\displaystyle\varphi_{\leqslant-M_{0}-5}(|\rho|-|-\eta+\sigma|)=\sum_{C\leqslant-M_{0}-5}\varphi_{C}(|\rho|-|-\eta+\sigma|).

We denote
A_{1,1;C}^{\epsilon_{1}\epsilon_{2}}
the terms correspond to the
\varphi_{C}
cutoff, which is given by
(changing variable
\sigma\mapsto\sigma+\eta
in (
8.50
))

\displaystyle\begin{split}A_{1,1,C}^{\epsilon_{1}\epsilon_{2}}(t,\xi):=\int_{0}^{t}sB(s)\frac{\xi}{\langle\xi\rangle}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}e^{-is(\langle\xi\rangle-\lambda)}R_{k_{2},k_{3},C}(f_{1},f_{2})(\eta)K_{3;k,k_{1},p}(\xi,\eta)d\eta\,\tau_{m}(s)ds,\end{split}

(8.53)

where

\displaystyle R_{k_{2},k_{3},C}(f_{1},f_{2})(s,\eta):=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{6}}\widetilde{f_{1}}(s,\sigma+\eta)\widetilde{f_{2}}(s,\rho)\frac{1}{|\sigma|}\frac{\varphi_{C}(|\sigma|-|\rho|)}{|\sigma|-|\rho|}d\sigma d\rho,

\displaystyle\widetilde{f_{1}}(s,\sigma+\eta):=\langle\sigma+\eta\rangle^{-1}\varphi_{k_{2}}(\sigma+\eta)e^{-is{\epsilon}_{1}\langle\sigma+\eta\rangle}\widetilde{f_{{\epsilon}_{1}}}(s,\sigma+\eta),

\displaystyle\widetilde{f_{2}}(s,\rho):=\langle\rho\rangle^{-1}\varphi_{k_{3}}(\rho)e^{-is{\epsilon}_{2}\langle\rho\rangle}\widetilde{f_{{\epsilon}_{2}}}(s,\rho).

We look at two cases:

Case 1:
C\leqslant C_{0}:=-500m
.
We first decompose in physical space

\displaystyle f_{1}:=\varphi_{\geqslant X}f_{1}+\varphi_{<X}f_{1}=:f_{11}+f_{12},\qquad X:=10m-\frac{1}{100}C,

and then treat the two pieces above separately.

For the term with
f_{11}
we bound, using the product estimate from Lemma
B.3
,
(recall that we have
k_{1},k_{2},k_{3}\leqslant\beta m
) and the boundedness of wave operators
on weighted spaces (
2.8
):

\displaystyle\sum_{C\leqslant C_{0}}\|R_{k_{2},k_{3},C}(f_{11},f_{2})\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{C\leqslant C_{0}}2^{10\beta m}\cdot{\big\|\widetilde{f_{11}}\big\|}_{L^{2}}{\big\|\widetilde{f_{2}}\big\|}_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{C\leqslant C_{0}}2^{10\beta m}\cdot 2^{-X}\cdot\|\langle x\rangle f_{11}\|_{L^{2}}\|f_{2}\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{C\leqslant C_{0}}2^{10\beta m}\cdot 2^{-10m+\frac{1}{100}C}\cdot\|\nabla\widetilde{f_{1}}\|_{L^{2}}\|f_{2}\|_{L^{2}}\lesssim 2^{-10m}Z,

which is sufficient.

For the term involving
f_{12}
we write

\displaystyle R_{k_{2},k_{3},C}(f_{12},f_{2})=R^{(1)}(f_{12},f_{2})+R^{(2)}(f_{12},f_{2})+R^{(3)}(f_{12},f_{2}),

\displaystyle R^{(1)}(f_{12},f_{2}):=\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{6}}\big[\widetilde{f_{12}}(s,\eta+\sigma)-\widetilde{f_{12}}(s,\eta+\sigma|\rho|/|\sigma|)\big]\widetilde{f_{2}}(s,\rho)\frac{1}{|\sigma|}\frac{\varphi_{C}\big(|\sigma|-|\rho|\big)}{|\sigma|-|\rho|}d\sigma d\rho,

\displaystyle R^{(2)}(f_{12},f_{2}):=\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{6}}\widetilde{f_{12}}(s,\eta+\sigma)\widetilde{f_{2}}(s,\rho)\frac{1}{|\sigma|^{2}}\varphi_{C}\big(|\sigma|-|\rho|\big)d\sigma d\rho,

\displaystyle R^{(3)}(f_{12},f_{2})=\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{6}}\widetilde{f_{12}}(s,\eta+\sigma|\rho|/|\sigma|)\widetilde{f_{2}}(s,\rho)\frac{|\rho|}{|\sigma|^{2}}\frac{\varphi_{C}\big(|\sigma|-|\rho|\big)}{|\eta|-|\sigma|}d\sigma d\rho.

Notice that the last term equals zero, as it can be seen passing to polar coordinates in
\sigma
.
For the second term, using again Lemma
B.3
, we find that

\displaystyle\|R^{(2)}(f_{12},f_{2})\|_{L^{2}}\lesssim 2^{10\beta m}\cdot 2^{C}\cdot\|f_{12}\|_{L^{2}}\|f_{2}\|_{L^{2}},

which is enough to conclude.
For the remaining term we first write that

\displaystyle\widetilde{f_{12}}(\eta+\sigma)-\widetilde{f_{12}}(\eta+\theta|\rho|)=\int_{0}^{1}\nabla\widetilde{f_{12}}(\eta+t\sigma+(1-t)|\rho|\theta)\cdot\theta(|\sigma|-|\rho|),

with
\theta:=\sigma/|\sigma|.
Using the symbol bounds on this expression ensuing from

|\nabla^{\alpha}\widetilde{f_{12}}|\lesssim 2^{|\alpha|X}
in combination with Lemma
B.3
,
we can obtain

\displaystyle\sum_{C\leqslant C_{0}}{\|R^{(1)}(f_{12},f_{2})\|}_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim 2^{10\beta m}\sum_{C\leqslant C_{0}}2^{15X}\cdot 2^{C}\cdot\|\nabla\widetilde{f_{12}}\|_{L^{2}}\|f_{2}\|_{L^{2}}\lesssim 2^{-10m}Z

in view of our choices of
X
and
C_{0}
.
We have then taken care of the region very close to the singularity of the
\mathrm{p.v.}

Case 2:
C_{0}<C\leqslant-M_{0}-5
.
For this case we use that the phase, see (
8.50
), is uniformly lower bounded:

\displaystyle|\Phi_{1}^{\epsilon_{1}\epsilon_{2}}(\xi,\sigma,\rho)|\gtrsim|\langle\eta\rangle-\epsilon_{1}\langle\sigma\rangle-\epsilon_{2}\langle\rho\rangle\big|-\big|\langle\xi\rangle-\lambda-\langle\eta\rangle|\gtrsim 2^{-\beta m}+O(2^{p})\gtrsim 2^{-\beta m};

(8.54)

see Lemma
5.10
.
Once again we will drop the signs
\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2}
since all cases can be treated identically.
Thanks to the lower bound on the phase,
we can then proceed very similarly to the case of
A_{0}
above; see the argument after (
8.44
).

More precisely, we integrate by parts in time in (
8.53
) and obtain

\displaystyle\|A_{1,1;C}\|_{L^{2}}\lesssim\Big\|\int_{0}^{t}sB(s)\frac{\xi}{\langle\xi\rangle}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{9}}e^{-is\Phi_{1}^{{\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2}}}}\langle\sigma\rangle^{-1}\partial_{s}\widetilde{f_{\epsilon_{1}}}(s,\sigma)\langle\rho\rangle^{-1}\widetilde{f_{\epsilon_{2}}}(s,\rho)\frac{\varphi_{k_{2}}(\sigma)\varphi_{k_{3}}(\rho)}{\Phi_{1}(\xi,\sigma,\rho)}

\displaystyle\times\varphi_{C}(|\eta-\sigma|-|\rho|)\nu_{1}^{S}(-\eta+\sigma,\rho)d\sigma d\rho K_{3;k,k_{1},p}(\xi,\eta)d\eta\,\tau_{m}(s)ds\Big\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle+\{\textrm{similar or easier terms}\}.

The “similar or easier terms” include the boundary terms, which can be treated in the same way as the main term,
and the terms where
\partial_{s}
hits
sB(s)
that are completely analogous.
We can also drop the dependence on the index
C
since in this case there are
O(m)
terms in the sum,
therefore a gain of a factor
2^{-\delta m}
for some
\delta>0
will ensure convergence.

We write, as in the case of
A_{0}
, see (
8.46
), that the main contribution to

{\big\|A_{1,1}(t,\cdot)\big\|}_{L^{2}}
is bounded by a numerical constant times

\displaystyle\begin{split}&2^{2m}\rho(2^{m})^{1/2}\cdot\sup_{s\approx 2^{m}}{\Big\|\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}K^{f}(\xi,\sigma)\,e^{is\langle\sigma\rangle}\partial_{s}\widetilde{f}(s,\sigma)d\sigma\Big\|}_{L^{2}_{\xi}},\\
\hfil\displaystyle\begin{split}K^{f}(\xi,\sigma):=\langle\sigma\rangle^{-1}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{6}}e^{is\langle\rho\rangle}\langle\rho\rangle^{-1}\widetilde{f}(s,\rho)\,\frac{\varphi_{k_{2}}(\sigma)\varphi_{k_{3}}(\rho)}{\Phi_{1}(\xi,\sigma,\rho)}K_{3}(\xi,\eta)\\
\times\varphi_{C}(|\eta-\sigma|-|\rho|)\nu_{1}^{S}(-\eta+\sigma,\rho)d\rho d\eta.\end{split}\end{split}

(8.55)

Note that we can insert a cut-off
\varphi_{\sim k_{3}}(-\eta+\sigma)
in
K^{f}
given the support property of
\nu_{1}^{S}
,
see (
5.17
) and (
B.5
).
Once again, in view of Lemma
4.5
, it suffices to show

\displaystyle{\|K^{f}\|}_{Sch}\lesssim 2^{-m/2-10\beta m}.

(8.56)

We split again
f=g+h
and denote
K^{g}
and
K^{h}
the corresponding kernels from (
8.55
).
For
K^{h},
if
k_{3}\leqslant-2m/5,
we can estimate, using (
8.30
),

\displaystyle\|K^{h}\|_{Sch}\lesssim 2^{\beta m}\cdot{\|K_{3}\|}_{Sch}{\|\varphi_{k_{3}}\widetilde{h}\|}_{L^{1}}\cdot 2^{-k_{3}}\lesssim 2^{8\beta m}\cdot 2^{-k_{3}}\cdot 2^{5k_{3}/2}Z,

(8.57)

which is acceptable. Note the factor of
2^{-k_{3}}
coming from the
1/|\eta-\sigma|
factor in
the leading order expression for
\nu_{1}^{S}(-\eta+\sigma,\rho)
, see (
5.17
).

If
k_{3}>-2m/5,
we integrate by parts in the formula for
K^{h}
using the identity,
for a differentiable function
F
,

\displaystyle\bigg(\frac{\eta-\sigma}{|\eta-\sigma|}\cdot\nabla_{\eta}+\frac{\rho}{|\rho|}\cdot\nabla_{\rho}\bigg)F\big(|\eta-\sigma|-|\rho|\big)=0,

(8.58)

and

\displaystyle\bigg(\frac{\eta-\sigma}{|\eta-\sigma|}\cdot\nabla_{\eta}+\frac{\rho}{|\rho|}\cdot\nabla_{\rho}\bigg)e^{is\langle\rho\rangle}=is\frac{|\rho|}{\langle\rho\rangle}e^{is\langle\rho\rangle}.

(8.59)

We can then estimate (disregarding faster decaying contributions)

\displaystyle\|K^{h}\|_{Sch}\lesssim 2^{\beta m}2^{-k_{3}}\cdot 2^{-m}2^{-k_{3}}\cdot\|K_{3}\|_{Sch}\cdot 2^{3k_{3}/2}\|\nabla\widetilde{h}\|_{L^{2}}\lesssim 2^{-3m/5}Z.

(8.60)

Remark 8.9

(About the term
A_{1,2}
)
.

Note that the case of the term
A_{1,2}
in (
8.50
)
is simpler since on its support we have
|\eta|\gtrsim 2^{-5\beta m}
and,
therefore,

|\sigma+\rho|\geqslant|\eta|-\big||\sigma+\rho|-|\eta|\big|\gtrsim 2^{-5\beta m}
, whenever we are close to the singularity,
say
||\sigma+\rho|-|\eta||\approx 2^{C}
with
C<-10\beta m
.
Moreover, the factors of
2^{-k_{3}}
in the bounds corresponding to (
8.57
) and (
8.60
)
can be replaced by a harmless
2^{5\beta m}
factor.

To estimate the kernel
K^{g}
we first note that we have
k_{3}\sim 0
,
and we can also insert a cut-off
\varphi_{\sim 0}(\eta-\sigma).

We then localize further introducing a cut-off

\varphi_{\ell}^{(\ell_{0})}(\langle\rho\rangle-2\lambda)
,
with
\ell\geqslant\ell_{0}:=\lfloor-m+\delta m\rfloor
,
\delta>0
small.
We denote the corresponding kernel by
K^{g}_{\ell}
and then estimate similarly to (
8.49
):
for
\ell=\ell_{0}
we have

\displaystyle\|K^{g}_{\ell_{0}}\|_{Sch}

\displaystyle\lesssim 2^{\beta m-k_{3}}\|K_{3}\|_{Sch}\|\varphi_{\leqslant\ell_{0}}(\langle\rho\rangle-2\lambda)\widetilde{g}\|_{L^{1}_{\rho}}

\displaystyle\lesssim 2^{11\beta m}\cdot 2^{\ell_{0}}\cdot 2^{m}\rho(2^{m})\lesssim 2^{-4m/5}2^{m}\rho(2^{m}),

which is sufficient for (
8.56
);
if instead
\ell>\ell_{0}
, we integrate by parts using identities (
8.58
) and (
8.59
) and obtain the same estimate.

8.4.3.
Estimate of
A^{{\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2}}}_{R}

Recall the definition in (
8.40
).
For the term
A^{{\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2}}}_{R}
the measure
\mu^{R}
is smooth (up to small losses);
see (
5.16
) and the pointwise estimates for
\nu_{1}^{R}
in (
5.19
).
Then, we can directly exploit integration by parts in both
\sigma
and
\rho
unless their size is too small.
Since the estimates will be independent of the signs
\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2}
we will drop these indices.

The arguments that follow are similar to those in Section
7
,
see in particular the integration by parts arguments
in the proofs of Lemmas
7.5
,
7.7
and
7.8
(and recall that
K_{3}
satisfies (
8.30
)).

We split again
f=g+h
and denote
A_{R}^{gg},A_{R}^{gh},A_{R}^{hg}
and
A_{R}^{hh}

the bilinear terms of the form (
8.40
) with arguments
(g,g)
,
(g,h)
,
(h,g)
and
(h,h)
respectively.
Also, it suffices to look at the case where
\mu^{R}(\eta,\sigma,\rho)
is replaced by
\nu^{R}_{1}(-\eta+\sigma,\rho)
,
(see (
5.16
)) since the other cases are completely analogous or easier.
Therefore, with the notation in (
8.40
) we identify, for
G,H\in\{g,h\}
,

\displaystyle\begin{split}&A_{R}^{GH}(t,\xi)=\int_{0}^{t}s\,B(s)\,\frac{\xi}{\langle\xi\rangle}\varphi_{k}(\xi)e^{-is(\langle\xi\rangle-\lambda)}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}D_{k_{2},k_{3},k_{4}}^{GH}(s,\eta)\,K_{3;k,k_{1},p}(\xi,\eta)d\eta\,\tau_{m}(s)ds,\\
\hfil\displaystyle\begin{split}D_{k_{2},k_{3},k_{4}}^{GH}(s,\eta):=\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{6}}\langle\sigma\rangle^{-1}e^{is\langle\sigma\rangle}\varphi_{k_{2}}(\sigma)\widetilde{G_{\epsilon_{1}}}(s,\sigma)\,\varphi_{k_{3}}(\rho)\langle\rho\rangle^{-1}e^{is\langle\rho\rangle}\widetilde{H_{\epsilon_{2}}}(s,\rho)\\
\times\varphi_{k_{4}}(\eta-\sigma)\nu_{1}^{R}(-\eta+\sigma,\rho)d\sigma d\rho.\end{split}\end{split}

(8.61)

Once again we have inserted additional localization in frequency
to
|\sigma|\approx 2^{k_{2}},|\rho|\approx 2^{k_{3}}
and
|\eta-\sigma|\approx 2^{k_{4}}
with
k_{2},k_{3},k_{4}\in[-3m,\beta m]
,
and omitted the summation up to a negligible loss of
O(m^{3})
.
Note that we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}{\big\|A_{R}^{GH}(t)\big\|}_{L^{2}_{\xi}}&\lesssim 2^{2m}\rho^{1/2}(2^{m})\cdot 2^{k^{-}}\cdot{\|K_{3;k,k_{1},p}\|}_{Sch}\sup_{s\approx 2^{m}}{\big\|D_{k_{2},k_{3},k_{4}}^{GH}(s)\big\|}_{L^{2}_{\eta}}\\
&\lesssim 2^{3m/2+10\beta m}\sup_{s\approx 2^{m}}{\big\|D_{k_{2},k_{3},k_{4}}^{GH}(s)\big\|}_{L^{2}_{\eta}},\end{split}

(8.62)

and that we can bound

\displaystyle{\big\|D_{k_{2},k_{3},k_{4}}^{GH}(s)\big\|}_{L^{2}_{\eta}}\lesssim{\|\varphi_{k_{2}}\widetilde{G}\|}_{L^{2}}\Big\|\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\frac{\varphi_{k_{3}}(\rho)}{\langle\rho\rangle}e^{is\langle\rho\rangle}\widetilde{H}(s,\rho)\,\varphi_{k_{4}}(\eta-\sigma)\nu_{1}^{R}(-\eta+\sigma,\rho)d\rho\Big\|_{Sch},

(8.63)

\displaystyle{\big\|D_{k_{2},k_{3},k_{4}}^{GH}(s)\big\|}_{L^{2}_{\eta}}\lesssim{\|\varphi_{k_{3}}\widetilde{H}\|}_{L^{2}}\Big\|\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\frac{\varphi_{k_{2}}(\sigma)}{\langle\sigma\rangle}e^{is\langle\sigma\rangle}\widetilde{G}(s,\sigma)\,\varphi_{k_{4}}(\eta-\sigma)\varphi_{\sim k_{3}}(\rho)\nu_{1}^{R}(-\eta+\sigma,\rho)d\sigma\Big\|_{Sch}.

(8.64)

Case 1: Estimate of
A_{R}^{hh}
.
In this case we can proceed similarly to the proof of Lemma
7.8
;
compare the expressions (
7.58
) and (
8.61
).
We fix
\delta>0
small and look at a few subcases.

Subcase 1.1:
k_{2}\vee k_{3}\leqslant-m/2+\delta m
.
In this configuration we use (
5.19
) to see that

\displaystyle{\big\|\varphi_{k_{4}}(x)\varphi_{k_{3}}(y)\nu_{1}^{R}(x,y)\big\|}_{Sch}\lesssim 2^{-2(k_{3}\vee k_{4})}2^{10M_{0}}\cdot 2^{(3/2)(k_{3}+k_{4})}\lesssim 2^{k_{3}\wedge k_{4}}2^{10M_{0}},

(8.65)

and (
8.63
) to obtain

\displaystyle{\|D_{k_{2},k_{3},k_{4}}^{hh}(s)\|}_{L^{2}_{\eta}}

\displaystyle\lesssim{\|\varphi_{k_{2}}\widetilde{h}\|}_{L^{2}}\cdot{\|\varphi_{k_{3}}\widetilde{h}\|}_{L^{1}}\cdot 2^{k_{3}\wedge k_{2}}\cdot 2^{10M_{0}}\lesssim 2^{k_{2}}Z\cdot 2^{5k_{3}/2}Z\cdot 2^{k_{3}\wedge k_{2}}2^{10M_{0}}

which, plugged into (
8.62
) is sufficient for
k_{2},k_{3}\leqslant-m/2+\delta m
with
\delta
small enough.

Subcase 1.2:
k_{2}\vee k_{3}\geqslant-m/2+\delta m
and
k_{2}\wedge k_{3}\leqslant-m/2+\delta m
.
By symmetry we may assume that
k_{2}\leqslant-m/2+\delta m\leqslant k_{3}
.
We then integrate by parts in
\rho
in the formula (
8.61
).
This leads to a main term where
\partial_{\rho}
hits the profile
\widetilde{h}(\rho)
.
Estimating in the same way that led to (
8.63
), we obtain an inequality like the second one in (
8.63
)
with
\widetilde{H}
replaced by
s^{-1}\rho/|\rho|^{2}\cdot\nabla\widetilde{h}(\rho)
;
this shows, using also (
8.65
), that for all
s\approx 2^{m}
,

\displaystyle{\|D_{k_{2},k_{3},k_{4}}^{hh}(s)\|}_{L^{2}_{\eta}}

\displaystyle\lesssim 2^{-m-k_{3}}{\big\|\varphi_{k_{3}}\nabla\widetilde{h}\big\|}_{L^{2}}\Big\|\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\varphi_{k_{2}}(\sigma)\langle\sigma\rangle^{-1}e^{is\langle\sigma\rangle}\widetilde{h}(s,\sigma)\varphi_{k_{4}}(\sigma-\eta)\varphi_{\sim k_{3}}(\rho)\nu_{1}^{R}(-\eta+\sigma,\rho)d\sigma\Big\|_{Sch}

\displaystyle\lesssim 2^{-m-k_{3}}Z\cdot{\|\varphi_{k_{2}}\widetilde{h}\|}_{L^{1}}\cdot{\|\varphi_{k_{4}}(x)\varphi_{k_{3}}(y)\nu_{1}^{R}(x,y)\|}_{Sch}

\displaystyle\lesssim 2^{-m-k_{3}}Z\cdot 2^{5k_{2}/2}Z\cdot 2^{k_{2}}2^{10M_{0}}

which is sufficient.

Subcase 1.3:
k_{2}\wedge k_{3}\geqslant-m/2+\delta m
.
Without loss of generality, we may assume
k_{2}\leqslant k_{3}
.
In this case we can integrate by parts in both
\rho
and
\sigma
and, estimating similarly to the previous cases, we see that

\displaystyle{\|D_{k_{2},k_{3},k_{4}}^{hh}(s)\|}_{L^{2}_{\eta}}

\displaystyle\lesssim 2^{-m-k_{3}}{\big\|\varphi_{k_{3}}\nabla\widetilde{h}\big\|}_{L^{2}}\Big\|\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\varphi_{k_{2}}(\sigma)e^{is\langle\sigma\rangle}\frac{1}{s}\frac{\sigma}{|\sigma|^{2}}\cdot\nabla_{\sigma}\widetilde{h}(s,\sigma)\varphi_{k_{4}}(\sigma-\eta)\varphi_{\sim k_{3}}(\rho)\nu_{1}^{R}(-\eta+\sigma,\rho)d\sigma\Big\|_{Sch}

\displaystyle\lesssim 2^{-m-k_{3}}Z\cdot 2^{-m-k_{2}}{\|\varphi_{k_{2}}\nabla\widetilde{h}\|}_{L^{1}}\cdot{\|\varphi_{k_{4}}(x)\varphi_{k_{3}}(y)\nu_{1}^{R}(x,y)\|}_{Sch}

\displaystyle\lesssim 2^{-m-k_{3}}Z\cdot 2^{-m+k_{2}/2}Z\cdot 2^{k_{3}}2^{10M_{0}}\lesssim Z^{2}2^{-2m+\beta m+10M_{0}}.

Inserting this bound in (
8.62
) gives the desired estimate for
A_{R}^{hh}
.

Case 2: Estimate of
A_{R}^{gg}
.

The case of
(g,g)
interactions is easier than the previous one since, on the support of (
8.61
),
when
G=H=g
, we must have
|\sigma|,|\rho|\approx 1
.
In particular,
\nu_{1}^{R}
is pointwise upper bounded by
2^{10M_{0}}
.

We have, using Schur’s test, and Hölder with
|\eta|\lesssim 2^{\beta m}
,

\displaystyle\begin{split}&{\big\|A_{R}^{gg}(t)\big\|}_{L^{2}}=\Big\|\int_{0}^{t}sB(s)\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{9}}e^{-is\Phi_{1}}\,\widetilde{g}(\sigma)\widetilde{g}(\rho)\,\varphi_{\sim 0}(\sigma)\varphi_{\sim 0}(\rho)\\
&\times\varphi_{k_{4}}(\eta-\sigma)\nu_{1}^{R}(-\eta+\sigma,\rho)d\sigma d\rho\,K_{3;k,k_{1},p}(\xi,\eta)d\eta\,\tau_{m}(s)ds\Big\|_{L^{2}}\\
&\lesssim 2^{2m}\rho(2^{m})^{1/2}\cdot{\|K_{3;k,k_{1},p}\|}_{Sch}\cdot 2^{(3/2)\beta m}\\
&\times\sup_{s\approx 2^{m}}\sup_{|\eta|\lesssim 2^{\beta m}}\Big|\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{6}}\,e^{is\langle\sigma\rangle}\widetilde{g}(\sigma)\,e^{is\langle\rho\rangle}\widetilde{g}(\rho)\,\varphi_{\sim 0}(\sigma)\varphi_{\sim 0}(\rho)\varphi_{k_{4}}(\eta-\sigma)\nu_{1}^{R}(-\eta+\sigma,\rho)d\sigma d\rho\Big|.\end{split}

(8.66)

Then, in view of (
8.30
), it suffices to show that the quantity on the last line above has
an upper bound by, say,
2^{-5m/3}
.
To see that this is the case, we insert cutoffs in the size of
\langle\sigma\rangle-2\lambda
and
\langle\rho\rangle-2\lambda
,
and aim to bound

\displaystyle\begin{split}\psi_{\ell_{2},\ell_{3}}(\eta)&:=\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{6}}e^{is\langle\sigma\rangle}(\varphi_{\sim 0}\widetilde{g})(\sigma)e^{is\langle\rho\rangle}(\varphi_{\sim 0}\widetilde{g})(\rho)\,\varphi_{\ell_{2}}^{(\ell_{0})}(\langle\sigma\rangle-2\lambda)\,\\
&\times\varphi_{\ell_{3}}^{(\ell_{0})}(\langle\rho\rangle-2\lambda)\varphi_{k_{4}}(\eta-\sigma)\nu_{1}^{R}(-\eta+\sigma,\rho)d\sigma d\rho,\qquad\ell_{0}:=\lfloor-m+\delta m\rfloor,\end{split}

(8.67)

with
\delta>0
sufficiently small.

In the case
\ell_{2}=\ell_{3}=\ell_{0}
we use (
4.24
), and integrate directly to get

\displaystyle{\|\psi_{\ell_{2},\ell_{3}}\|}_{L^{\infty}_{\eta}}

\displaystyle\lesssim 2^{\ell_{0}}{\|\widetilde{g}\|}_{L^{\infty}}\cdot 2^{\ell_{0}}{\|\widetilde{g}\|}_{L^{\infty}}\cdot 2^{10M_{0}}

\displaystyle\lesssim 2^{-2m+2\delta m+10M_{0}}\big(2^{m}\rho(2^{m})\big)^{2},

which suffices for
\delta
small enough.

If
\ell_{2},\ell_{3}>\ell_{0}
we can resort to integration by parts in both
\rho
and
\sigma

in the formula (
8.67
), and estimate, up to faster decaying terms,

\displaystyle\begin{split}\big|\psi_{\ell_{2},\ell_{3}}(\eta)\big|&\lesssim 2^{-m}{\|\varphi_{\ell_{2}}\nabla_{\sigma}\widetilde{g}\|}_{L^{1}}\cdot 2^{-m}{\|\varphi_{\ell_{3}}\nabla_{\rho}\widetilde{g}\|}_{L^{1}}\cdot 2^{10M_{0}}\\
&\lesssim 2^{-2m+10M_{0}}\big(m2^{m}\rho(2^{m})\big)^{2},\end{split}

having used (
4.27
).

The cases
\ell_{3}=\ell_{0}<\ell_{2}
and
\ell_{2}=\ell_{0}<\ell_{3}
can be treated similarly using direct
integration in the variable corresponding to the index
\ell_{0}
,
and integration by parts in the other.

Case 3: Estimate of
A_{R}^{gh}
and
A_{R}^{hg}
.

These terms are all treated using strategies already employed in previous two cases,
and we only give the argument for the slightly worse case of
A_{R}^{gh}
.
We start by looking at the formula (
8.61
) with
G=g
and
H=h
, and introduce
a cut-off
\varphi_{\ell_{2}}^{(\ell_{0})}(\langle\sigma\rangle-2\lambda)
, with the usual choice
\ell_{0}=\lfloor-m+\delta m\rfloor
.

Subcase 1.1:
k_{3}\leqslant-m/2+\delta m
.
In this case we use first (
8.62
) and then (
8.64
) to obtain

\displaystyle{\big\|A_{R}^{gh}(t)\big\|}_{L^{2}_{\xi}}

\displaystyle\lesssim 2^{3m/2+10\beta m}\sup_{s\approx 2^{m}}{\big\|D_{k_{2},k_{3},k_{4}}^{gh}(s)\big\|}_{L^{2}_{\eta}}

\displaystyle\lesssim 2^{3m/2+10\beta m}\cdot{\|\varphi_{k_{3}}\widetilde{h}\|}_{L^{2}}

(8.68)

\displaystyle\times{\Big\|\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\frac{\varphi_{k_{2}}(\sigma)}{\langle\sigma\rangle}e^{is\langle\sigma\rangle}\widetilde{g}(\sigma)\,\varphi_{\ell_{2}}^{(\ell_{0})}(\langle\sigma\rangle-2\lambda)\varphi_{k_{4}}(\eta-\sigma)\varphi_{k_{3}}(\rho)\nu_{1}^{R}(-\eta+\sigma,\rho)d\sigma\Big\|}_{Sch}.

For the Schur norm in (
8.68
), resorting to integration by parts if
\ell_{2}>\ell_{0}
,
and using (
4.27
) and (
4.24
), we have a bound of

\displaystyle C2^{-m+\delta m}m\rho(2^{m})2^{m}\cdot{\big\|\varphi_{k_{4}}(x)\varphi_{k_{3}}(y)\nu_{1}^{R}(x,y)\big\|}_{Sch}\lesssim 2^{-m+2\delta m}\cdot 2^{k_{3}}.

(8.69)

Using this and
{\|\varphi_{k_{3}}\widetilde{h}\|}_{L^{2}}\lesssim 2^{k_{3}}Z
in (
8.69
)
gives a sufficient bound, since
k_{3}\leqslant-m/2+\delta m
.

Subcase 1.2:
k_{3}\geqslant-m/2+\delta m
.
In this case we can proceed as above, but first integrate by parts in
\rho

(the frequency variable of
\widetilde{h}
) in the formula for
D^{gh}
in (
8.61
).
Then we can bound using (
8.62
) and (
8.64
) as done in (
8.68
) above,
and obtain the same estimate but with

2^{-m-k_{3}}{\|\varphi_{k_{3}}\nabla\widetilde{h}\|}_{L^{2}}
instead of
{\|\varphi_{k_{3}}\widetilde{h}\|}_{L^{2}}
;
since
2^{-m-k_{3}}{\|\varphi_{k_{3}}\nabla\widetilde{h}\|}_{L^{2}}\lesssim 2^{-m/2-\delta m}Z
in the present case,
we can then conclude as above.

8.4.4.
Estimate of
A^{{\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2}}}_{Re}

The estimates for
A^{{\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2}}}_{Re}
are substantially easier than those for
A^{{\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2}}}_{R}
.
Looking at the formula (
8.40
), and comparing with (
8.61
),
we just need to replace
\mu^{Re}(\eta,\sigma,\rho)
for
\nu_{1}^{R}(-\eta+\sigma,\rho)
in (
8.61
).
Then we recall that, in view of the assumptions of Proposition
8.7
,
on the support of the integral we must have
|\eta|\gtrsim 2^{-5\beta m}
;
therefore,
\max\{|\eta|,|\sigma|,|\rho|\}\gtrsim 2^{-5\beta m},

and in view of (
5.24
), we have a negligible loss coming from the measure.
Therefore we can follow the same arguments in §
8.4.3
above,
based on integration by parts in frequency. We skip the details.
∎

8.5.
Estimate of
II_{m,k,k_{1}}(G),G\in\{g,h\}

We prove the following:

Lemma 8.10
.

Under the a priori assumptions of Proposition
4.2
,
and with the definition (
8.7
), we have, for all
m=0,1,\dots
,

\displaystyle\sum_{k,k_{1}\in{\mathbb{Z}}}{\big\|II_{m,k,k_{1}}(G)\big\|}_{L^{2}}\lesssim 2^{m}\rho(2^{m})^{1-\beta+\delta}{\varepsilon}^{\beta},\qquad G\in\{g,h\}

(8.70)

for some
\delta\in(0,\beta/2)
.

Proof.

To show estimates on
II_{m,k,k_{1}}
we first consider a slightly more general term of the form

\displaystyle III_{m,k,k_{1}}:=\varphi_{k}(\xi)\int_{0}^{t}B(s)\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}e^{-is\Phi(\xi,\eta)}\varphi_{k_{1}}(\eta)\,\langle\eta\rangle^{-1}\widetilde{G}(s,\eta)\nu^{\prime}(\xi,\eta)d\eta\,\tau_{m}(s)ds,\qquad G\in\{g,h\},

for some
\nu^{\prime}
, and establish some general bounds for it.

First, we write that

\displaystyle\|III_{m,k,k_{1}}(t,\xi)\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim 2^{m}\rho(2^{m})^{1/2}\cdot 2^{3k/2}\cdot\sup_{s\approx 2^{m}}\sup_{|\xi|\approx 2^{k}}\bigg|\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}e^{-is\Phi(\xi,\eta)}\varphi_{k_{1}}(\eta)\,\langle\eta\rangle^{-1}\widetilde{G}(s,\eta)\nu^{\prime}(\xi,\eta)d\eta\bigg|

\displaystyle\lesssim 2^{m}\rho(2^{m})^{1/2}\cdot 2^{3k/2}\|\varphi_{k_{1}}(\eta)\widetilde{G}(\eta)\|_{L^{1}_{\eta}}\sup_{|\xi|\approx 2^{k},|\eta|\approx 2^{k_{1}}}|\nu^{\prime}(\xi,\eta)|.

(8.71)

Second, we can integrate by parts in
\eta
and obtain

\displaystyle\|III_{m,k,k_{1}}\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|III_{m,k,k_{1}}^{(1)}\|_{L^{2}}+\|III_{m,k,k_{1}}^{(2)}\|_{L^{2}},

\displaystyle III_{m,k,k_{1}}^{(1)}

\displaystyle:=\varphi_{k}(\xi)\int_{0}^{t}\tau_{m}(s)s^{-1}B(s)\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\big(\frac{\eta}{|\eta|^{2}}\cdot\nabla\big)\widetilde{G}(s,\eta)\nu^{\prime}(\xi,\eta)\varphi_{k_{1}}(\eta)d\eta ds,

\displaystyle III_{m,k,k_{1}}^{(2)}

\displaystyle:=\varphi_{k}(\xi)\int_{0}^{t}\tau_{m}(s)s^{-1}B(s)\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\widetilde{G}(s,\eta)\textrm{div}\bigg(\frac{\eta}{|\eta|^{2}}\nu^{\prime}(\xi,\eta)\varphi_{k_{1}}(\eta)\bigg)d\eta ds.

Therefore we can write as above that

\displaystyle{\|III_{m,k,k_{1}}^{(1)}\|}_{L^{2}}\lesssim\rho(2^{m})^{1/2}\bigg\|\varphi_{k_{1}}(\eta)\frac{\nabla\widetilde{G}(s,\eta)}{|\eta|}\bigg\|_{L^{1}_{\eta}}\sup_{|\xi|\approx 2^{k},|\eta|\approx 2^{k_{1}}}|\nu^{\prime}(\xi,\eta)|.

(8.72)

In the same vein, we can write that

\displaystyle\begin{split}\|III^{(2)}_{m,k,k_{1}}(t,\xi)\|_{L^{2}}&\lesssim\rho(2^{m})^{1/2}2^{3k/2}\max\bigg(\sup_{|\xi|\approx 2^{k},|\eta|\approx 2^{k_{1}}}|\nabla_{\eta}\nu^{\prime}(\xi,\eta)|,\,2^{-k_{1}}\sup_{|\xi|\approx 2^{k},|\eta|\approx 2^{k_{1}}}|\nu^{\prime}(\xi,\eta)|\bigg)\\
&\times\bigg\|\varphi_{k_{1}}(\eta)\frac{\widetilde{G}(s,\eta)}{|\eta|}\bigg\|_{L^{1}_{\eta}}.\end{split}

(8.73)

We need one more ingredient to deal with large outputs
|\xi|
.
Notice that

\displaystyle-\Delta_{x}\big(\partial_{\xi}\psi(x,\xi)\big)+V\partial_{\xi}\psi(x,\xi)=2\xi\psi(x,\xi)+|\xi|^{2}\partial_{\xi}\psi(x,\xi),

so that, integrating by parts in
x
, we obtain

\displaystyle\begin{split}&|\xi|^{2}\partial_{\xi}\nu(\xi,\eta)\\
&=-\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\Delta\big(\partial_{\xi}\overline{\psi(x,\xi)}\big)\psi(x,\eta)\phi(x)dx+\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\partial_{\xi}\overline{\psi(x,\xi)}\psi(x,\eta)V(x)\phi(x)dx-2\xi\nu(\xi,\eta)\\
&=-\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\partial_{\xi}\overline{\psi(x,\xi)}\Delta\big(\psi(x,\eta)\phi(x)\big)dx+\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\partial_{\xi}\overline{\psi(x,\xi)}\psi(x,\eta)V(x)\phi(x)dx-2\xi\nu(\xi,\eta)\\
&:=\nu_{1}(\xi,\eta)+\nu_{2}(\xi,\eta)+\nu_{3}(\xi,\eta).\end{split}

(8.74)

We are now ready to estimate
II_{m,k,k_{1}}(G),G\in\{g,h\}
.
We distinguish two main cases, depending on whether the input is
g
or
h.

Case
G=h
.
We distinguish two main cases depending on the relation between
k
and
k_{1}
.

Case 1:
k<k_{1}
.
We look at two subcases.

Subcase 1.1:
k_{1}>10m/N
or
k_{1}\leqslant-5m
.
Using (
8.71
) with
\nu^{\prime}=\partial_{\xi}\nu

together with (
8.6
) as well as the bootstrap assumption on the
H^{N}
norm (
1.47
), we find

\displaystyle\|II_{m,k,k_{1}}(t,\xi)\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim 2^{m}\rho(2^{m})^{1/2}\cdot 2^{3k/2}2^{3k_{1}/2}\|\varphi_{k_{1}}(\eta)\widetilde{h}(s,\eta)\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim 2^{m}\rho(2^{m})^{1/2}\cdot 2^{3k/2}2^{3k_{1}/2}\cdot\big(2^{-Nk_{1}^{+}/2}\wedge 2^{k_{1}}\big)\cdot Z,

which allows us to conclude in this case.

Case 1.2:
-5m<k_{1}\leqslant 10m/N
.
Integrating by parts in the formula (
8.7
) for
II_{m\color[rgb]{1,0,0.7},k\color[rgb]{0,0,0},k_{1}}
,
and using (
8.72
) and (
8.73
) with
\nu^{\prime}=\partial_{\xi}\nu
together with (
8.6
), we find

\displaystyle\|II_{m,k,k_{1}}(t,\xi)\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\rho(2^{m})^{1/2}\cdot 2^{3k/2}\cdot 2^{-k_{1}}\cdot\big(2^{-k_{1}}{\|\varphi_{\sim k_{1}}(\eta)\|}_{L^{6/5}}{\|\widetilde{h}(\eta)\|}_{L^{6}}+{\|\varphi_{k_{1}}\nabla\widetilde{h}(\eta)\|}_{L^{1}}\big)

\displaystyle\lesssim\rho(2^{m})^{1/2}2^{3k/2}2^{k_{1}/2}\cdot Z,

which is enough to conclude in this case.

Case 2:
k\geqslant k_{1}
. In this case we also distinguish two subcases between medium and other frequencies.

Case 2.1:
k_{1}>10m/N
or
k_{1}\leqslant-5m
.
In this case we consider
|\xi|^{2}II_{m,k,k_{1}}
; using (
8.74
) we have three terms to estimate,
corresponding to
\nu_{i},\,i=1,2,3
, which we denote by
|\xi|^{2}II_{m,k,k_{1}}^{(i)}
.
We write, using (
8.5
), (
8.6
) and (
8.71
)

\displaystyle\big\||\xi|^{2}II_{m,k,k_{1}}^{(i)}(t,\xi)\big\|_{L^{2}}\lesssim 2^{m}\rho(2^{m})^{1/2}2^{3k/2}2^{3k_{1}/2}\big(2^{-(N/2)k_{1}^{+}}\wedge 2^{k_{1}}\big)2^{2k_{1}^{+}}\cdot Z,\qquad i=1,2.

For
i=3,
we can bound using Plancherel similarly to (
8.16
)

\displaystyle\big\||\xi|^{2}II_{m,k,k_{1}}^{(3)}(t,\xi)\big\|_{L^{2}}\lesssim 2^{m}\rho(2^{m})^{1/2}\cdot 2^{k}\cdot 2^{-(N/2)k^{+}}\cdot\big(2^{-(N/2)k_{1}^{+}}\wedge 2^{k_{1}}\big)\cdot Z.

Putting the above two estimates together with an
L^{2}
-bound from (
8.71
), we conclude that

\displaystyle\|II_{m,k,k_{1}}^{(i)}\|_{L^{2}}\lesssim 2^{-2k^{+}}\cdot 2^{m}\rho(2^{m})^{1/2}\cdot\big(2^{-(N/2)k_{1}^{+}}\wedge 2^{k_{1}}\big)\big(2^{3k/2+3k_{1}/2}2^{2k_{1}^{+}}+2^{k}2^{-(N/2)k^{+}}\big)\cdot Z.

Given our assumptions on
k,k_{1}
this is sufficient to conclude.

Case 2.2:
-5m<k_{1}\leqslant 10m/N
.
We make use of (
8.72
), (
8.73
) with
\nu^{\prime}=\nu_{i},i=1,2
to write that

\displaystyle\||\xi|^{2}II_{m,k,k_{1}}^{(i)}\|_{L^{2}}\lesssim\rho(2^{m})^{1/2}\cdot 2^{3k/2}2^{k_{1}/2}2^{2k_{1}^{+}}\cdot Z.

For
i=3,
we use the Strichartz estimate (Lemma
2.6
)
with parameters
(\widetilde{q},\widetilde{r},\gamma)=(2,\infty,0)
and (
4.19
) to obtain

\displaystyle\||\xi|^{2}II_{m,k,k_{1}}^{(3)}\|_{L^{2}}\lesssim 2^{k}\cdot 2^{m/2}\rho(2^{m})^{1/2}\cdot 2^{-k_{1}^{+}}\|P_{k_{1}}e^{isL}h\|_{L^{6}_{x}}\lesssim 2^{k}\cdot 2^{-m+10\delta_{N}m}\cdot Z.

The desired bound follows in all cases.

Case
G=g
.

Note that in this case
k_{1}\sim 0,
therefore we will disregard the sum over that index.
We insert cutoffs
\varphi_{\ell}^{(\ell_{0})}(\langle\xi\rangle-2\lambda),\ell_{0}:=\lfloor-m+\delta m\rfloor,

where
\delta>0
is chosen suitably small.

Case 1:
k<m/10
.
If
\ell>\ell_{0}
we integrate by parts in
\eta
in the formula (
8.7
).
Using (
8.72
), (
8.73
), as well as (
4.27
) and (
4.24
), we find

\displaystyle\|II_{m,k}(t,\xi)\|_{L^{2}_{\xi}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\rho(2^{m})^{1/2}\cdot 2^{3k/2}\big(\|\varphi_{\ell}(\langle\xi\rangle-2\lambda)\nabla_{\eta}\widetilde{g}(s,\eta)\|_{L^{1}_{\eta}}+{\|\widetilde{g}\|}_{L^{1}}\big)

\displaystyle\lesssim\rho(2^{m})^{1/2}\cdot 2^{3m/20}\cdot 2^{m}\rho(2^{m})m^{2},

which suffices.
A similar estimate holds if
\ell=\ell_{0},
integrating directly using (
4.24
).

Case 2:
k\geqslant m/10
.
In this case we use the identity (
8.74
) and the same notation in Case 2.1 above.
As above we integrate by parts in frequency if
\ell>\ell_{0}
and integrate directly otherwise.
Using (
8.72
), (
8.73
) as well as (
4.27
) and (
4.24
), we find, for
i=1,2
,

\displaystyle\big\||\xi|^{2}II_{m,k}^{(i)}\big\|_{L^{2}_{\xi}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\rho(2^{m})^{1/2}\cdot 2^{3k/2}\big(\|\varphi_{\ell}(\langle\xi\rangle-2\lambda)\nabla_{\eta}\widetilde{g}(s,\eta)\|_{L^{1}_{\eta}}+{\|\widetilde{g}\|}_{L^{\infty}}\big)

\displaystyle\lesssim\rho(2^{m})^{1/2}\cdot 2^{3k/2}(2^{m}\rho(2^{m})m^{2}).

For the last case, we can conclude as we did for the profile
h
above using Strichartz
estimates as well as (
4.17
):

\displaystyle\big\||\xi|^{2}II_{m,k}^{(3)}(t,\xi)\big\|_{L^{2}_{\xi}}\lesssim 2^{k}\cdot 2^{m/2}\rho(2^{m})^{1/2}\|e^{itL}g\|_{L^{6}_{x}}\lesssim 2^{k}\cdot\rho(2^{m})m.

Upon dividing by
2^{2k}
and summing over
k\geqslant m/10
, the bound above is more than sufficient to conclude.
∎

8.6.
The remainder terms

Here we explain how to estimate the remainder terms of mixed type
RM
in (
4.9
);
since these are easier to treat compared to
M_{1}
and
M_{2}
above, we will leave some details to the reader.

The term (
4.9a
)

Using (
3.34
), the term (
4.9a
) can be written as

\displaystyle RM_{1}

\displaystyle=RM_{1,1}+RM_{1,2}+RM_{1,3},

\displaystyle RM_{1,1}

\displaystyle:=\int_{0}^{t}\overline{B(s)}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}e^{-is(\langle\xi\rangle-\langle\eta\rangle+\lambda)}\langle\eta\rangle^{-1}\widetilde{h}(s,\eta)\nu(\xi,\eta)d\eta\,ds,

\displaystyle RM_{1,2}

\displaystyle:=\int_{0}^{t}\overline{B(s)}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}e^{-is(\langle\xi\rangle-\langle\eta\rangle+\lambda)}\langle\eta\rangle^{-1}\widetilde{g}(s,\eta)\nu(\xi,\eta)d\eta\,ds,

\displaystyle RM_{1,3}

\displaystyle:=\int_{0}^{t}\big(\overline{A(s)}-\overline{B(s)}\big)\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}e^{-is(\langle\xi\rangle-\langle\eta\rangle+\lambda)}\langle\eta\rangle^{-1}\widetilde{f}(s,\eta)\nu(\xi,\eta)d\eta\,ds.

The term
RM_{1,1}
is similar to the term
M_{1}
treated in subsections above,
where the only difference is the opposite sign of the oscillation
of the amplitude of the internal mode
e^{-is\lambda}
.
The arguments above are clearly insensitive to this change,
and we can then apply the same estimates verbatim.
The starting point is the analogue of (
8.24
)
with the phase now given by
\Phi(\xi,\eta)=\langle\xi\rangle+\lambda-\langle\eta\rangle
.
For example, the contributions corresponding to (
8.40
)
now have phases
\langle\xi\rangle+\lambda-\epsilon_{1}\langle\sigma\rangle-\epsilon_{2}\langle\rho\rangle
.
This does not impact the estimates of the term (
8.42
) since (
8.45
)
still holds by changing the sign of
\lambda

(we are restricting to
|\langle\xi\rangle+\lambda-\langle\eta\rangle|\approx 2^{p}
in this case).
The same goes for (
8.50
) and (
8.54
).

The term
RM_{1,2}
enjoys the same estimates satisfied by
M_{2}
,
which are based on Strichartz estimates and are not affected by the different sign of the oscillation
of the discrete component.

Using
A-B=O(A^{2})
, we see that
RM_{1,3}
has the form

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}O(A^{2}(s))\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}e^{-is(\langle\xi\rangle-\langle\eta\rangle-\lambda)}\langle\eta\rangle^{-1}\widetilde{f}(s,\eta)\nu(\xi,\eta)d\eta ds.

(8.75)

After taking a
\partial_{\xi}
derivative at the cost of adding an
s
factor,
we can directly apply the usual Strichartz estimate from Lemma
2.6
,
as in the estimate of
M_{2}
,
and use the
L^{6}_{x}
a priori decay estimate for
e^{itL}f
, see Lemma
4.4
,
and
O(A^{2}(s))=\langle s\rangle^{-1}
, to get

\displaystyle\big\|\partial_{\xi}RM_{1,3}\big\|_{L^{2}}\lesssim\sum_{m}\|sA^{2}(s)\|_{L^{2}_{s\approx 2^{m}}}\sup_{s\approx 2^{m}}\|\phi\,e^{isL}f\|_{L^{1}}\lesssim\sum_{m}2^{m/2}\cdot 2^{(-1+\beta+\delta_{N})m}\varepsilon^{\beta}\lesssim 1.

The term (
4.9b
)

Writing (
4.9b
) in terms of
A
gives expressions of the form

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}A_{\epsilon_{1}}(s)\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}e^{-is(\langle\xi\rangle-\epsilon_{1}\lambda+\langle\eta\rangle)}\langle\eta\rangle^{-1}\overline{\widetilde{f}}(s,\eta)\nu(\xi,\eta)d\eta ds,

and we see that the oscillating phase is lower bounded by
\max(\langle\eta\rangle,\langle\xi\rangle)
.
Then this term is clearly easier to bound than
M_{1}
and
M_{2}
above.

The term (
4.9c
)

This is almost identical to the term
RM_{1,3}
in (
8.75
), and can be handled
as
above.

9.
Energy estimates

In this section we first prove energy estimates for
h
,
along with the
H^{N}
scattering statement (
1.32
) in Theorem
1.2
.
Then we prove Lemma
5.4
about control of the weighted norm for high frequencies.

9.1.
Sobolev norms and scattering

We prove the following:

Proposition 9.1
.

Under the a priori assumption of Proposition
4.2
, there exists
\delta>0

such that, for any
1\leqslant t_{1}<t_{2}\leqslant T
we have

\displaystyle{\|h(t_{1})-h(t_{2})\|}_{H^{N}}\lesssim{\varepsilon}^{1+\delta}\,t_{1}^{-\delta}\color[rgb]{1,0,0.7}.\color[rgb]{0,0,0}

(9.1)

The above proposition implies the conclusion (
4.14
) for the
H^{N}
norm,
as well as scattering in
H^{N}
for
h(t)
.
Recall that
h
satisfies (
4.6
).
In the next two subsections, we will then estimate the
H^{N}
norm of the terms appearing in (
4.6
).

9.1.1.
Source terms

We start by inserting the usual
\tau_{m}
cutoffs (see (
1.74
)) in the time integral defining
the source terms (
4.7
),
and denote
S_{i}^{m}
,
i=1,2,3
the corresponding terms.
The following lemma gives an estimate consistent with (
9.1
) for these source terms.

Lemma 9.2
.

Under the assumptions of Proposition
4.2
,
there exists
\delta>0
such that, for all
m=0,1,\dots
, we have, for
i=1,2,3,

\displaystyle\|S_{i}^{m}\|_{H^{N}}\lesssim 2^{-\delta m}{\varepsilon}^{1+\delta}.

(9.2)

Proof.

We first look at

\displaystyle S_{1}^{m}(t,\xi):=\int_{0}^{t}e^{-is(\langle\xi\rangle-2\lambda)}(A^{2}(s)-B^{2}(s))\tau_{m}(s)ds\,\widetilde{\theta}(\xi),

see (
4.7a
),
and split it as

\displaystyle S_{1}^{m}=S_{-}^{m}+S_{+}^{m},\qquad\widetilde{S_{-}^{m}}(t,\xi):=\varphi_{\leqslant-m/10}(\langle\xi\rangle-2\lambda)\widetilde{S_{1}^{m}}(t,\xi).

We then estimate directly by Hölder and (
3.34
)

\displaystyle{\|S_{-}^{m}(t)\|}_{H^{N}_{x}}\lesssim 2^{-m/20}{\|\widetilde{S_{1}^{m}}\|}_{L^{\infty}}\lesssim 2^{-m/20}\cdot 2^{m}\rho^{3/2}(2^{m}),

which suffices since
\rho\lesssim{\varepsilon}^{2}
.
For
S_{+}^{m}
we instead integrate by parts in
s
to obtain

\displaystyle{\big\|S_{+}^{m}(t)\big\|}_{H^{N}_{x}}

\displaystyle\lesssim{\Big\|\frac{\varphi_{>-m/10}(\langle\xi\rangle-2\lambda)}{\langle\xi\rangle-2\lambda}\Big|\int_{0}^{t}e^{-is(\langle\xi\rangle-2\lambda)}\frac{d}{ds}\Big[(A^{2}(s)-B^{2}(s))\tau_{m}(s)\Big]ds\,\langle\xi\rangle^{N}\widetilde{\theta}(\xi)\Big|\Big\|}_{L^{2}_{\xi}}

\displaystyle\lesssim 2^{m/20}2^{m}\rho^{2}(2^{m}),

which is also sufficient.
For
S_{2}^{m}
, see (
4.7b
), we can also integrate by parts at no cost since
|\langle\xi\rangle-2\lambda|\gtrsim 1
,
and then use (
3.34
) and (
3.5
) to obtain an even stronger bound:

\displaystyle{\|S_{2}^{m}(t)\|}_{H^{N}_{x}}\lesssim 2^{m}\rho^{2}(2^{m}).

The same arguments apply to the two terms in (
4.7c
). Looking at the first one (the other can be treated identically) we write

\displaystyle S_{3}^{m}

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{t}e^{-is\langle\xi\rangle}\big(|A|^{2}(s)-|B|^{2}(s)\big)\,\tau_{m}(s)ds\widetilde{\theta}(\xi)+\int_{0}^{t}e^{-is\langle\xi\rangle}|B|^{2}(s)\,\tau_{m}(s)ds\widetilde{\theta}(\xi)

and then proceed as for
S_{1}^{m}
to deal with the first of the two terms above (this is in fact even easier
since the phase does not vanish), and just integrate by parts, as we did for
S_{2}^{m}
, to deal with the second.
∎

9.1.2.
Mixed terms

As in the previous paragraph, we localize the mixed terms (
4.8
)-(
4.9
) in time by inserting time cutoffs.
We denote the corresponding terms
M_{i}^{m},i=1,2
and
RM_{i}^{m},i=1,2,3
respectively. We prove a similar result as in the previous subsection:

Lemma 9.3
.

We have for
m=0,1,\dots

\displaystyle\|(M_{1}^{m}-M_{2}^{m})\|_{H^{N}}\lesssim 2^{-\delta m}{\varepsilon}^{1+\delta},

\displaystyle\|RM_{i}^{m}\|_{H^{N}}\lesssim 2^{-\delta m}{\varepsilon}^{1+\delta},\quad i=1,2,3,

for some
\delta>0.

Proof.

We first show how to bound the
H^{N}
norm of the (time-localized) mixed term

\displaystyle(M_{1}^{m}-M_{2}^{m})(t)=\int_{0}^{t}e^{-isL}e^{-is\lambda}B(s)\big(\phi(x)\,L^{-1}\Im w(s)\big)\,\tau_{m}(s)ds,\quad m=0,1,2,\dots.

(9.3)

Recall that
f=h-g
and
e^{itL}f=w=(\partial_{t}+iL)v
;
note also that with the same argument below we could estimate
M_{1}^{m}
and
M_{2}^{m}
separately as well.
The idea here is to use Strichartz estimates and the gain of one derivative on
w

- due to the ‘strongly semilinear’ nature of (
1.6
) -
followed by interpolation through the Gagliardo-Nirenberg inequality for
1\leqslant N_{1}\leqslant N

\displaystyle\|w\|_{H^{N_{1}}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|w\|_{H^{N}}^{\frac{N_{1}-1}{N-1}}\|w\|_{L^{6}}^{\frac{N-N_{1}}{N-1}}\lesssim\varepsilon^{\frac{N_{1}-1}{N-1}}\cdot\rho(2^{m})^{(1-\beta)\frac{N-N_{1}}{N-1}}2^{\frac{5}{3}m\delta_{N}\frac{N-N_{1}}{N-1}}\varepsilon^{\beta\frac{N-N_{1}}{N-1}},

(9.4)

where we used (
4.19
) for the last part of the inequality.

Relying on the boundedness of wave operators, followed by Lemma
2.6
, and using (
9.4
),
we find

\displaystyle{\|(M_{1}^{m}-M_{2}^{m})(t)\|}_{H^{N}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{n_{1}+n_{2}\leqslant N}{\Big\|\int_{0}^{t}B(s)e^{-isL}\big(D^{n_{2}}\phi(x)\cdot L^{-1}D^{n_{1}}\Im w(s)\big)\,\tau_{m}(s)ds\Big\|}_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{n_{1}+n_{2}\leqslant N}{\Big\|B(s)\tau_{m}(s)\,\langle D\rangle^{n_{2}}\phi\,\cdot\langle D\rangle^{n_{1}-1}\Im w(s)\Big\|}_{L^{2}_{t}L^{1}_{x}}

\displaystyle\lesssim{\|B(s)\|}_{L^{2}_{s\approx 2^{m}}}\cdot\sup_{s\approx 2^{m}}{\|w(s)\|}_{H^{N-1}_{x}}

\displaystyle\lesssim{\|B(s)\|}_{L^{2}_{s\approx 2^{m}}}\cdot{\varepsilon}^{\frac{N-2}{N-1}}\cdot\Big(\rho(2^{m})^{1-\beta}2^{\frac{5}{3}\delta_{N}m}{\varepsilon}^{\beta}\Big)^{\frac{1}{N-1}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\big(2^{m}\rho(2^{m})\big)^{1/2}\cdot{\varepsilon}^{\frac{N-2}{N-1}}\cdot{\varepsilon}^{\frac{1-\beta}{N-1}}2^{-\frac{1-\beta}{2(N-1)}m}2^{\frac{5\delta_{N}}{3(N-1)}m}{\varepsilon}^{\frac{\beta}{N-1}}

\displaystyle\lesssim{\varepsilon}\cdot 2^{-\frac{1-\beta}{2(N-1)}m}2^{\frac{5\delta_{N}}{3(N-1)}m},

where we used
\rho(t)\leqslant{\varepsilon}t^{-1/2}
for the line before last.
Since
(1-\beta)/2>5\delta_{N}/3
, this concludes the proof of the first estimate.

The remainder terms can be treated following the same strategy decomposing

A=A-B+B=O(\rho^{1/2})
(recall (
3.34
)), therefore we can skip the details.
∎

9.1.3.
Field interactions

Finally, we look at the nonlinear interactions that are quadratic in the field
w
,
and show the following:

Lemma 9.4
.

Under the a priori assumptions of Proposition
4.2
, we have for
m=0,1,\dots

\displaystyle\|F_{m}\|_{H^{N}}

\displaystyle\lesssim 2^{-\delta m}{\varepsilon}^{1+\delta},\qquad F_{m}:=\int_{0}^{t}\tau_{m}(s)e^{-isL}\big(L^{-1}\Im w(s)\big)^{2}ds,

(9.5)

for some
\delta>0.

Proof.

Using the boundedness of wave operators and distributing the derivatives, it suffices to show that

\displaystyle\begin{split}{\Big\|\int_{0}^{t}e^{-isL}L^{N}\big(L^{-1}\Im w(s)\big)\big(L^{-1}\Im w(s)\big)\,\tau_{m}(s)ds\Big\|}_{L^{2}}\lesssim 2^{-\delta m}\varepsilon^{1+\delta}\color[rgb]{1,0,0.7}.\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\end{split}

(9.6)

Using Lemma
2.6
with
(\tilde{q},\tilde{r},\gamma)=(6,3,2/3)
,
together with the boundedness of wave operators and a Sobolev product estimate,
we have that the right-hand side of (
9.6
) is bounded by

\displaystyle\begin{split}&C\sum_{k\geqslant 0}2^{-k/3}{\big\|P_{k}L\big[L^{N}\big(L^{-1}\Im w\big)\big(L^{-1}\Im w\big)\big]\big\|}_{L^{6/5}_{s\approx 2^{m}}L_{x}^{3/2}}\\
&\lesssim{\big\|w\big\|}_{L^{6/5}_{s\approx 2^{m}}L^{6}_{x}}{\big\|L^{N}w\big\|}_{L^{\infty}_{s\approx 2^{m}}L^{2}_{x}}\\
&\lesssim 2^{5m/6}\rho(2^{m})^{1-\beta}2^{\frac{5}{3}\delta_{N}m}\cdot{\varepsilon}.\end{split}

(9.7)

Using that
\rho(2^{m})\lesssim 2^{-\alpha m}{\varepsilon}^{2(1-\alpha)}

with
\alpha=5/6+10\beta
, with
\beta
small enough, gives us the desired estimate.

∎

9.2.
Weighted norm at high frequency: Proof of Lemma
5.4

We focus on proving the hardest estimate (
5.9
) since (
5.10
) can be proven with similar arguments.
We split
F_{H}
as a sum of the terms

\displaystyle\begin{split}&F_{H,{\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2}}}^{(i,j,k)}(a,b):=\int_{0}^{t}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{6}}\frac{e^{-is\Phi_{\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2}}(\xi,\eta,\sigma)}}{\langle\eta\rangle\langle\sigma\rangle}\widetilde{a}(s,\eta)\widetilde{b}(s,\sigma)\mu_{H}^{(i,j,k)}(\xi,\eta,\sigma)d\eta d\sigma\,ds,\\
&\qquad\Phi_{\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2}}(\xi,\eta,\sigma):=\langle\xi\rangle-{\epsilon}_{1}\langle\eta\rangle-{\epsilon}_{2}\langle\sigma\rangle,\end{split}

with

\displaystyle\begin{split}\mu_{H}^{(i,j,k)}(\xi,\eta,\sigma):=\mu(\xi,\eta,\sigma)\varphi_{i}(\langle\xi\rangle)\varphi_{j}(\langle\eta\rangle)\varphi_{k}(\langle\sigma\rangle),\end{split}

where
i,j,k\in\{+,-\},(i,j,k)\neq(+,+,+),

and we have defined
\varphi_{+}:=\varphi_{\leqslant M_{0}}
(frequencies
\lesssim 2^{M_{0}}
)
and
\varphi_{-}:=\varphi_{>M_{0}}
(frequencies
\gtrsim 2^{M_{0}}
).
Define also

\displaystyle\mathcal{B}(f,g):=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{6}}f(\eta)g(\sigma)\mu(\xi,\eta,\sigma)d\eta d\sigma.

We rely on the distributional identity in Lemma
A.3

(ignoring the easier case where the derivative falls on the outer localizer).
Recall that
\mathcal{R}^{\alpha}
denotes the euclidean Riesz transform.
In the sequel we will slightly abuse notations and still write
\mathcal{R}^{\alpha}
for the symbol of that operator,
i.e.,
\mathcal{R}^{\alpha}_{\xi}=\xi^{\alpha}/|\xi|
.
Using Lemma
A.3
we estimate (note that we will mostly drop the dependence on the index
m
below to improve legibility)

\displaystyle\|\partial_{\xi}F_{H}^{(i,j,k)}\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{m}\|I_{H}^{(i,j,k)}\|_{L^{2}}+\|II_{H}^{(i,j,k)}\|_{L^{2}}+\|III_{H}^{(i,j,k)}\|_{L^{2}},

with

\displaystyle I_{H}^{(i,j,k)}(\xi)

\displaystyle:=\mathcal{R}_{\xi}^{\alpha}\varphi_{i}(\xi)\int_{0}^{t}e^{-is\langle\xi\rangle}

\displaystyle\times\mathcal{B}\bigg(\partial_{\eta^{\beta}}\bigg(\mathcal{R}_{\eta}^{\beta}\frac{e^{is\epsilon_{1}\langle\eta\rangle}}{\langle\eta\rangle}\varphi_{j}(\eta)\widetilde{f_{\epsilon_{1}}}(s,\eta)\bigg),\frac{e^{is\epsilon_{2}\langle\sigma\rangle}}{\langle\sigma\rangle}\varphi_{k}(\sigma)\widetilde{f_{\epsilon_{2}}}(s,\sigma)\bigg)\tau_{m}(s)ds,

\displaystyle II_{H}^{(i,j,k)}(\xi)

\displaystyle:=\mathcal{R}_{\xi}^{\alpha}\varphi_{i}(\xi)\int_{0}^{t}e^{-is\langle\xi\rangle}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\times\mathcal{B}\bigg(E_{\eta}\bigg(\frac{e^{is\epsilon_{1}\langle\eta\rangle}}{\langle\eta\rangle}\varphi_{j}(\eta)\widetilde{f_{\epsilon_{1}}}(s,\eta)\bigg),\frac{e^{is\epsilon_{2}\langle\sigma\rangle}}{\langle\sigma\rangle}\varphi_{k}(\sigma)\widetilde{f_{\epsilon_{2}}}(s,\sigma)\bigg)\tau_{m}(s)ds

\displaystyle+\mathcal{R}_{\xi}^{\alpha}E_{\xi}\varphi_{i}(\xi)\int_{0}^{t}e^{-is\langle\xi\rangle}\mathcal{B}\bigg(\frac{e^{is\epsilon_{1}\langle\eta\rangle}}{\langle\eta\rangle}\varphi_{j}(\eta)\widetilde{f_{\epsilon_{1}}}(s,\eta),\frac{e^{is\epsilon_{2}\langle\sigma\rangle}}{\langle\sigma\rangle}\varphi_{k}(\sigma)\widetilde{f_{\epsilon_{2}}}(s,\sigma)\bigg)\tau_{m}(s)ds,

\displaystyle III_{H}^{(i,j,k)}(\xi)

\displaystyle:=\mathcal{R}_{\xi}^{\alpha}\varphi_{i}(\xi)\int_{0}^{t}is\frac{\xi^{\alpha}}{\langle\xi\rangle}e^{-is\langle\xi\rangle}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\times\mathcal{B}\bigg(\frac{e^{is\epsilon_{1}\langle\eta\rangle}}{\langle\eta\rangle}\varphi_{j}(\eta)\widetilde{f_{\epsilon_{1}}}(s,\eta),\frac{e^{is\epsilon_{2}\langle\sigma\rangle}}{\langle\sigma\rangle}\varphi_{k}(\sigma)\widetilde{f_{\epsilon_{2}}}(s,\sigma)\bigg)\tau_{m}(s)ds.

Note that we can also alternatively differentiate the other profile and write

\displaystyle\|\partial_{\xi}F_{H}^{(i,j,k)}\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{m}\|I_{H}^{(i,j,k)^{\prime}}\|_{L^{2}}+\|II_{H}^{(i,j,k)^{\prime}}\|_{L^{2}}+\|III_{H}^{(i,j,k)}\|_{L^{2}},

\displaystyle I_{H}^{(i,j,k)^{\prime}}(\xi)

\displaystyle:=\mathcal{R}_{\xi}^{\alpha}\varphi_{i}(\xi)\int_{0}^{t}e^{-is\langle\xi\rangle}

\displaystyle\times\mathcal{B}\bigg(\frac{e^{is\epsilon_{1}\langle\eta\rangle}}{\langle\eta\rangle}\varphi_{j}(\eta)\widetilde{f_{\epsilon_{1}}}(s,\eta),\partial_{\sigma^{\beta}}\bigg(\mathcal{R}_{\sigma}^{\beta}\frac{e^{is\epsilon_{2}\langle\sigma\rangle}}{\langle\sigma\rangle}\varphi_{k}(\sigma)\widetilde{f_{\epsilon_{2}}}(s,\sigma)\bigg)\bigg)\tau_{m}(s)ds,

\displaystyle II_{H}^{(i,j,k)^{\prime}}(\xi)

\displaystyle:=\mathcal{R}_{\xi}^{\alpha}\varphi_{i}(\xi)\int_{0}^{t}e^{-is\langle\xi\rangle}

\displaystyle\times\mathcal{B}\bigg(\frac{e^{is\epsilon_{1}\langle\eta\rangle}}{\langle\eta\rangle}\varphi_{j}(\eta)\widetilde{f_{\epsilon_{1}}}(s,\eta),E_{\sigma}\bigg(\frac{e^{is\epsilon_{2}\langle\sigma\rangle}}{\langle\sigma\rangle}\varphi_{k}(\sigma)\widetilde{f_{\epsilon_{2}}}(s,\sigma)\bigg)\color[rgb]{1,0,0.7}\bigg)\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\tau_{m}(s)ds,

\displaystyle+\mathcal{R}_{\xi}^{\alpha}E_{\xi}\varphi_{i}(\xi)\int_{0}^{t}e^{-is\langle\xi\rangle}

\displaystyle\times\mathcal{B}\bigg(\frac{e^{is\epsilon_{1}\langle\eta\rangle}}{\langle\eta\rangle}\varphi_{j}(\eta)\widetilde{f_{\epsilon_{1}}}(s,\eta),\frac{e^{is\epsilon_{2}\langle\sigma\rangle}}{\langle\sigma\rangle}\varphi_{k}(\sigma)\widetilde{f_{\epsilon_{2}}}(s,\sigma)\bigg)\tau_{m}(s)ds.

Estimate of
II_{H}^{(i,j,k)}
.

We use either dispersive bounds or energy bounds depending on whether the input is low or high frequency.
More precisely, we use for
2<p\leqslant 6
and
s\approx 2^{m}
that, for
j=+,-
,

\displaystyle\|\widetilde{\mathcal{F}}^{-1}\big(\varphi_{j}(\eta)e^{is\langle\eta\rangle}\widetilde{f}(s,\eta)\big)\|_{L^{p}}\lesssim 2^{\frac{6-3p}{2p}m+5M_{0}}\cdot Z,

(9.8)

which is a direct consequence of (
4.19
), and of Sobolev’s embedding with the Sobolev a priori bound in (
4.13
)
and (
4.25
).
Then, using Lemma
A.1
, we obtain that

\displaystyle\|II_{H}^{(i,j,k)}\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{0}^{t}\bigg\|\widetilde{\mathcal{F}}^{-1}\big(e^{is\epsilon_{1}\langle\eta\rangle}\frac{\varphi_{j}(\eta)}{\langle\eta\rangle}\widetilde{f_{\epsilon_{1}}}(s,\eta)\big)\bigg\|_{L^{3-}}\bigg\|\widetilde{\mathcal{F}}^{-1}\big(e^{is\epsilon_{2}\langle\sigma\rangle}\frac{\varphi_{k}(\sigma)}{\langle\sigma\rangle}\widetilde{f_{\epsilon_{2}}}(s,\sigma)\big)\bigg\|_{L^{6}}\tau_{m}(s)ds

\displaystyle\lesssim 2^{m}\cdot 2^{20M_{0}}2^{-3m/2}\cdot Z^{2},

which is acceptable.

Estimate of
I_{H}^{(i,j,k)}
.

The two main cases to consider are when the derivative falls on the exponential and on the profile:

\displaystyle\|I_{H}^{(i,j,k)}\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|I_{H,1}^{(i,j,k)}\|_{L^{2}}+\|I_{H,2}^{(i,j,k)}\|_{L^{2}},

\displaystyle I_{H,1}^{(i,j,k)}

\displaystyle:=\mathcal{R}_{\xi}^{\alpha}\varphi_{i}(\xi)\int_{0}^{t}e^{-is\langle\xi\rangle}

\displaystyle\qquad\times\mathcal{B}\bigg(\frac{e^{is\epsilon_{1}\langle\eta\rangle}}{\langle\eta\rangle}\varphi_{j}(\eta)\mathcal{R}_{\eta}^{\beta}\partial_{\eta^{\beta}}\widetilde{f_{\epsilon_{1}}}(s,\eta),\frac{e^{is\epsilon_{2}\langle\sigma\rangle}}{\langle\sigma\rangle}\varphi_{k}(\sigma)\widetilde{f_{\epsilon_{2}}}(s,\sigma)\bigg)\tau_{m}(s)ds,

\displaystyle I_{H,2}^{(i,j,k)}

\displaystyle:=\mathcal{R}_{\xi}^{\alpha}\varphi_{i}(\xi)\int_{0}^{t}e^{-is\langle\xi\rangle}

\displaystyle\qquad\times\mathcal{B}\bigg(is\epsilon_{1}\frac{\eta^{\beta}}{\langle\eta\rangle}\frac{e^{is\epsilon_{1}\langle\eta\rangle}}{\langle\eta\rangle}\varphi_{j}(\eta)\mathcal{R}_{\eta}^{\beta}\widetilde{f_{\epsilon_{1}}}(s,\eta),\frac{e^{is\epsilon_{2}\langle\sigma\rangle}}{\langle\sigma\rangle}\varphi_{k}(\sigma)\widetilde{f_{\epsilon_{2}}}(s,\sigma)\bigg)\tau_{m}(s)ds.

Assume first that
k=-.

In this case we use Sobolev embedding as well as the
H^{N}
bound in the bootstrap and (
4.25
) to write that,
for
p\geqslant 2
,

\displaystyle\|\widetilde{\mathcal{F}}^{-1}\big(\varphi_{-}(\eta)e^{is\langle\eta\rangle}\widetilde{f}(s,\eta)\big)\|_{L^{p}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\widetilde{\mathcal{F}}^{-1}\big(\varphi_{-}(\eta)e^{is\langle\eta\rangle}\widetilde{f}(s,\eta)\big)\|_{H^{2}}\lesssim 2^{-M_{0}(N-2)}\|f\|_{H^{N}}\lesssim 2^{-\frac{5(N-2)}{N}m}\varepsilon^{1-}.

Therefore we can bound

\displaystyle\|I_{H,1}^{(i,j,-)}\|_{L^{2}}\lesssim\int_{0}^{t}\big\|\nabla_{\eta}\widetilde{f}(s,\eta)\big\|_{L^{2}}\|\widetilde{\mathcal{F}}^{-1}e^{is\epsilon_{1}\langle\sigma\rangle}\varphi_{-}(\sigma)\widetilde{f}(s,\sigma)\|_{L^{\infty}}\tau_{m}(s)ds

\displaystyle\lesssim 2^{m}\cdot Z\cdot 2^{-\frac{5(N-2)}{N}m}\cdot{\varepsilon}^{1-},

which yields the desired conclusion.

We can bound
I_{H,2}^{(i,j,-)}
similarly and obtain

\displaystyle\|I_{H,2}^{(i,j,-)}\|_{L^{2}}\lesssim 2^{2m}\cdot Z\cdot 2^{-\frac{5(N-1)}{N}m}.

Note that since we can choose which profile to differentiate, the case
j=-
is handled identically estimating
I_{H}^{(i,-,k)^{\prime}}
instead.

If
j,k=+
then necessarily
i=-
and in this case we write,
using boundedness of wave operators and distributing derivatives,

\displaystyle\|I_{H,2}^{(-,+,+)}\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim 2^{-5m}\int_{0}^{t}\bigg\|D^{N}\widetilde{\mathcal{F}}^{-1}\mathcal{B}\bigg(is\epsilon_{1}\frac{\eta^{\alpha}}{|\eta|}\frac{e^{is\epsilon_{1}\langle\eta\rangle}}{\langle\eta\rangle}\varphi_{+}(\eta)\widetilde{f_{\epsilon_{1}}}(s,\eta),\frac{e^{is\epsilon_{2}\langle\sigma\rangle}}{\langle\sigma\rangle}\varphi_{+}(\sigma)\widetilde{f_{\epsilon_{2}}}(s,\sigma)\bigg)\bigg\|_{L^{2}}\tau_{m}(s)ds
