\displaystyle\lesssim 2^{-5m}\sum_{n_{1}+n_{2}\leqslant N}\int_{0}^{t}s\|D^{n_{1}}L^{-1}P_{\leqslant M_{0}}w\|_{L^{2}}\|D^{n_{2}}L^{-1}P_{\leqslant M_{0}}w\|_{L^{\infty}}\tau_{m}(s)ds

\displaystyle\lesssim 2^{-5m}\cdot 2^{2m}\cdot 2^{M_{0}}{\varepsilon}^{2-},

where for the last line we used the
H^{N}
assumption in the bootstrap (
4.13
), and (
4.25
). This suffices for the desired estimate.

For the remaining term
I_{H,1}^{(-,+,+)}
we split
\partial_{\eta}f=\partial_{\eta}g+\partial_{\eta}h
.
For the
h
part, we use the Strichartz estimate in Lemma
2.6
with parameters
(6,3,2/3)
,
Bernstein’s inequality to gain a derivative, and then distribute this derivative over the inputs;
for the
g
part we use the boundedness of
wave operators and distribute derivatives. Overall we obtain

\displaystyle\|I_{H,1}^{(-,+,+)}\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{\ell\geqslant M_{0}}2^{-\ell/3}\Bigg(\Bigg\|\langle\nabla\rangle\widetilde{\mathcal{F}}^{-1}_{\xi\rightarrow x}\mathcal{B}\bigg(\frac{e^{is\epsilon_{1}\langle\eta\rangle}}{\langle\eta\rangle}\varphi_{+}(\eta)\mathcal{R}_{\eta}^{\alpha}\partial_{\eta^{\alpha}}\widetilde{h_{\epsilon_{1}}}(s,\eta),\frac{e^{is\epsilon_{2}\langle\sigma\rangle}}{\langle\sigma\rangle}\varphi_{+}(\sigma)\widetilde{f_{\epsilon_{2}}}(s,\sigma)\bigg)\Bigg\|_{L^{6/5}_{s\approx 2^{m}}L^{3/2}_{x}}

\displaystyle+\sum_{\ell\geqslant M_{0}}2^{-N\ell}\cdot 2^{m}\cdot\sum_{n_{1}+n_{2}\leqslant N}\big\|D^{n_{1}}L^{-1}\varphi_{+}(D)\widehat{\mathcal{F}}^{-1}\partial_{\eta}\widetilde{g}(s,\eta)\big\|_{L^{2}}\sup_{s\approx 2^{m}}\|D^{n_{2}}L^{-1}w(s)\|_{L^{2}}.

The first term is bounded by
2^{5m/6}\sup_{s\approx 2^{m}}\|\varphi_{+}(L)e^{isL}f(s)\|_{L^{6}_{x}}\cdot Z,

which is sufficient for the desired result.
Using (
4.25
) we see that the last term is bounded by
2^{-NM_{0}+m}\cdot 2^{m/2}{\varepsilon}^{1-}
, which is sufficient given our choice of parameter
M_{0},
see (
5.4
).

Estimate of
III_{H}^{(i,j,k)}
.

This term can be handled following the same strategy as for
I_{H,2}^{(i,j,k)},

so we skip the details.
The proof of the proposition is concluded.
\hfill\Box

Appendix A
Basic results about the NSD

We recall several results derived in
[
16
]
for the interaction distribution of three distorted complex exponentials
as defined in (
1.20
).
We first define some useful operators and state bounds that they satisfy:

Lemma A.1

(Theorem 3.4 in
[
16
]
)
.

With the definitions (
2.5
), let

\displaystyle R^{\alpha}:=\mathcal{W}\frac{x^{\alpha}}{|x|}\mathcal{W}^{\ast},\ \ \ \mathcal{E}:=\big[|x|,\mathcal{W}\big]\mathcal{W}^{\ast},

and

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}^{\alpha}:=\widetilde{\mathcal{F}}R^{\alpha}\widetilde{\mathcal{F}}^{-1},\ \ \ E:=\widetilde{\mathcal{F}}\mathcal{E}\widetilde{\mathcal{F}}^{-1}.

Both are bounded from
L^{p}
to
L^{p+\delta}
for any
1\leqslant p<\infty
and
\delta>0
small enough.

Remark A.2
.

\mathcal{R}^{\alpha}
is the euclidean Riesz transform. It commutes with differentiation.

The next result is an identity for the distributional derivative of
\mu
.

Lemma A.3

(Identity (3.56) in
[
16
]
)
.

We have, in the distributional sense that

\displaystyle\partial_{\xi^{\alpha}}\mu

\displaystyle=\mathcal{R}_{\xi}^{\alpha}\mathcal{R}_{\eta}^{\beta}\partial_{\eta^{\beta}}\mu+\mathcal{R}_{\xi}^{\alpha}E_{\eta}^{\ast}\mu-\mathcal{R}_{\xi}^{\alpha}E_{\xi}^{\ast}\mu

\displaystyle=\mathcal{R}_{\xi}^{\alpha}\mathcal{R}_{\sigma}^{\beta}\partial_{\sigma^{\beta}}\mu+\mathcal{R}_{\xi}^{\alpha}E_{\sigma}^{\ast}\mu-\mathcal{R}_{\xi}^{\alpha}E_{\xi}^{\ast}\mu.

Therefore, we have the following identity

\displaystyle\begin{split}\partial_{\xi}^{\alpha}\big(\widetilde{fg}\big)&=\mathcal{R}_{\xi}^{\alpha}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{6}}\mathcal{R}_{\eta}^{\beta}\partial_{\eta^{\beta}}\widetilde{f}(\eta)\widetilde{g}(\sigma)\mu(\xi,\eta,\sigma)d\eta d\sigma+\mathcal{R}_{\xi}^{\alpha}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{6}}E\widetilde{f}(\eta)\widetilde{g}(\sigma)\mu(\xi,\eta,\sigma)d\eta d\sigma\\
&-E\mathcal{R}_{\xi}^{\alpha}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{6}}\widetilde{f}(\eta)\widetilde{g}(\sigma)\mu(\xi,\eta,\sigma)d\eta d\sigma\\
&=\mathcal{R}_{\xi}^{\alpha}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{6}}\widetilde{f}(\eta)\mathcal{R}_{\sigma}^{\beta}\partial_{\sigma^{\beta}}\widetilde{g}(\sigma)\mu(\xi,\eta,\sigma)d\eta d\sigma+\mathcal{R}_{\xi}^{\alpha}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{6}}\widetilde{f}(\eta)E\widetilde{g}(\sigma)\mu(\xi,\eta,\sigma)d\eta d\sigma\\
&-E\mathcal{R}_{\xi}^{\alpha}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{6}}\widetilde{f}(\eta)\widetilde{g}(\sigma)\mu(\xi,\eta,\sigma)d\eta d\sigma.\end{split}

(A.1)

Remark A.4
.

Note that we slightly abused notations above and denoted
\mathcal{R}^{\alpha}_{\xi}
the symbol of the euclidean Riesz transform.

Remark A.5
.

A convenient feature in (
A.1
) is that one can pick which profile to differentiate.

As a direct consequence we obtain a proof of Lemma
5.5

and nonlinear estimates for short times in the next two subsections.

A.1.
Proof of Lemma
5.5

Note that at least one of the profiles is localized at very low frequency.
We pick one of them and differentiate the other profile, using (
A.1
)
as well as Bernstein’s inequality:

\displaystyle\|\partial_{\xi}F_{L,\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2}}\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{0}^{t}\big(\|\mathcal{R}_{\eta}^{\alpha}\langle\eta\rangle^{-1}\partial_{\eta^{\alpha}}\widetilde{f}\|_{L^{2}}+\bigg\|\mathcal{R}_{\eta}^{\alpha}\frac{s\eta^{\alpha}}{\langle\eta\rangle^{2}}e^{is\langle\eta\rangle}\widetilde{f}\bigg\|_{L^{2}}+\|Ef\|_{L^{2}}\bigg)\|f_{\leqslant s^{-5}}\|_{L^{\infty}}ds

\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{0}^{t}\big(\langle s\rangle^{1/2+}+s{\varepsilon}+{\varepsilon}\big)\langle s\rangle^{-15/2}ds\,{\varepsilon},

which is sufficient to conclude. We used the crude bound
\|\partial_{\eta}\widetilde{f}\|_{L^{2}}\lesssim\langle s\rangle^{1/2+},
which follows from (
4.26
) and the bootstrap assumption (
4.13
).

\hfill\Box

A.2.
Local existence

Here we prove that (
4.15
) implies (
4.16
).
Since
t\leqslant{\varepsilon}^{-1},
(
t\geqslant 1
)
the proof of the bootstrap estimates is much simpler than in the general case.
Therefore, we only give an outline of the proof, typically discarding similar and lower order terms.

First, we have from Lemma
2.5
that

\displaystyle\|e^{itL}f\|_{L^{q}}\lesssim t^{(-1+10\delta_{N})\frac{3(q-2)}{2q}}{\varepsilon}^{1-\beta}.

(A.2)

We bound the terms that appear in (
4.6
). The source terms are treated as in Lemma
4.7
,
therefore we skip the details.
For the mixed terms, we write that (treating the worse case where the derivative falls on the phase)
using Strichartz estimates with parameters
(q,r,\gamma)=(2,\infty,0)

\displaystyle\|\partial_{\xi}M(t)\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\bigg\|\int_{0}^{t}a(s)s\frac{\xi}{\langle\xi\rangle}e^{is\langle\xi\rangle}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\frac{e^{-is\langle\eta\rangle}}{\langle\eta\rangle}\widetilde{f}(s,\eta)\nu(\xi,\eta)d\eta ds\bigg\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim{\big\|s\|e^{itL}f\|_{L^{6}_{x}}\big\|}_{L^{2}_{t}}{\varepsilon}\lesssim t^{1/2+20\delta_{N}}{\varepsilon}^{2-\beta}\leqslant{\varepsilon}^{3/2-2\beta},

where we used the dispersive estimate (
A.2
),
and disregarded the simpler contribution to the integral for
s\in[0,1]
.

We come to the quadratic terms. Using Lemma
A.3
we find, neglecting the lower order terms,

\displaystyle\|\partial_{\xi}\widetilde{F}(t)\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\bigg\|\int_{0}^{t}s\int_{\mathbb{R}^{6}}\frac{\eta^{\alpha}}{\langle\eta\rangle^{2}}e^{is\langle\eta\rangle}\widetilde{f}(\eta)\frac{e^{is\langle\sigma\rangle}}{\langle\sigma\rangle}\widetilde{f}(\sigma)\mu(\xi,\eta,\sigma)d\eta d\sigma ds\bigg\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle+\bigg\|\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{6}}\frac{e^{is\langle\eta\rangle}}{\langle\eta\rangle}\partial_{\eta^{\alpha}}\widetilde{f}(\eta)\frac{e^{is\langle\sigma\rangle}}{\langle\sigma\rangle}\widetilde{f}(\sigma)\mu(\xi,\eta,\sigma)d\eta d\sigma ds\bigg\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{0}^{t}s\|e^{itL}L^{-1}f\|_{L^{6}}\|e^{itL}L^{-1}f\|_{L^{3}}ds+\int_{0}^{t}\|L^{-1}\mathcal{F}^{-1}\big(\partial_{\xi}\widetilde{f}\big)\|_{L^{3}}\|e^{itL}L^{-1}f\|_{L^{6}}ds

\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{0}^{t}s\cdot\langle s\rangle^{-1+10\delta_{N}}\cdot\langle s\rangle^{-1/2+5\delta_{N}}ds\,{\varepsilon}^{2-2\beta}+\int_{0}^{t}\langle s\rangle^{-1}ds\,{\varepsilon}^{3/2-2\beta}

\displaystyle\lesssim\langle t\rangle^{1/2+15\delta_{N}}{\varepsilon}^{2-2\beta}+{\varepsilon}^{3/2-3\beta},

where we used the bound, coming from the bootstrap assumption and (
4.26
),

\displaystyle\|\partial_{\xi}\widetilde{f}\|_{L^{2}}\lesssim\|\partial_{\xi}\widetilde{h}\|_{L^{2}}+\|\partial_{\xi}\widetilde{g}\|_{L^{2}}\lesssim{\varepsilon}^{1-\beta}+t^{3/2}{\varepsilon}^{2}|\log{\varepsilon}|^{2}\leqslant{\varepsilon}^{1/2-},

which yields the desired result given that
t\leqslant{\varepsilon}^{-1}
provided
\beta
is small enough.

\hfill\Box

Appendix B
Structure of the NSD and product estimates

In order to deal with nonlinear problems and understand oscillations in distorted Fourier space,
we need a more refined analysis of (the singularities of) the NSD. We first recall the decomposition result of
[
41
]
, under the more generous assumption
V\in\mathcal{S}

as in Proposition
5.6
; we refer the reader to
[
41
]
for the precise statement under
finite regularity and localization assumptions on the potential
V
.

Proposition B.1

(
[
41
]
)
.

The distribution (
4.1
) can be written as

\displaystyle\begin{split}(2\pi)^{9/2}\mu(\xi,\eta,\sigma)&=(2\pi)^{3/2}\delta_{0}(\xi-\eta-\sigma)-\frac{1}{4\pi}\mu_{1}(\xi,\eta,\sigma)\\
&-\frac{1}{(4\pi)^{2}}\mu_{2}(\xi,\eta,\sigma)-\frac{1}{(4\pi)^{3}}\mu_{3}(\xi,\eta,\sigma),\end{split}

(B.1)

where:

•

The distribution
\mu_{1}
is given by

\displaystyle\mu_{1}(k,\ell,m)

\displaystyle=\nu_{1}(-k+\ell,m)+\nu_{1}(-k+m,\ell)+\overline{\nu_{1}(-\ell-m,k)}

(B.2)

where

\displaystyle\nu_{1}(p,q)=\nu_{0}(p,q)+\nu_{L}(p,q)+\nu_{R}(p,q),

(B.3)

and:

(1)

The leading order is

\displaystyle\nu_{0}(p,q):=\frac{b_{0}(p,q)}{|p|}\Big[i\pi\,\delta(|p|-|q|)+\mathrm{p.v.}\frac{1}{|p|-|q|}\Big]

(B.4)

with
b_{0}
satisfying the bounds

\displaystyle\big|\varphi_{P}(p)\varphi_{Q}(q)\,\nabla_{p}^{\alpha}\nabla_{q}^{\beta}b_{0}(p,q)\big|\lesssim 2^{-|\alpha|P}\big(2^{|\alpha|Q}+2^{(1-|\beta|)Q_{-}}\big)\cdot\mathbf{1}_{\{|P-Q|<5\}},

(B.5)

for all
P,Q\leqslant A
,
and
|\alpha|+|\beta|\leqslant N_{2}
,
for any arbitrarily large integer
11
11

11

The value of
N_{2}
can be related to
the localization and smoothness of the potential
V
, and it can be chosen to be approximately
N/2

when
\langle x\rangle^{N}V\in H^{N}
.
Since we assume, for simplicity, that
V\in\mathcal{S}
, we can let
N_{2}
be arbitrarily large.

N_{2}
.
Recall our notation
Q^{-}=\min(Q,0)
.

(2)

The lower order terms
\nu_{L}(p,q)
can be written as

\displaystyle\nu_{L}(p,q)=\frac{1}{|p|}\sum_{a=1}^{N_{2}}\sum_{J\in{\mathbb{Z}}}b_{a,J}(p,q)\cdot 2^{J}K_{a}\big(2^{J}(|p|-|q|)\big)

(B.6)

with
K_{a}\in\mathcal{S}
and
b_{a,J}
satisfying

\displaystyle\sum_{J\in{\mathbb{Z}}}\big|\varphi_{P}(p)\varphi_{Q}(q)\nabla_{p}^{\alpha}\nabla_{q}^{\beta}b_{a,J}(p,q)\big|\lesssim 2^{-|\alpha|P}\big(2^{|\alpha|Q}+2^{(1-|\beta|)Q_{-}}\big)\cdot\mathbf{1}_{\{|P-Q|<5\}},

(B.7)

for all
P,Q\leqslant A
,
|\alpha|+|\beta|\leqslant N_{2}
.

(3)

The remainder term
\nu_{R}
satisfies the estimates

\displaystyle\begin{split}\big|\varphi_{P}(p)\varphi_{Q}(q)\nabla_{p}^{\alpha}\nabla_{q}^{\beta}\nu_{R}(p,q)\big|\lesssim 2^{-2\max(P,Q)}\cdot 2^{-(|\alpha|+|\beta|)\max(P,Q)}\cdot 2^{(|\alpha|+|\beta|+2)5A}\end{split}

(B.8)

for
|P-Q|<5
, and

\displaystyle\begin{split}\big|\varphi_{P}(p)\varphi_{Q}(q)\nabla_{p}^{\alpha}\nabla_{q}^{\beta}\nu_{R}(p,q)\big|\lesssim 2^{-2\max(P,Q)}\cdot 2^{-|\alpha|\max(P,Q)}\max(1,2^{(1-|\beta|)Q_{-}})\cdot 2^{(|\alpha|+|\beta|+2)5A}\end{split}

(B.9)

for
|P-Q|\geqslant 5
,
for all
P,Q\leqslant A
,
|\alpha|+|\beta|\leqslant N_{2}
.

•

The measure
\mu_{2}
is given by

\displaystyle\mu_{2}(k,\ell,m)

\displaystyle=\nu_{2}^{1}(k,\ell,m)+\nu_{2}^{2}(k,\ell,m)+\nu_{2}^{2}(k,m,\ell)

(B.10)

with the following properties:

–

Let
k,\ell,m\in{\mathbb{R}}^{3}
with
|k|\approx 2^{K},|\ell|\approx 2^{L}
and
|m|\approx 2^{M}
,
and assume that
K,L,M\leqslant A
for some
A>0
.
Then we can write

\displaystyle\nu_{2}^{1}(k,\ell,m)=\nu_{2,0}^{1}(k,\ell,m)+\nu_{2,R}^{1}(k,\ell,m),

(B.11)

where:

(1)

\nu_{2,0}^{1}(k,\ell,m)
can be written as

\displaystyle\nu_{2,0}^{1}(k,\ell,m)=\frac{1}{|k|}\sum_{i=1}^{N_{2}}\sum_{J\in{\mathbb{Z}}}b_{i,J}(k,\ell,m)\cdot K_{i}\big(2^{J}(|k|-|\ell|-|m|)\big)

(B.12)

with
K_{i}\in\mathcal{S}
and the symbols
b_{i,J}
satisfy

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\big|\varphi_{K}(k)\varphi_{L}(\ell)\varphi_{M}(m)\nabla_{k}^{a}\nabla_{\ell}^{\alpha}\nabla_{m}^{\beta}b_{i,J}(k,\ell,m)\big|\\
&\lesssim 2^{-|a|K}\cdot\big(2^{|a|\max(L,M)}+2^{(1-|\alpha|)L}2^{(1-|\beta|)M}\big)\mathbf{1}_{\{|K-\max(L,M)|<5\}},\end{split}

(B.13)

for all
K,L,M\leqslant A
, and
|a|+|\alpha|+|\beta|\leqslant N_{2}
.

(2)

For
M\leqslant L
, the remainder term
\nu_{2,R}^{1}
satisfies

\displaystyle\begin{split}\big|\nabla_{k}^{a}\nabla_{\ell}^{\alpha}\nabla_{m}^{\beta}\nu_{2,R}^{1}(k,\ell,m)\big|&\lesssim 2^{-2\max(K,L)}\cdot 2^{-|a|\max(K,L)}\\
&\times 2^{-|\alpha|\max(K,L)}\max(1,2^{-(|\beta|-1)M})\cdot 2^{(|a|+|\alpha|+|\beta|+2)5A}\end{split}

(B.14)

for all
K,L,M\leqslant A
and
|a|+|\alpha|+|\beta|\leqslant N_{2}
. A similar statement holds when
L\leqslant M
exchanging the roles of
L
and
M
(and
\alpha
and
\beta
).

–

Let
k,\ell,m\in{\mathbb{R}}^{3}
with
|k|\approx 2^{K},|\ell|\approx 2^{L}
and
|m|\approx 2^{M}
,
and
K,L,M\leqslant A
for some
A>0
.
Then we can write

\displaystyle\nu_{2}^{2}(k,\ell,m)=\nu_{2,+}^{2}(k,\ell,m)+\nu_{2,-}^{2}(k,\ell,m)+\nu_{2,R}^{2}(k,\ell,m),

(B.15)

where:

(1)

The leading order is

\displaystyle\nu_{2,\pm}^{2}(k,\ell,m)=\frac{1}{|\ell|}\sum_{i=1}^{N_{2}}\sum_{J\in{\mathbb{Z}}}b_{i,J}^{\pm}(k,\ell,m)\cdot K_{i}\big(2^{J}(|k|\pm|\ell|-|m|)\big)

(B.16)

with

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\big|\varphi_{K}(k)\varphi_{L}(\ell)\varphi_{M}(m)\nabla_{k}^{a}\nabla_{\ell}^{\alpha}\nabla_{m}^{\beta}b_{i,J}^{\pm}(k,\ell,m)\big|\\
&\lesssim 2^{-|\alpha|L}\cdot\big(2^{|\alpha|\max(K,M)}+2^{(1-|a|)K}2^{(1-|\beta|)M}\big)\mathbf{1}_{\{\max(K,L,M)-\mbox{\tiny$\mathrm{med}$}(K,L,M)<5\}},\end{split}

(B.17)

for all
K,L,M\leqslant A
, and
|a|+|\alpha|+|\beta|\leqslant N_{2}
.

(2)

The remainder term satisfies, for
K\leqslant M
,

\displaystyle\begin{split}\big|\nabla_{k}^{a}\nabla_{\ell}^{\alpha}\nabla_{m}^{\beta}\nu_{2,R}^{2}(k,\ell,m)\big|&\lesssim 2^{-2\max(L,M,K)}\cdot 2^{-(|\alpha|+|\beta|)\max(L,M,K)}\\
&\times\max(1,2^{-(|a|-1)K})\cdot 2^{(|a|+|\alpha|+|\beta|+2)5A}\end{split}

(B.18)

for all
K,L,M\leqslant A
and
|a|+|\alpha|+|\beta|\leqslant N_{2}
. A similar estimate holds when
M\leqslant K
by exchanging the roles of
M
and
K
(and
a,\beta
).

•

Let
k,\ell,m\in{\mathbb{R}}^{3}
with
|k|\approx 2^{K},|\ell|\approx 2^{L}
and
|m|\approx 2^{M}
,
and assume that
K,L,M\leqslant A
for some
A>0
.
Then we can write

\displaystyle\mu_{3}(k,\ell,m)=\mu_{3,0}(k,\ell,m)+\mu_{3,R}(k,\ell,m),

(B.19)

where:

(1)

The leading order has the form

\displaystyle\mu_{3,0}(k,\ell,m)=\sum_{i=0}^{N_{2}}\sum_{J\in{\mathbb{Z}}}b_{i,J}(k,\ell,m)\cdot K_{i}\big(2^{J}(|k|-|\ell|-|m|)\big)

(B.20)

with
K_{i}\in\mathcal{S}
, and

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\big|\varphi_{K}(k)\varphi_{L}(\ell)\varphi_{M}(m)\nabla_{k}^{a}\nabla_{\ell}^{\alpha}\nabla_{m}^{\beta}b_{i,J}(k,\ell,m)\big|\\
&\lesssim\max(1,2^{-(|a|-1)K})\max(1,2^{-(|\alpha|-1)L})\max(1,2^{-(|\beta|-1)M})\mathbf{1}_{\{|K-\max(L,M)|<5\}}\end{split}

(B.21)

for all
K,L,M\leqslant A
, and
|a|+|\alpha|+|\beta|\leqslant N_{2}
.

(2)

The remainder term satisfies

\displaystyle\begin{split}\big|\nabla_{k}^{a}\nabla_{\ell}^{\alpha}\nabla_{m}^{\beta}\mu_{3,R}(k,\ell,m)\big|\lesssim\max(1,2^{-(|a|-1)K})\max(1,2^{-(|\alpha|-1)L})\\
\cdot\max(1,2^{-(|\beta|-1)M})\cdot 2^{4A(|a|+|\alpha|+|\beta|+1)}\end{split}

(B.22)

for all
K,L,M\leqslant A
and
|a|+|\alpha|+|\beta|\leqslant N_{2}
.

Next, we collect all the needed bilinear
estimates for the operators associated to the various pieces
appearing in the decomposition of Proposition
B.1
.
Again these results are borrowed from
[
41
]
.

Given a symbol
b=b(\xi,\eta,\sigma)
, we define the bilinear operators

\displaystyle T_{0}[b](g,h)(x)

\displaystyle:=\widehat{\mathcal{F}}^{-1}_{\xi\rightarrow x}\iint_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}g(\eta)h(\sigma)\,b(\xi,\eta,\sigma)\,\delta_{0}(\xi-\eta-\sigma)\,d\eta d\sigma

(B.23)

and

\displaystyle T_{1}^{1}[b](g,h)(x)

\displaystyle:=\widehat{\mathcal{F}}^{-1}_{\xi\rightarrow x}\iint_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}g(\xi-\eta)h(\sigma)\,b(\xi,\eta,\sigma)\,\nu_{1}(\eta,\sigma)\,d\eta d\sigma,

(B.24)

\displaystyle T_{1}^{2}[b](g,h)(x)

\displaystyle:=\widehat{\mathcal{F}}^{-1}_{\xi\rightarrow x}\iint_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}g(-\eta-\sigma)h(\sigma)\,b(\xi,\eta,\sigma)\,\overline{\nu_{1}(\eta,\xi)}\,d\eta d\sigma,

(B.25)

\displaystyle T_{2}[b](g,h)(x)

\displaystyle:=\widehat{\mathcal{F}}^{-1}_{\xi\rightarrow x}\iint_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}g(\eta)h(\sigma)\,b(\xi,\eta,\sigma)\,\mu_{2}(\xi,\eta,\sigma)\,d\eta d\sigma,

(B.26)

\displaystyle T_{3}[b](g,h)(x)

\displaystyle:=\widehat{\mathcal{F}}^{-1}_{\xi\rightarrow x}\iint_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}g(\eta)h(\sigma)\,b(\xi,\eta,\sigma)\,\mu_{3}(\xi,\eta,\sigma)\,d\eta d\sigma.

(B.27)

Theorem B.2

(Bilinear bounds 1)
.

Assume that:

•

The symbol
b
is such that

\displaystyle\textrm{supp}(b)\subseteq\big\{(\xi,\eta,\sigma)\in\mathbb{R}^{9}\,:\,|\xi|+|\eta|+|\sigma|\leqslant 2^{A},\,|\eta|\approx 2^{k_{1}},\,|\sigma|\approx 2^{k_{2}}\big\},

(B.28)

for some
A\geqslant 1
.

•

For all
|\xi|\approx 2^{k}
,
|\eta|\approx 2^{k_{1}}
and
|\sigma|\approx 2^{k_{2}}

\displaystyle|\nabla_{\xi}^{a}\nabla^{\alpha}_{\eta}\nabla^{\beta}_{\sigma}b(\xi,\eta,\sigma)|\lesssim 2^{-|a|k}2^{-|\alpha|k_{1}}2^{-|\beta|k_{2}}\cdot 2^{(|a|+|\alpha|+|\beta|)A},\qquad|a|,|\alpha|,|\beta|\leqslant 5.

(B.29)

•

There is
10A\leqslant D\leqslant 2^{A/10}
such that

\displaystyle\begin{split}\mathcal{D}(g,h):=\|g\|_{L^{2}}\|h\|_{L^{2}}+\min\big({\|\partial_{k}g\|}_{L^{2}}{\|h\|}_{L^{2}},{\|g\|}_{L^{2}}{\|\partial_{k}h\|}_{L^{2}}\big)\leqslant 2^{D}.\end{split}

(B.30)

Then, the following estimates hold:
For any
p,q\in[1,\infty]
and
r>1
with

\displaystyle\frac{1}{p}+\frac{1}{q}>\frac{1}{r},\qquad

(B.31)

we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}{\big\|T_{1}^{1}[b]\big(g,h\big)\big\|}_{L^{r}}&\lesssim{\big\|\widehat{g}\big\|}_{L^{p}}{\big\|\widehat{h}\big\|}_{L^{q}}\cdot 2^{\max(k_{1},k_{2})}\cdot 2^{C_{0}A}+2^{-D}\mathcal{D}(g,h),\end{split}

(B.32)

and

\displaystyle\begin{split}{\big\|P_{K}T_{1}^{2}[b](g,h)\big\|}_{L^{r}}&\lesssim{\big\|\widehat{g}\big\|}_{L^{p}}{\big\|\widehat{h}\big\|}_{L^{q}}\cdot 2^{\max(k_{1},k_{2})}\cdot 2^{C_{0}A}+2^{-D}\mathcal{D}(g,h),\end{split}

(B.33)

where
12
12

12

This is a convenient value of the absolute constant
C_{0}

but it can certainly be improved.
In the course of nonlinear estimates for the evolution equation
we impose conditions on the smallness of
C_{0}\delta_{N}
(or similar quantities); see (
1.45
) for the definition of
\delta_{N}
. Then, a smaller value of the constant
C_{0}
in Theorem
B.2
would reduce the total number of derivatives
N
required for our initial data.

C_{0}:=65
.
In the above
P_{K}
denotes the flat/regular Littlewood-Paley projection.
Moreover, we have

\displaystyle\|T_{0}[b](g,h)\|_{L^{r}},\,\|T_{2}[b](g,h)\|_{L^{r}},\,\|T_{3}[b](g,h)\|_{L^{r}}\lesssim\|\widehat{g}\|_{L^{p}}\|\widehat{h}\|_{L^{q}}\cdot 2^{(C_{0}+1)A}.

(B.34)

We also recall the following statement from
[
41
]
, which is used in the proof of Theorem (
B.2
):

Lemma B.3
.

Let
j\geqslant 1,
and consider the bilinear operator

\displaystyle B_{j}[b](g,h)(x)=\mathcal{F}^{-1}_{\xi\mapsto x}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{6}}g(\eta-\xi)h(\sigma)b(\xi,\eta,\sigma)\chi\big(2^{j}(|\eta|-|\sigma|)\big)\,d\eta d\sigma,

where
\chi
is a Schwartz function. Assume that:

•

For some
A\geqslant 1
,
K,L,M\in{\mathbb{Z}}
, with
L\gg-j
,
we have

\displaystyle\textrm{supp}(b)\subseteq\big\{(\xi,\eta,\sigma)\in\mathbb{R}^{9}\,:\,|\xi|+|\eta|+|\sigma|\lesssim 2^{A},\,|\xi|\approx 2^{K},\,|\eta|\approx 2^{L},\,|\sigma|\approx 2^{M}\};

•

The following estimates hold:

\displaystyle|\nabla_{\xi}^{a}\nabla_{\eta}^{\alpha}\nabla_{\sigma}^{\beta}b(\xi,\eta,\sigma)|\lesssim 2^{-|a|K-|\alpha|L-|\beta|M},\qquad|a|,|\alpha|,|\beta|\leqslant 4.

Then for
p,q,r\in[1,\infty],
we have

\displaystyle\|B_{j}[b](g,h)\|_{L^{r}}\lesssim 2^{-j}\cdot 2^{2L}\cdot 2^{8A}\cdot\|\widehat{g}\|_{L^{p}}\|\widehat{h}\|_{L^{q}},\qquad\frac{1}{p}+\frac{1}{q}=\frac{1}{r}.
